treadcore · lab report · brain

The brain: lab report and design

A lab report on the bot AI (treadcore-brain) as it stood on 18 October 2026, the research it was reviewed against, and the design it is being rebuilt to: one weighted, tunable decision pattern on shared team memory, memory and compute first. The same report, with its two diagrams drawn, is the shared document Treadcore: Status Update & Brain AI Lab Report; this file is the copy that lives with the code. The work it plans is step 12 of ROADMAP.md.

How to read it now. Sections 1 to 10 are the review and the design of 17 and 18 October. Section 2, the brain as it stood then, is archived in place: it is the baseline every later number is measured against, and the improved prototype has replaced it. Sections 11 to 19 are the record of building the design, a step and a census at a time. Section 20 is the brain as it stands now. Which old methods were archived, and which still run with an improved challenger switched off, is the register archive/BRAIN-METHODS.md.

Five conclusions, numbered so the rest can cite them:

  • C1. The brain is a stack of first-match ladders with 22 tunable weights, all in combat; each tank decides alone, on a full copy of the world, twenty times a second (section 2, findings L1 to L8).
  • C2. Step one is memory: static map facts, one shared team board per team at 1 Hz, a small fixed mind per tank; parameters as one flat Weights array and Caps bits; thinks staggered by layer (section 5). Nothing else lands before this and a measured baseline.
  • C3. One scoring pattern for every decision: features, response curves, weighted products, a confirming selector, a held intent. Behaviours plug in as components that can be switched off and tuned (section 4), after the utility, Killzone 3 and Spring designs of section 3.
  • C4. The behaviours asked for are components (sections 6, 7, 9): fill up, give the base one item and one improvement, road out, and escort pillboxes home along the road; a small island is a bad base and a priced crossing picks boat or bridge (6 tiles or fewer preferred, 20 at most); column on a bridge; desire vectors that need several thinks of confirmation, except retreat and dodge; forts, fronts and rotation across the team.
  • C5. Every match records each tank's weights, capabilities and seed beside its result; ratings and an offline evolution strategy find better weights, and fair fleet roles, by measurement (section 8).

Source: documentation/LAB-REPORT-BRAIN.md, built by orrery's tools/report/collect.py.

1. Method

Read: source/treadcore-brain/src (8,458 lines on 18 October: commander.rs 2,966, lib.rs 2,150, hop.rs 766, fleet.rs 719, tactics.rs 562, fortify.rs 538, crew.rs 486, rotation.rs 271) and the sim's observe and routing. The key claims were checked against the source. Nothing was run for the report: every number in sections 2 and 6 to 9 is a code constant or a starting guess, never a measurement. The measurement is step 12a.

2. The brain as it stood on 17 October (archived)

Archived 18 October 2026: the baseline. This is the brain step 12 started from, kept as the development record and as the baseline the census measures against. The brain now is section 20; what replaced each method here, and what is still current, is archive/BRAIN-METHODS.md. Findings L1 to L8 keep their numbers, since the rest of the report cites them.

Who runs, how often

LevelCodeRateOutput
Fleet commandercrew::FleetCommand in the Leader's or Builder's Commander20 Hz; musters every 30 sRequest{Muster, Build, Fetch, Haul} events, pings
Commander, one per tankcommander.rs think_map20 HzVec<Command>
Auto ladderauto(): DefendBase > Fortify > Turrets > Hunt > Expand > Guard20 Hza goal
Wingmanfly_wing: Defend > Ahead > Pillbox > Depot > Help > Regroup20 Hz, 3 s commita Move to a task or wedge slot
Doctrine, the inner RuleBotlib.rs think_map4 Hz (rookies 2 Hz)drones, bubble, gun; its Moves dropped by keep_inner
Tacticiantactics.rseach doctrine thinktarget score, Lanchester odds (ours squared over theirs squared), Mass/Engage/Press, kite
Hophop.rseach doctrine thinkbridge (at most BRIDGE_MAX 20 water tiles), boat, landfall
Plan, Rotation, Crewfortify.rs, rotation.rs, crew.rs20 Hznext build item, push and defence points, the job

Fleet commander (Leader or Builder)
   |  one-tick events: Request, Pinged                        (L5)
   v
Commander, each tank, 20 Hz, ~17 branches, first match wins   (L1)
   |  <-- observation: a full world copy per bot per tick --   (L2)
   |  --> commands --> sim router, A* per Move
   +-- Auto ladder (6 rungs)
   +-- Wingman (3 s commit)                                   (L7)
   +-- Crew job (Build / Fetch / Haul)
   +-- Rotation, fortify plan
   +-- Doctrine, 4 Hz (its Moves dropped)
          +-- Tactician (22 weights)
          +-- Hop (bridge <= 20, failures not cached)         (L4)
          +-- Dodge (indirect shells only)

The Commander's branches, in order, first return wins: downed, dodge, revive, bridge help, shore gather, afloat, chain help, pings and rally, fleet, crew job, leader takeover, scoot, needs_home, goal dispatch, doctrine guns and drones, alliances.

Findings

  • L1. Decisions are first-match ladders. Weights exist only in Tactics (22 coefficients); the Auto ladder and the wingman's priorities are fixed orders with on/off rungs.
  • L2. Every bot gets a fresh copy of the world every tick. World::observe clones every depot, pillbox, structure, player name and visible tank (route Vec and all) per bot, 20 times a second, and runs two 41 x 41 terrain scans with sorts; RuleBot and Defender think at 4 Hz.
  • L3. Repeated work. auto() cloned the whole Plan and ran add_turrets on the copy every tick for two yes/no answers; Tactician built a Vec<String> of reasons per candidate; six why strings and a doing string were formatted every tick.
  • L4. A failed bridge search was never cached. Map::crossing is a Dijkstra with no heuristic over 65,536 states and 768 KB of buffers per call, rerun at 4 Hz while a crossing is over 20 tiles or impossible.
  • L5. No shared team memory. Push points, odds and assignments are recomputed per tank; coordination rides on one-tick events, and a missed event is a missed job.
  • L6. Lists that never forget. given_up, depots_given_up, revive_failed, Hop's bad_* and no_bridge, the fleet's fails, the Defender's *_tries grow for the match and are searched linearly; what they hold is never retried.
  • L7. Uneven commitment. go() re-issues a Move only on a 1-tile shift, 4 s or a stop, but targets that follow a moving thing (wedge slots, rallies, kite step-ins, chain help) re-route nearly every tick; hysteresis exists in patches (WING_COMMIT 3 s, w_stick 0.4, landfall 6 points over 2 s, ALARM_HOLD 6 s) with no common rule.
  • L8. The economy is reactive. Trees are felled only when a build is short, the squad holds 8; nothing fills up ahead of need, so a starved plan turns Fortify off. Rotation balances one tank (refill to 90%, at most 25 s), never the team.

Also: two resupply latches that disagree (doctrine under 6 shells, Commander at 4 shells or 35 armour) and the doctrine's can take an Auto tank over through hop.send; direct fire is never dodged before it lands; a Hunt target given up after 12 bearings is given up for good; partial_cmp().unwrap() would panic on a NaN.

3. Research review

Every source here was opened and read (the page, the paper or the source code); what could not be confirmed is marked unverified.

4. Design: a weighted decision graph of components

One scoring pattern at the team and unit levels:

Team brain, 1 Hz (posture, jobs, defence points)       Weights and Caps
        | posture, threat                              (sliders, bits)
        v                                                    |
Features, per tank --> Considerations --> Action scores, 4 Hz <-+
(32 values, 0..1)      (curves C(x))      U = W x prod C, masked by Caps
                                                 |
                       Intent (held)  <------ Selector (beat by 15%
                             |                 for 2 thinks)
                             v
Survival bypass ====> Reflex, 20 Hz -------> Commands to the sim
(dodge, retreat)      (desire vectors,              | match result
                       fire gate)                   v
                                              Ledger and tuner --> better weights

The score of an action a:

U(a) = W[a] * cap[a] * P[posture][a] * (prod_i C_i(x_i))^(1/n) * (1 + m * [a is current])

W[a] is the action's slider (0 to 2, default 1); cap[a] is 0 or 1 from Caps; P is the team posture's multiplier for the action's component (section 9); the geometric mean keeps a five-consideration action comparable with a two-consideration one (compensation in its simplest form); m is the commitment bonus of section 7. A curve C(x) is linear, quadratic or logistic with four parameters, and those are sliders too, so one flat Weights array holds every number a tuner may touch.

A component is a trait object that does not allocate:

rust
trait Component {
    const CAP: Cap;
    fn actions(&self, f: &Features, board: &TeamBoard, out: &mut ArrayVec<Action, 16>);
    fn score(&self, a: &Action, f: &Features, w: &Weights) -> f32;
    fn emit(&self, a: &Action, mind: &mut TankMind, out: &mut Vec<Command>);
}
ComponentCaps bitActionsMain considerationsFrom
CombatKITE, FOCUSattack, kite, mass, pressodds, target score, range, shellsTactics, its 22 weights as they are
Survivalalwaysdodge, retreat, reviveeta, armour, shellsDodge, needs_home
EconomyFILLfill, give item, give improvementmaterial, plan gap, forest nearPlan::next, fell
Roads and crossingsROADS, BRIDGES, BOATSlay road, bridge, boatpriced crossing (6.2), supply distancehop.rs
FortifyFORTIFY, TURRETScastle item, turret slot, yardplan state, quietfortify.rs
HaulHAULSfetch, tow, escortroad to pillbox, crews freecrew.rs, help_chain
FormationWINGwedge slot, column, wing taskleader distance, bridge aheadfly_wing
RotationROTATEpush point, defence point, swapteam armour spread, point threatrotation.rs

The team level uses the same pattern with postures for actions. The reflex layer is not scored: fixed rules at 20 Hz are cheaper, and survival should not hang on a slider.

5. Step one: memory, parameters, compute

Work each fact out once per team per think, keep it in a fixed-size board, and lend it to every tank by reference.

MemoryLivesHoldsSize
MapFactsthe match; updated when a tile changesisland id per tile (u16); the island table (area, forest, shore, pads); the crossing cache (from, to) -> Option<tiles>, failures included (L4)~40 KB
TeamBoardper team, rebuilt at 1 Hz16 x 16 sector grids of 8 x 8 tiles (threat, strength, territory, forest left); the job board (persistent jobs, L5); the defence-point roster; the road graph~8 KB
TankMindper tankthe intent and its score, desire vectors, confirmation counters, recent memories with expiry ticks (L6)under 1 KB

Parameters: Weights([f32; N]) named by a W enum and lent as &Weights; Caps(u64), one bit per capability, a capability off is never scored and costs nothing; Features([f32; 32]) per tank per think, read by pure scorers fn(&Features, &Weights) -> f32. The Observation stops being a per-bot copy of the world: one fog-filtered snapshot per team per tick, or a borrowed view with a vision mask.

Scratch: each bot reuses its buffers (A*, crossing); status strings become codes formatted only when the HUD asks; the plan answers turret_slot and yard_left without a clone (L3).

LayerRateStaggered byWork
Reflex20 Hznonedodge, retreat trigger, fire gate, desire vectors, hold the command
Unit4 Hztick % 5 == seat % 5score ~12 actions, choose the intent, at most one Move
Team1 Hztick % 20 == team * 6 % 20rebuild the board, post jobs, assign roles and points, pick a posture
Strategy0.2 Hzone team a secondsites, road plans, the front, crossings (cached)

Measure first: per-layer timers and counters in the census runs (thinks, Moves, A* calls, crossing calls, allocations), reported as microseconds per bot per tick, before and after each change.

6. Behaviours to add

6.1 The base cycle (L8)

  1. Fill. Shells and armour at the depot; the squad to its 8 material from the forest nearest the base, from the board's forest grid.
  2. Give one item. One plan item at the base, chosen by utility, so the plan never starves while the fleet is out.
  3. Give one improvement. Mend the worst pillbox, a mine on the moat, works on a pad; 11g's upgrades fill this slot when decided.
  4. Connect. Road toward the nearest established friendly base or pillbox not yet joined to ours, planned once by the strategy layer (grass 1, forest 1 and a fell, water a bridge's price), kept on the board as road jobs any tank can lay.
  5. Mission. When a road reaches an outlying pillbox, an escort job: two haulers on the Tow chain, the rest in column screening the threat side; the pillbox comes home along the road and is set in the castle while it has fewer than its two inside and one on the road, else at a post along a road between bases, never at a junction (section 10, the castle rule).

Weights w_fill, w_give_item, w_give_upgrade, w_connect, w_escort. A threat at base skips the cycle, as today.

6.2 Islands, boats and bridges

A base site's score:

S = w_forest * min(1, F_island / F_need) + w_pads * P + w_choke * K
    - w_small * max(0, 1 - A_island / A_min)

F_need is what the plan will ask for (castle, turrets, yard, roads out) in trees at 4 material each; A_min a tunable area. A small island with few trees scores low.

A crossing is priced both ways, with scarcity s = F_need / (F_reachable + 1):

C_bridge(L) = L * (1 + s) + k_long * max(0, L - 6)^2
C_boat      = c_boat * (1 + s) + t_launch

Up to 6 tiles the long term is zero and a short bridge wins; from 7 to 20 the square grows fast, and a long bridge wins only for a road the network will use; BRIDGE_MAX stays 20. On a small island the high s makes the boat (BOAT_COST 5) beat a long bridge. Starting guesses, for the tuner: k_long 0.5, c_boat 5, t_launch 3.

A part-laid bridge is a job on the board, picked up by the next tank with material, dropped only when the strategy layer drops the crossing.

6.3 Column on a bridge

When the leader is on a bridge or narrow tile, or its next 3 route tiles are: the leader drives straight at full speed (no gathering, no formation); slot k is k x 2 tiles behind along the bridge axis, no side offsets; wingmen still on land wait at the bridge head in slot order; the wedge returns when the last tank is 2 tiles past the far end.

7. Decision smoothing

A tank keeps doing its last command until a new one is confirmed over several thoughts; only the two survival reflexes break in at once.

  1. Hold the last command. The reflex layer replays the intent every tick between thinks; a think that finds nothing better emits nothing; a new Move only when the intent changes or its target moves more than a tile.
  2. Desire vectors. D = (1 - alpha) D + alpha d, A = (1 - beta) A + beta a, with alpha 0.4, beta 0.3 at 4 Hz: a full swing in 3 or 4 thinks.
  3. Confirmation with hysteresis. A challenger b replaces a when U(b) > U(a) (1 + h) for n thinks in a row, with U(a) raised by (1 + m) while held: h 0.15, n 2 (3 for a turret target), m 0.1. Advance above A = +0.35, fall back below -0.35, hold between. The aim point is led, then smoothed (EMA 0.5).
  4. Survival bypass. Dodge (a shell with eta 4 s or less landing within 3.3 tiles) and retreat (one threshold pair, retreat_armor and retreat_shells) skip smoothing and confirmation at full speed; the old intent comes back after, unchanged.
  5. Brain power per system. The cadences of section 5 decide how often a system thinks; the weights decide how much it counts; both tunable, both measured by section 8.

8. Weight seeds and the ledger

One record per seat per match, JSON lines in reports/ledger/:

FieldNote
genomea hash of the fields below, the key
weightsevery slider: posture, actions, the Tactics 22, the smoothing constants
capsthe components that were on
seedthe per-seat seed
roleLeader, Guard, Ranger, Wing, Builder
parentlineage
resultwon, kills, deaths, revives, territory-seconds, plan items built, road tiles, bridges finished and started, pillboxes hauled, time to first castle, starved-seconds
score = 10 win + k_kill kills - k_death deaths + k_terr territory_s / T
        + k_build items + k_road tiles - k_starve starved_s

Offline only, on the ignored census tests, headless at 4x: a hall of fame of 16 per role ranked by a rating (Elo or TrueSkill, mu - 3 sigma); a regression of score on each weight's offset, to show which sliders matter; a (1 + lambda) evolution strategy, CMA-ES past about 20 weights, on fixed seed sets; a genome becomes the default only by beating the default over a fixed census with a confidence interval clear of zero. Role sets are genomes too, so the fair fleet start is measured.

9. Territory, fronts, logistics, forts, rotation

Map (16 x 16 sectors, 1 Hz)Built fromUsed for
Influence = ours - theirspillbox coverage and tank worth, decayed per sectorthe front where it crosses zero; territory where positive
Supply distanceroad distance to a friendly depotlogistics cost
Forest lefttrees per sectorfelling; site scarcity
Favouritesforest, pads, chokes, distance to the frontwhere forts go

Forts are the castle plan and the yard on a favourite site; a second fort when the front passes a sector beyond the first's guns. Roads between forts are the attack lines. Defence points are the chokes of the road network (bridge heads, gates, junctions near the front), two to four of them.

Rotation across the team: each second rank the points by threat and the tanks by readiness (armour x shells, time to supply); swap when a tank at a hot point is more than 0.3 below one at a cold point, confirmed over two team thinks; each tank keeps DWELL 6 s and the 90% refill as a floor.

PostureWhen it scores high
Economylow material, a starved plan, no threat near
Fortifyan unfinished fort on a favourite site
Connectroad gaps between forts or to pillboxes
Expand / Assaultfront odds at least press_odds 1.5 and an attack line
Defend / Rotatethreat in our territory, or a wide armour spread

10. The plan

Step 12 of ROADMAP.md, in this order, each part behind a Caps bit so the brain of 17 October stays the baseline, each judged by a census before it is the default: 12a measure, 12b memory, 12c weights and caps, 12d intent and smoothing, 12e the team board and team brain, 12f behaviours, 12g ledger and tuner.

Paul's answers, 18 October: every either/or is a scalar ("should it x or y, we choose a scalar of how much x, and how much y is weighted"). Each question became a weight whose default is his answer and whose best value the ledger and the tuner find (ROADMAP 12h):

Upgrades and the ways to buy them, which 11g left open, are designed in ROADMAP goal 5: in-match, for supply earned from what a side holds, installed by the squad at the base; twelve base upgrades and four for the tank; bought at a depot from the coordination screen, or by a fleet commander's bot through the improvement slot, its team's posture leaning the choice.

11. Implementation and first measurement, 18 October

Built the same day, in treadcore-brain, each part behind its bit of Caps and off by default (ROADMAP goal 4, the status list):

PartModuleBitProof

12b memoryfacts.rs, memory.rs, fortify.rs turret_preview, tactics.rs valueforget for expiry; the rest alwaysthe_islands_are_counted, a_memory_runs_out_unless_kept_for_ever

Two departures from the design above. The ledger's "territory-seconds" is held_s, the pillboxes and depots a team held, summed each second: the board's territory needs the board on, and the census compares teams with it off. And the turret's confirmation needs three picks by the margin, the move's desire vector applies only to shifts under jitter (3 tiles), and the commitment bonus is in utility for the components still to be ported.

The first census, naval.map, three seeds of 900 s, respawns 3:

TrialTrial team, mean score a seatBaseline teamsCrossings searched / cachedPlan copiesCost
the baseline itself13.958.93213 / 9561028.0 us a seat-tick
every new bit on10.2911.53207 / 1,3681128.6 us a seat-tick

The baseline against itself differs by five points a seat, so three seeds measure the dice, not the parts: nothing is promoted. Over the same matches the trial team spent 0 to 44 seat-seconds a match in the base cycle and 2 to 36 relieving a teammate, and laid fewer bridge tiles, 4.7 a match against the baseline teams' 9.3 (0 to 22), which is what the crossing price asks, though not yet more than the dice allow.

Later the same day, Paul's answers (section 10) went in as step 12h: twelve more weights (68 in all) and the castle_cap bit (14 bits). The capped castle draws castle_inside pillboxes inside its wall and, once it stands, puts castle_road slots beside its road where the bastions were; past that, the Commander adds road posts every post_spacing tiles on the line to each other depot of the side, beside the road and junction_clear tiles from any junction, and the turrets rung carries outlying pillboxes out to them. In a match alone with three outlying pillboxes and a second depot, the castle stood with two inside, and the road slot and the first post were filled by 371 s (a_capped_castle_fills_then_lines_the_road). Brain tests: 55 passed, 8 ignored.

12. The capped castle made the default, 18 October

Paul made castle_cap the default the same evening ("make castle_cap the default"). The bit's default turned on in weights.rs, so every Commander, a fleet's or a person's autopilot, draws the castle of 12h: two pillboxes inside, one outside beside its road, then road posts. The castle of 11b and 11h (three towers, two bastions) stays one bit away: caps = baseline, -castle_cap in a weights file, or CENSUS_CAPS=-castle_cap in the census. The two proofs written for it, a_commander_fortifies_its_depot and a_commander_climbs_the_ladder, now set that bit off and prove the old castle still works; a_capped_castle_fills_then_lines_the_road proves the default, and default_weights_are_todays_constants says the cap is on. Brain tests: 55 passed, 8 ignored.

The measurement, and what it showed about the census

Naval.map, six seeds of 900 s, respawns 3, the trial team rotated over the three starts:

RunTrial teamBaseline teamsTrial a seat, by seed
the default against itself (the null run)12.666.446.39, 13.35, 12.51, 21.01, 11.54, 11.15
the old castle (-castle_cap) against the default11.707.676.96, 11.84, 10.48, 14.87, 21.58, 4.45

The null run plays the same settings on every team, yet its trial team scored six points more a seat than the rest: with the seeds fixed, the seat the trial team draws on each seed is a good one more often than not. So the census's trial-against-baseline figure is biased, and the only fair reading is paired, the trial run against the null run on the same seeds. Read that way, the old castle scored 0.96 a seat less than the capped one, inside the spread of either (4.45 to 21.58). The capped castle costs nothing measurable, and is the default by Paul's choice, not by the census. The first seed's castles were not finished in 900 s under either rule, and that seed's numbers barely moved.

What this changes in the method (ROADMAP goal 4): a part is judged against a null run on the same seeds, not against the baseline teams of its own matches; the tuner's fitness (trial less baseline, over fixed seeds) still ranks children fairly against one another, since every child meets the same bias.

13. The upgrades proposal, 18 October

Paul asked for the proposal the same evening, and it is PROPOSAL-UPGRADES.md. It is in GOVERNANCE.md's form, because it changes the rules and the wire: Change, Why, Must not change, Proof, the questions left for discussion, and the traces it needs before it can be accepted. In short:

  • Supply. A side earns a second count from what it holds: 1 every 10 s a pillbox, 2 a depot, 1 every 20 s a powered structure, 10 a kill, 5 a capture. Over the naval censuses (section 12) that is about 12 to 50 a minute.
  • The upgrades. Twelve for a base or the whole side, and four for the tank, each with its levels, supply price, material and how it is installed. They are hardened walls, pillbox calibre and plating, a watchtower (11j's flag), a radar mast, a gatehouse, depot stores, a yard workshop, a minefield, a dock, bridge works and a supply road; and for the tank, plating, bay, squad and hopper.
  • Buying.
    • The world checks every purchase made with Command::Buy.
    • Upgrades that need building are installed by the squad

    (PillboyTask::Install).

    • A works tab on the coordination screen lists them.
    • A fleet commander's bot buys through the base cycle's

    improvement slot, by upgrade_vs_mend, with its team's posture leaning the choice and a w_up_<name> weight for each upgrade.

    • Allies can hand each other supply (11e).
  • Must not change. The world stays deterministic and hashed. Old replays check under Scenario::upgrades false. A match where nobody buys plays as today. Nothing is carried between matches.

For this report, the proposal ties the brain to the game's economy. The improvement slot of 12h, and the weight upgrade_vs_mend that Paul's answer created, are where the bots will spend supply. The team's posture (12e) chooses what they buy. Each upgrade's worth is a weight in the flat array (12c), so the tuner (12g) can learn the bots' buying as it learns their fighting. Until the sim step lands, upgrade_vs_mend has nothing to weigh, and the brain buys nothing.

14. The fair census, 18 October

Section 12 found the census biased: the trial team's seat on each fixed seed is a good one more often than not. The cure, backlog item 16, is in brain_census: each seed is played three times, every team in the trial seat once (CENSUS_FAIR, on by default; CENSUS_FAIR=0 keeps the old rotation, a third of the matches). With every team the same, the three matches of a seed are the same match, so a null run reads exactly even; one seed of 120 s read 13.93 against 13.93.

The capped castle measured again, fairly, on naval.map, three seeds of 900 s, nine matches:

RunOld castle (trial)Capped castle (baseline)Difference a seat
paired against a null run, six seeds (section 12)11.7012.66-0.96
fair, three seeds8.369.08-0.72

The two methods agree: the old castle is a little behind and inside the noise, and the capped castle stays the default. A fair census costs three times the matches, so make tune now defaults to two seeds (six matches a child).

15. Smoothing finished: the ladder and the advance, 18 October

The rest of 12d, under smooth:

  • The Auto ladder confirms a change of rung. A new rung must win confirm thinks in a row while the old one still holds; a think is a quarter second. A threat to the base (DefendBase), or the old rung failing its own condition, changes the rung at once (Commander::rung_challenger).
  • The decision to close is an averaged advance scalar. The odds are read as -1 (at engage_odds or under) to +1 (at press_odds or over). The reading is averaged once a think by beta and held between thinks. The tank presses only above advance_band (0.35, a new weight), falls back to mass only below minus it, and engages in between (Tactician::smooth). Proof: in the_square_law, one think of two-to-one odds is not yet a press, and two are.

The fair census, naval.map, two seeds of 900 s, six matches:

TrialTrial teamBaseline teamsMoves a match, all seats
smooth7.559.474,661

Smoothing cost 1.9 a seat over six matches; the spread of a team's score across them was 3.28 to 14.29. It did not cut the Moves asked of the world either (the old castle's nine matches asked 4,391 a match). So smooth stays off. The likely reasons to look at next: holding a rung for half a second delays leaving a rung whose work is done, and a smoothed stance arrives at a fight a think or two late. Its weights (confirm, beta, advance_band, margin) are the tuner's to try before the bit is judged again.

16. The escort, 18 October

Paul's first message asked that the tanks "go out on a mission with the team to start moving the pillbox along the newly created road leading back to the base". The pieces existed: the turrets rung fetches an outlying pillbox, the wingmen fly the leader's wedge, and the world's router already prefers road (a road tile costs 0.6 against grass's 1.0), so a pillbox carried home comes along the road where there is one. What was missing was the leaving together. Under the new bit escort, a fleet leader at its base whose fetch trip runs farther than fetch_gather (20 tiles, a new weight) gathers its wingmen first, with the same gathering the hunt and the expand use. The fleet then goes out in its wedge and screens the carry home. Proof: a_far_fetch_gathers_the_fleet. With the wingman 45 tiles off and the pillboxes 26 and 30 out, the leader gathered for the fetch and its squad lifted the first pillbox by 324 s. Brain tests: 56 passed.

The fair census (two naval seeds, six matches of 900 s) read 7.70 against 7.70 exactly. No team reached a far fetch in 900 s, so the bit never acted and the matches were the null run's. escort stays off until a longer census (goal 5's longer matches) can see it work.

17. The sliders, 18 October

Paul's first message asked for "a slider for each coefficient weights". The weights page is the coordination screen's second page: U opens it (a new rebindable action, tune). It has a row for each of the 15 capabilities, a switch, and a row for each of the 70 weights, a slider (client::tune).

  • Up and down pick a row; left and right move it one step.
  • Delete puts the row back to its default; Enter saves; U or Esc goes back to the ladder.
  • Each row shows its value and a bar from nothing to twice the default, the default at the middle. A * marks a row that differs from the default. Under the rows is the doc line of the picked row, with its default and its step.

A step is one for a rounded weight (the doc says "(rounded)"; five docs gained the word: focus_cap, answer_cap, kite, confirm, confirm_turret). Otherwise it is a tenth of the default, taken to 1, 2 or 5 of its power of ten: alpha 0.05, near_scale 2, forget_for 10 (W::step). Settings::nudge never goes under nothing and keeps four places, so three steps up and three down is the number it was.

Whose bots, the table of section 10 decided: the host's --weights file stays every bot's, and a player's sliders are their own team's. The page gives its settings to the tank under the goal at once, and, when the window hosts, to the bots of the player's side, again at each new match (Brain::set_settings, new, a no-op for a brain without weights). A remote player's page reaches only their own tank, and it says so. Enter keeps the team's settings in ~/.config/treadcore/weights, beside the keys file, and the next start reads them for the team only. Without that file the page starts from --weights or the defaults.

Two changes in the brain made a mid-match change safe:

  • Commander::set_settings updates the tactician in place, keeping its target and the team's calls.
  • The four switches it used to turn off for good (dodge, pings, rotate, bridges) now put away the role's own setting when turned off and give it back when turned on again (CapHeld).

Before this, no settings ever reached the window's pilot. It now has the page's, so --weights or the team file steer the person's own tank on auto too.

Some switches are read only when a match is dealt: board gives a team its board in fleet::equip. Set on the page, such a switch takes hold at the next match.

Proof:

  • a_slider_steps_and_steps_back; a_switch_comes_back_and_a_slider_keeps_the_target;
  • the_page_moves_a_weight_and_puts_it_back, the_page_switches_a_capability_and_wraps, a_saved_page_reads_back, the_bar_marks_the_default_in_the_middle.
  • Brain tests: 58 passed; client: 31.
  • The window, driven with hyprctl dispatch sendshortcut and shot with grim (journal 0043): Space, then U, then a switch and a slider moved, Delete, then Esc back to the ladder with the fleet listed.

The page measures nothing by itself, so it has no census. What it changes is who can tune: a player can now try a weight in the match in front of them, and a saved file is a genome the ledger can read.

18. Bridge jobs on the team board, 18 October

Section 6.2 planned it: "A part-laid bridge is a job on the board, picked up by the next tank with material, dropped only when the strategy layer drops the crossing." Before this, a bridge given up (stalled 40 s, no material in reach, the layer dead) stayed in the water half laid. Its layer went on by boat and wrote the target off.

Under the new bit bridge_jobs, which needs board:

  • Every member laying a bridge posts it to its team board each think: the water tiles still to lay, in order from the laid end, and what it crosses to (Hop::bridge_job, TeamBoard::post_bridge). The same bridge is known by a tile or the target in common.
  • Tiles laid by anyone come off; a bridge laid, or untouched for bridge_keep (180 s, new), comes off the board (prune_bridges). That is the "strategy layer drops the crossing" of the plan, as a time.
  • A bridge whose layer has not posted for 3 s is free. A teammate free of more pressing work takes the nearest whose next tile is within bridge_reach (40 tiles, new): the squad aboard with material, not resupplying, no trip under way, a bridge it has not given up itself (take_bridge, Hop::bridge_refused).
  • The taker's hop lays the rest from the laid end and stops there (Hop::adopt_bridge). The crossing was the other tank's errand, so a bridge finished or given up ends the trip; a given-up one stays on the board for another.

Who is free of more pressing work was the step's finding. First it was a tank on auto whose rung was expand or guard. A temporary counter in one census match showed no such tank: the free bridges were the leader's, given up as it sailed on, and the only teammates near them with material were its wingmen. So who takes became a scalar, per the either/or rule: bridge_wing (rounded, default 1) lets a wingman with material take a free bridge, leaving the wedge to do it.

The census gained CENSUS_BASE_CAPS, the baseline teams' capabilities, so a part that needs another is tried against that one on: here board for every team and bridge_jobs for the trial team. It also counts the trial team's seat-seconds "for the team".

Proof:

  • a_part_laid_bridge_waits_for_the_next_tank: posted, not free while its layer lays it, not taken from too far or by a tank that gave it up, taken by the nearest, laid tiles struck off, dropped untouched and when laid.
  • a_teammate_finishes_a_part_laid_bridge: a channel four tiles wide across the map, its first tile laid; a tank on auto with material took the other three and laid them by 9 s, and the bridge came off the board. With the bit off nobody touched it in 240 s.
  • Brain tests: 60 passed.

The fair census (naval.map, two seeds, six matches of 900 s, respawns 3) read 7.31 a seat for the trial team against 8.01. The bit acted in five of the six matches, 69 to 783 seat-seconds. One trial team laid 36 bridge tiles against 15 to 20 in the others. The 0.70 a seat it cost is about the noise of six matches; bridge_jobs stays off, and bridge_wing, bridge_reach and bridge_keep are for the tuner.

19. A dodge for direct fire, measured first, 18 October

Backlog 8 and finding "direct fire is never dodged before it lands" asked for a dodge of direct fire beside the dodge of indirect shells. Before designing one, the step measured whether a tank that sees a direct shell launched can get out of its way at all.

The numbers are against it:

  • Direct range is 10 tiles and a direct shell flies at 40 tiles a second, so it is in the air five ticks at most (0.25 s).
  • It hits if the tank is within DIRECT_HIT_RADIUS (0.8 tiles) of where it was aimed when it lands, and a bot aims where the tank will be at the speed and heading it has now (lead).
  • In 0.25 s a standing tank accelerating at 4 tiles a second squared covers 0.125 tiles. A tank at full speed turning at its 3 radians a second strays about v w t^2 / 2 = 4 x 3 x 0.0625 / 2, roughly 0.4 tiles, off the line the gunner led; braking gains about 0.125.

Measured (a_led_direct_shot_cannot_be_dodged, lib.rs): a tank crossing at full speed, a gunner square to it at 4, 7 and 9.5 tiles firing a led shot, and the target answering the launch five ways (carry on, reverse, turn away, turn toward, stop). Every one was hit at every range. The test asserts it, so a change of the rules that made a dodge possible would show.

A weave before the shot is no better against a bot, because it leads from the speed and heading at the moment it fires, and only what changes during the flight counts.

So the only defence against direct fire is denying the shot: not being inside an enemy gun's range, in its line, when that gun is ready before one's own. Every tank's reload is in the observation. Kiting already does this against the one enemy a tank is fighting; a builder, a hauler or a bridge-layer shot by a tank it is not fighting has nothing. Three ways on, for Paul to choose (ROADMAP backlog 17):

  1. a "duck" reflex behind its own switch: out of range or line when a hostile gun ready before one's own covers the tank and the exchange is not one it wins, with a weight for how far it outranks the errand; measured by the fair census;
  2. close the item as impossible, as this section records;
  3. change the rules (a slower direct shell, a smaller hit radius) so a dodge is possible, which changes how every match plays.
