L7 uneven commitmentdesire vectors and confirmation, as smooth measured worse in four readings, and since LR 46 a weight, w_smooth, 0.05 by default
L8 a reactive economythe base cycle (cycle, off)
partial_cmp().unwrap() on a NaNanswered (LR 23): every float comparison a total_cmp, in the brain, the client and the sim

The numbers, 18 October evening:

  • 12,566 lines in 17 files (8,458 in 8 on 18 October morning).
  • Brain tests: 64 passed, 8 ignored; client: 31; sim: 50.
  • 76 weights and 17 switches, with the duck (LR 21); 77 and 18 with the posture (LR 24); 83 with its scores (LR 25).
  • 23 to 33 us a seat-tick in the census.
  • On by default: kite, pings, dodge, bridges, rotate, and since 18 October castle_cap; since 19 October the landfall's three weights (landfall_memory 30, landfall_shy 1, landfall_wait 10, LR 38).
  • Measured fairly: the capped castle against the old, +0.72 a seat; smooth -1.92; escort even (never reached); bridge_jobs -0.70 with board on for every team; duck -0.51 (LR 21). At twelve seeds each, with the error (LR 24): none outside two errors.
  • 19 October (LR 27): 13,193 lines; 349 functions, 122 undocumented; brain tests 69 passed, 8 ignored; 87 weights; 24.8 us a seat-tick. Where it dies: the dodge (43% of deaths in 4.5% of the time) and the run for the beach, landfall (20% in 3.2%); the ladder, rotation and the fleet a small share.
  • 19 October evening (LR 38): 13,566 lines; 360 functions, 121 undocumented; brain tests 73 passed, 8 ignored; 90 weights. The first part since the dodge measured outside the noise: guns remembered for the landfall, +2.31 +/- 0.58 a seat over 48 seeds with its two companions, landfall deaths halved; on by default. The dodge itself measured: off, -2.45 +/- 0.67 (LR 29).

What the prototype has not shown yet: that any of its new parts makes the bots win more. The fair census can now tell a real difference from the starts, but six matches of 900 s are not enough to see the parts that pay late (the escort, bridges, the base cycle), and none has had the twelve seeds the roadmap asks for. That is the next measurement, and goal 5's longer matches the one after.

21. The duck: direct fire denied, 18 October

Paul chose the first of section 19's three ways: "build the duck reflex". A shot that cannot be dodged once fired can still be denied, by not being in reach when the gun is ready.

The reflex (Commander::duck, the bit duck, off):

  • Which guns. A hostile tank with shells, not afloat, within 12 tiles (direct range plus 2, since its lead reaches past its range by the target's own motion) and in line. Its gun is ready within duck_ready (1.5 s), and before mine, or I have no shells.
  • Seen or remembered. Sight and direct range are both 10 tiles, so a gunner just past sight leads its shot into it unseen. The first duel showed exactly that: the gun that fired was not in the observation. So a gun is remembered for duck_memory (5 s) where it was last seen, its reload counted down. A direct shell in flight refreshes the memory, seen or not, because it carries its gun's position (from) and says the gun has just reloaded.
  • When. Only with the tank's armour share at or under w_duck. This is the scalar between safety and the errand: 1 always ducks, 0 never.
  • Where to. It looks at sixteen points around it, at the distance that clears the gun with a margin (13 tiles out, 3 to 8 tiles away). Of those out of its reach or line, it takes the one nearest the errand (the last Move's goal); if none, it backs straight away.
  • It holds the point a second at a time. The guns stay free while it ducks, as in a dodge. It never ducks afloat or while its squad mends a wreck. Once the gun is no longer a threat, the errand plans again.

The census counts the trial team's seat-seconds "ducking".

Proof: a_tank_ducks_a_gun_ready_before_its_own.

  • The rules. It ducks out of reach when the gun will fire first, and not when its own gun is ready first, not with w_duck 0, and not with the bit off.
  • The duel. A gunner standing 7 tiles off fires a led shot whenever it can; the tank holds its post with its gun held reloading, never able to answer. Kiting is off in both arms, since kiting already holds a fighting tank past the gun's reach; the duck is for the tank that does not kite. Over 40 s: hit 2 times ducking, 3 standing. The hit that remains comes when the memory lapses and the post, 7 tiles from the gun, draws the tank back into its fire. Holding a post under a waiting gun is the trade w_duck is there to weigh.
  • Brain tests: 62 passed, 8 ignored.

Building it took four duels, each of which found something before the reflex did:

  1. Three direct hits down a tank, so counting hits needed a fair cap.
  2. A tank whose gun is ready does not duck, by the rule, and a duel that silenced its gun hid that.
  3. Return fire and strike drones ended the match in four seconds.
  4. The unseen gun at 10.3 tiles, the real finding.

Fair census (naval.map, two seeds, six matches of 900 s, respawns 3): 7.01 a seat for the trial team against 7.52. It ducked in all six matches, 2 to 16 seat-seconds in five and 567 in one. The 0.51 is inside the noise; duck stays off, and its four weights are for the tuner.

22. The status as codes, 18 October

The last of 12b's string work. Finding L3 counted "six why strings and a doing string formatted every tick" as repeated work. The step measured the cost first, and found the case for codes was not speed.

The cost. One census match (naval.map, 300 s), as it was and with the why formatting stubbed out: 14.9 and 15.0 us a seat-tick as it was, 15.3 and 15.7 without. The difference is noise. why is built only for tanks on auto. A doing string is one or two formats a think, about 0.1 us against a think of 15.

The case. The brain's own logic and its measurements read the sentences back:

  • a guard counted as idle if its words began "on guard" or "to the post", and the rotation looked for exactly "on guard";
  • a focus call went out if the target's reason said "called", "on it" or "one shot";
  • the census counted its parts by substring, the quad census its kiting steps by phrase and its revives by "REVIVE:";
  • the tests read the words.

Rewording any of those sentences would have changed play or a count, silently.

The change (status.rs, new):

  • Act. What a Commander is doing, 48 codes, three of them carrying a KiteStep and two a step of the hop or the base cycle. It is set beside the words at all 81 places the Commander writes them: 76 through set_doing, and 5 that hold a borrow of the fort plan set the two fields directly.
  • HopAct. The island hop's 23 steps, set at its 28 writes (Hop::say).
  • CycleAct. The base cycle's steps; Cycle::improvement returns the code, not words.
  • KiteStep. The tactician's eight kiting steps. Kite::word became Kite::step, and its words come from step.word().
  • Tags. The tactician keeps bits beside its reason (one shot, called, on it), and the focus call reads them.
  • Why. The ladder's reasons, a code and its numbers; the coordination screen puts them in words with why_text.
  • Brain::act. The census and the quad census count by code, the ledger can carry one, and code() is a stable dotted name (kite.step_in.reloading, hop.bridging).
  • The words stay, exactly as they were, for people: the status line, the coordination screen, the logs.

The quad census's diagnostic histograms (the status shapes, what a seat was doing when it went down, the stuck detector) still read the words. They want detail the codes do not carry: one hop code covers "bridging, the squad is laying it" and "bridging, fetching material".

Proof:

  • every_code_is_its_own: no two codes the same.
  • The tests that checked logic by words now check codes: the duck, the escort, the base under threat, and the tactician's three kiting tests.
  • The equivalence. The codes must change nothing a tank does. One census match (600 s, board, cycle, escort, duck, bridge_jobs on for the trial team, board for all) before and after: identical, in score, kills, deaths, thinks, moves and crossings.
  • Brain tests: 63 passed, 8 ignored; client 31.

What is left of 12b is the Observation shared per team, a sim change that must keep the world hash.

23. No panic on a NaN, 18 October

Backlog 10 and 19, from the review's "also" (section 2): partial_cmp().unwrap() would panic on a NaN. Comparing two floats with partial_cmp gives None when either is NaN, and .unwrap() on that ends the process. In the brain that was 74 calls: picking the nearest pillbox, the closest depot, the least hurt tank, the best landing. One NaN in a position, from a bug anywhere or a malformed observation, would have stopped every bot of a host at once.

The change. Every one is now a total_cmp, which orders every float, NaN included, and never fails:

  • 74 in the brain, in commander.rs (26), lib.rs (25), hop.rs (10), fortify.rs (8), crew.rs (4) and rotation.rs (1);
  • 19 more in the window's code (input.rs 5, goal.rs 1) and the simulation (lib.rs 11, map.rs 2), where a NaN panic is as bad.

A one-off script rewrote each X.partial_cmp(Y).unwrap() as X.total_cmp(Y), matching parentheses. One comparison was of a tuple (material short, distance), which has no total_cmp; it now compares the parts in turn. Two in the map's route search already fell back to "equal" and could not panic; they stay as they were.

total_cmp orders the real numbers exactly as partial_cmp did. It differs only by putting -0.0 before +0.0, and the values compared here are distances, health and scores, never a signed zero.

Proof:

  • The bug, shown. a_nan_does_not_panic_the_brain puts a hostile tank, a pillbox and a depot at NaN in an observation and lets a Commander think forty times under each of seven goals. Put into the code of the commit before, it panics on the first goal (Auto) at a partial_cmp().unwrap(). On the fixed code it passes.
  • Nothing else changed. One census match (600 s, five new switches on) before and after: identical, in score, kills, deaths, thinks, moves and crossings, and the same numbers as LR 22's run. Every crate's tests pass: brain 64, client 31, net 14, proto 3, sim 50, the sim's replay and world-hash tests among them.

One wrong turn on the way. The first run of the new test sat for ten minutes and looked like a NaN driving something into a loop. It was the build: changing the sim rebuilds every crate above it. Run by itself under a hard timeout, the test passed in an instant.

24. Each switch alone, twelve seeds; the posture read, 18 October

Backlog 18 and the roadmap's "next": every capability that is off, measured alone over the fair census at twelve seeds. Until now a part was judged "inside the noise" by eye, from two or three seeds, with no number for the noise.

The noise as a number. The census now prints the difference a seat seed by seed, and its standard error over the seeds (ledger::mean_se, the_standard_error_of_a_sample). A seed's three matches share its starts, so a seed is one paired reading; within two standard errors of zero is "inside the noise". A null run reads 0 with an error of 0 on every seed, as it must. make switches (scripts/switches.sh) runs the census once for each switch and prints the table below; a switch that needs the board is tried with the board on for every team.

The measurement (naval.map, respawns 3, 900 s, 12 seeds, every team in the trial seat: 36 matches a switch, 396 in all; run three at a time, so the microseconds read high):

SwitchWithTrialBaselineDifference a seatErrorActed (trial team, seat-seconds)
smooth7.989.96-1.981.25always (it smooths every move)
team_rotateboard8.259.09-0.830.87relieving 879
forget8.548.90-0.360.23(retries are not counted)
column8.989.05-0.060.41on bridges
board8.758.750.000.00never: the same matches as the baseline
island_site8.758.750.000.00never
escort8.768.75+0.010.01far fetches, one seed
priced_crossing9.209.09+0.110.56cheaper by boat 0; road posts 92 against 30
cycle9.088.77+0.310.43base cycle 243
duck8.938.54+0.391.04ducking 911
bridge_jobsboard9.118.67+0.440.91for the team 4,438

None is outside two standard errors. smooth comes nearest (1.6 errors below), where LR 15 put it with six matches (-1.92). duck and bridge_jobs, slightly behind at two seeds, are slightly ahead at twelve: the sign of a difference under one error is the dice's.

What twelve seeds can and cannot tell. A seed's difference spreads by 0.8 to 4.3 a seat (smooth 4.3, duck 3.6, team_rotate 3.0, forget 0.8). To put a real half point a seat outside two errors takes (2 x spread / 0.5)^2 seeds: 10 for forget, 140 for team_rotate, 200 for duck; a whole point, a quarter of that. The 396 matches took 22 minutes three at a time, about 1,000 an hour, so 200 seeds (600 matches) is a 35-minute run: every switch at a half point is one night's census. The tuner's two seeds (make tune) can only move weights whose effect is several points.

Three switches never acted, and the census could not have found that by score alone, only by the identical matches:

  • island_site chooses among the side's depots only when a tank has no base yet. In a fleet every tank's base is its leader's depot, found first, so the island rule is never asked. It needs to stand where a naval start actually chooses: which neutral depot the leader takes first.
  • escort acts on a far fetch, and a 900 s naval match has about one (LR 16 said so).
  • board alone changes no decision. It draws influence, the front, the defence points and a posture, and only team_rotate and bridge_jobs read any of it; its own act, adopting a job a crew missed, happened in none of 36 matches. The team's picture was drawn and nobody looked at it.

The last is the brain's structure, not a tuning: goal 4's team board was to be the one picture the team decides on (LR 5, LR 9). The step that follows makes the posture read.

The posture read (posture, off; needs board). The board's posture is what the team should mostly be doing; the Auto ladder is what a tank does, six rungs taken first-match, each a yes or no (L1). The ladder now reads the posture by a blend, as every either/or is a scalar (Rung::pick):

score(rung) = (1 - w) order(rung) + w lean(rung, posture)
order: 1 for DefendBase down to 0 for Guard, in steps of 0.2
lean:  1 for a rung the posture leans toward, else 0

The rung taken is the best of those switched on that hold. w is w_posture; at 0 the pick is the first that holds, as the ladder always was, and a leaned rung climbs past fewer than 5 w / (1 - w) rungs above it, so from 0.5 the posture decides among the rungs that hold. DefendBase, holding, is never passed. The leanings (Rung::leaned): Defend to Guard; Fortify to Fortify and Turrets; Connect to Turrets and Expand; Expand to Expand and Hunt; Economy, short of material, to Expand (depots).

What the first match found. On the first census match the trial team scored 3.64 against 11.02. The meter (new: the postures read and the picks they changed) said why: the posture was Defend in 94% of the picks, and the lean sent the tanks home to Guard. The posture had never been read, so it had never been checked. Two faults:

  • Defend's test was the sector threat at the base over 0.5. Each hostile tank adds 1 / (1 + d / 8) to every sector of the map, so eight tanks 80 tiles off add 0.73 between them. The base was always under threat.
  • Defend was also taken when the members' readiness spread past 0.6: any team with one tank low on shells.

The fix: Defend is hostile worth within the base's alarm (BASE_ALARM, 16 tiles), a full tank 1, over 0.5; the spread is left to rotation (team_rotate), which is where it belongs. the_team_defends_a_threat_at_the_base_not_far_off puts eight hostile tanks 80 tiles off and then one at the gate. On the old rule it fails; on the new it passes. After the fix, over 48 seeds, the posture read Fortify 47%, Connect 19%, Expand 18%, Economy 16% and Defend 0.4%.

The measurement (as above, with board on for every team):

w_postureSeedsPicks changedDifference a seatError
0.2510+0.08(one seed)
0.35125,122+0.390.36
0.5 (= 1)1250,677+1.211.31
0.548164,606-0.810.65

At 0.25 a lean climbs one rung, and the posture's leanings sit two below the rung that holds, so nothing changed. At 0.5 and at 1 the matches are identical, as the formula says they must be. The first twelve seeds read +1.21, which is promising. Thirty-six more seeds, run three ranges at a time (CENSUS_SEED0), read -1.30, -2.16 and -1.01; over all 48 the difference is -0.81 with an error of 0.65, 21 seeds ahead and 27 behind. The twelve were the dice. posture stays off, and w_posture's default is 0.5, the value measured.

What the step shows about the method. A part that is never read is never tested; the first match it is read in finds what was wrong. A reading of +1.2 on twelve seeds with an error of 1.3 is no reading. A part is promising only outside two errors, and confirmed only on seeds it was not chosen on.

Proof:

  • the_posture_leans_the_ladder: at weight 0 or with no posture, the pick is the first rung that holds for all 4,096 combinations of rungs on and holding; DefendBase is never passed; 0.25 climbs one rung and 0.5 every rung; a switched-off rung is never picked.
  • the_team_defends_a_threat_at_the_base_not_far_off, failing on the old rule.
  • the_standard_error_of_a_sample.
  • make test: brain 67 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

25. The posture as scores, 18 October

Backlog 21, from LR 24 and CI-001 finding A3: the board's posture was itself a first-match ladder on thresholds, where LR 9 designed five scores ("when it scores high"). By Paul's rule, every either/or is a scalar.

The change (board.rs, fortify.rs, weights.rs). Each posture has a score from 0 to 1, from what the board and the rebuilding member know (Home: the plan starved, Plan::castle_left, Plan::slots_left; each member now posts its squad's material):

PostureScoreLR 9's "when it scores high"
Defendhostile worth within the base's alarm, half a tank fullthreat in our territory
Economy1 if the plan is starved, else the share of the team's material spentlow material, a starved plan
Fortifythe share of the castle not yet builtan unfinished fort
Connectthe share of pillbox slots and road posts still emptyroad gaps between forts or to pillboxes
Expandthe team's odds against the hostiles it sees: 0 at even, 1 at press_odds; none seen is no front, evenfront odds at least press_odds

Each score has its weight (w_pos_defend, w_pos_economy, w_pos_fortify, w_pos_connect, w_pos_expand, all 1). The ladder of 12e is kept, and the posture is the blend:

value(p) = (1 - k) [p is the ladder's pick] + k w_p score_p

The highest value wins; ties go in the ladder's order (Defend, Economy, Expand, Connect, Fortify); if nothing scores, Fortify, as the ladder had it. k is posture_scored, 1 by default: at 0 the posture is the ladder's, exactly. The board keeps both picks and the scores, for the screen and the tests. The weights now number 83.

Proof: each_posture_scores_its_own_case builds the five cases. A castle just planned at even odds is Fortify; the castle up with half its slots empty is Connect; the squads spent is Economy; a starved plan is Economy; eight of ours against two of theirs in sight is Expand; a hostile at the gate is Defend over a planned castle. At posture_scored 0 the pick is the ladder's in every case. The ladder alone would have said Fortify in three of the cases, since it has no term for empty slots, spent material or odds.

The measurement (the fair census as in LR 24, posture at w_posture 0.5, board on for every team, 48 seeds in three ranges of 16, the same seeds as LR 24's):

Postured a seatErrorPostures read (econ / fort / conn / exp / def)Picks changed
the ladder's (LR 24)-0.810.6516% / 47% / 19% / 18% / 0.4%164,606
scored (posture_scored 1)-1.040.5837% / 43% / 6% / 15% / 0.5%131,278
scored less the ladder's, seed by seed-0.230.74
the two averaged, seed by seed-0.930.49

The ranges read +0.40, -1.46 and -2.07. The last 16 seeds alone were outside two errors, and the 48 are not. That is one more case of a range reading its own luck.

What it shows. How the posture is worked out makes no measurable difference: scored and ladder's are -0.23 ± 0.74 apart. What both have in common is that they override the ladder's own order, and together they read about a point a seat behind (-0.93 ± 0.49, 1.9 errors). The lean is the suspect, not the posture. The likely mechanism is in the leanings. Economy is now read 37% of the time, from material spent on building, and it leans to Expand, so tanks leave Fortify and Turrets for depots. That is a hypothesis; the census does not yet count which rungs gained. Backlog 27 adds that count, and the leanings themselves are weights for the tuner once it can see a point (backlog 23).

posture stays off. The scored posture is the board's default, since it is LR 9's design and measures no worse; the ladder is posture_scored = 0.

  • make test: brain 68 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

26. Which rungs the lean moves, and where the time goes, 18 October

Backlog 27, from LR 25: leaning the Auto ladder by the team's posture reads about a point a seat behind in both forms of the posture. The hypothesis: Economy, read 37% of the time from material spent, leans tanks off Fortify and Turrets.

The instruments (no change to play):

  • Brain::rung: the Auto rung a think took, or None if the think did not reach the ladder (a reflex, a fleet slot, a job, another goal). The Commander keeps rung_now, cleared at each think.
  • The census counts the trial team's seat-seconds on each rung.
  • The meter counts each pick the posture changed, by the rung that held first and the rung taken (Meter::moved, 6 x 6).
  • CENSUS_ACTS=1: the trial team's every seat-second by status code (Act::code), dead seats apart, ladder thinks marked.

Which rungs (48 seeds, the same as LR 24 and 25; trial team seat-seconds; "no lean" is the null run, board on for all):

RungNo leanLeanLean, Economy off (w_pos_economy 0)
DefendBase609381422
Fortify14,2629,96710,609
Turrets8,4271,0263,961
Hunt14245151
Expand9,19711,0348,928
Guard03134
Picks changed0131,27844,975
d a seat0-1.04 +/- 0.58-0.92 +/- 0.54

With the lean, 84% of the changed picks were Fortify to Expand and 12% Turrets to Expand. The castle stands later, so the Turrets rung, which fills it with pillboxes, nearly vanishes (-88%). Economy caused two thirds of the changed picks: building spends material, spent material reads as Economy, and Economy leans away from building, a feedback. With Economy off, Turrets recovers about half. The score does not move: Economy off against Economy on, seed by seed, is +0.13 +/- 0.47. The hypothesis is not confirmed. The lean does move tanks off the castle, but the census cannot see that cost, if it is one.

Where the time goes (CENSUS_ACTS, 16 seeds, null run, the trial team's 205,537 seat-seconds, the 40 commonest codes):

ShareWhat
46.7%dead (waiting to respawn, or out of respawns)
18.9%the island hop: sailing 5.5%, bridging 4.4%, by sea 2.1%, idle 1.5%, to the boat 1.5%, holding the launch 1.2%, and the rest
7.3%rotation (push and defence points)
5.6%the Auto ladder: fortify 2.1%, turrets 1.2%, take a depot 0.9%, no way to the post 0.7%, gather 0.6%
4.5%dodging
4.2%the fleet: wing 2.7%, wing at sea 1.5%
3.9%rallies and kiting toward a ping
2.4%taking depots and pillboxes outside the ladder
1.7%crew jobs
0.7% to 1.9% eachgather 1.9%, revive 1.0%, resupply 0.9%, escort 0.7%

Three things follow:

  1. The ladder is a small lever. It decides 5.6% of a team's seat-seconds, about 11% of its living time. A change to the ladder's pick can only move that share. The posture's lean changed most ladder picks and moved the score by a point at most.
  2. Half the time is dead. A seat dies 3.4 times a match on average with 3 respawns; a respawn waits 20 s, so most of the dead time is seats out of respawns. The census, as set, measures attrition. A part that pays late (the castle's turrets, the escort, bridge jobs, the cycle) is cut off before it can.
  3. The island hop is the largest living share (about 36% of living time), on naval.map. Speeding a crossing is worth more than any rung.

What this changes. Backlog 28: survival, since what kills half the team is the largest lever there is. The quad census's "what a seat was doing when it went down" can now be read by code. Backlog 29: a census with unlimited respawns, or longer matches (goal 5), so the late parts are measured at all. The leanings (a 5 x 6 table of 0s and 1s) stay as they are until the census can see a point.

Proof: no behaviour changed. The lean run of LR 25 run again gives the same per-seed differences to the hundredth, and the null run reads 0 on every seed. make test: brain 68 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

27. What kills half the team, 19 October

Backlog 28, from LR 26: 46.7% of the trial team's seat-seconds are dead. The hypothesis: what a seat was doing when it went down, read by code, names the two or three situations that kill most.

The instruments (no change to play):

  • CENSUS_ACTS now also keeps each trial seat's last five codes, one a second, and at each TankDestroyed counts the code held last, every code held in the 5 s before (once a death), and what took the tank (killed_by: the weapon whose hit names it on that tick, whether it sank, and whether the shooter was the lone RuleBot or no one).
  • The meter counts the Commander's shell dodges, ashore and afloat (Dodge::metered), and those hit, those hit by the dodged shell itself (on its landing tick), and those killed, between the dodge's start and its shell's landing (Dodge::watch, meter::dodge, meter::dodge_hit). A dead seat still reads its dodge (Brain::dead, called each tick in place of a think).
  • Act::Landfall: afloat with no hop under way, the doctrine's run for the beach (RuleBot::landfall_order). It read hop.idle before.
  • The dodge's four constants are weights (dodge_horizon 4 s, dodge_margin 0.8, dodge_clear 2.5 tiles) and one new one, dodge_hold (0): the share of the shell's flight a tank stays at its dodge point once there. At 0 it goes back to its errand on arrival, as before; at 1 it stays out until the shell lands. The default numbers pick the same point as the constants (a_dodge_holds_out_of_the_splash_by_its_weight).

The reading (16 seeds, null run, naval.map, 900 s, respawns 3; the trial team's 819 deaths, 17.1 a match):

Code held lastDeathsShare of deathsShare of timeHeld in the 5 s before
dodge35443.2%4.5%67.3%
landfall16520.1%3.2%30.2%
hop.sailing657.9%4.7%15.4%
resupply323.9%0.9%6.3%
hop.holding_launch283.4%1.1%8.1%
hop.bridging242.9%4.3%4.3%
hop.by_sea222.7%2.0%4.9%
everything else living12915.8%32.9%

What took them (the twelve commonest causes, 791 of the 819): direct fire 253, indirect 275, strikes 246, pillboxes 17; 399 of the 791 sank in a boat. The commonest pairs: direct fire on a boat at landfall 132 (16.1%), indirect fire on a boat in a dodge 91, indirect fire ashore in a dodge 87, a strike in a dodge 78.

The dodge's own meter (the same run):

DodgesHit before the shell landedBy the dodged shellKilled
Ashore7,9112,691 (34.0%)1,314 (16.6%)582 (7.4%)
Afloat3,408621 (18.2%)258 (7.6%)567 (16.6%)

Three things follow:

  1. Two situations make 63% of the deaths: the dodge and the run for the beach, in 7.7% of the time. A seat dies once every 26 s it is dodging and once every 40 s on landfall, against once every 135 s of living time overall. The hypothesis is confirmed in its form: two situations, not ten.
  2. A dodge is a symptom before it is a cause. A tank dodges because a shell is on it; under fire is where tanks die. That a dodge precedes most deaths does not say the dodge kills. Ashore, a sixth of dodges are still hit by the shell they dodged, and a third by something. Afloat, a hit is a kill 91% of the time: a boat sinks. The test is causal: the dodge off (-dodge) and dodge_hold 1, each at 48 seeds against the null run (next step).
  3. Landfall is the second killer, and it is not a dodge. 132 deaths are a boat sunk by direct fire while running for a beach, though landfall_order prices each beach by the threats near it. A boat in range of a tank's gun is under fire it cannot answer.

What this changes. Backlog 28's look is done; its part is the dodge, measured causally first, then the landfall (backlog 30). The ladder, rotation and the fleet are a small share of deaths.

Proof: no behaviour changed. The null run reads 0 on every seed (trial 8.38, baseline 8.38). make test: brain 69 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

28. Holding out of the splash, 19 October

Backlog 28, the first causal reading after LR 27. A sixth of ashore dodges are caught by the shell they dodged. One reading: the tank reaches its dodge point early, drops the dodge (Dodge::holding holds only while it drives) and goes back to its errand under the shell. If so, dodge_hold 1, which keeps the tank at its point until the shell lands, should cut the hits by the dodged shell and save tanks.

The run: the defaults dumped (CENSUS_DUMP) with dodge_hold = 1, as the trial team's weights; 48 seeds in three ranges of 16 (CENSUS_SEED0 0, 16, 32), naval.map, 900 s, respawns 3, the fair census, the ledger off. Against the null run (the defaults for all).

d a seatAshore: hit, by the shell, killedAfloat: hit, by the shell, killed
Null (LR 27, 16 seeds)034.0%, 16.6%, 7.4% of 7,91118.2%, 7.6%, 16.6% of 3,408
dodge_hold 1 (48 seeds)-1.18 +/- 0.5534.2%, 16.7%, 7.3% of 23,09915.5%, 6.0%, 13.9% of 10,002

The meter counts every Commander's dodges, the two baseline teams' as well as the trial team's, so its rates move a third as far as the trial team's own.

Not confirmed, and worse. Holding at the dodge point does not cut the hits by the dodged shell ashore (16.7% against 16.6%). The tank that is caught is caught before it gets there, not after. The trial team loses 1.18 a seat, just outside two errors (1.10): a tank that stands out the whole flight leaves its errand for nothing. Afloat the rates fall a little, but that difference is diluted by the baseline teams and the score says the trade is a loss. dodge_hold stays 0.

What it says about the dodge: the hits come in the drive, so the levers are when it starts (dodge_horizon) and where it goes (dodge_clear), and whether to dodge at all, which the switch off (-dodge, running) answers next.

Proof: no code changed. The weights file differs from the defaults in the one line.

29. The dodge off, 19 October

Backlog 28, the second causal reading. LR 27 found the dodge last held at 43% of deaths; LR 28 that holding it longer costs. Does the dodge save tanks at all, or does it walk them into fire?

The run: CENSUS_CAPS=-dodge for the trial team, the default for the others; 48 seeds as in LR 28.

The dodge pays: -2.45 +/- 0.67 a seat with it off, outside the noise at 3.7 errors (by range: -4.11 +/- 1.10, -1.39 +/- 1.14, -1.84 +/- 1.19). It is the largest single part measured since the capped castle, and it was on all along. The 43% of deaths it precedes are where the fire is, as LR 27 suspected; without it there would be more.

Ashore and afloat apart. With the dodge off, order_with still runs and still meters (commander.rs, the order is dropped by dodges after it), so the meter counts the shells the trial team would have dodged and did not, mixed with the baseline teams' dodges. Taking each team to draw a third of the shells, the undodged rate is three times the run's rate less twice the null's:

Null (dodged)-dodge run (mixed)Undodged, estimated
Ashore, hit by the shell16.6%21.1% of 20,783about 30%
Ashore, killed7.4%8.6%about 11%
Afloat, hit by the shell7.6%7.8% of 9,109about 8%
Afloat, killed16.6%15.9%about 14.5%

The estimate leans on equal shares and should be read as a direction. (LR 30 measured it by team: the shares were not equal, and afloat the dodge cuts the kills by a fifth. The afloat reading below is withdrawn.) Ashore, the dodge nearly halves the dodged shell's hits. Afloat it does nothing: a boat is slow to turn, its dodge point is often water it cannot reach in time, and it is hit at the same rate dodging or not. What it gives up afloat is its crossing.

What this changes. The dodge stays on. The next dodge part is the either/or this table names: dodge afloat or keep sailing. A weight, dodge_afloat (1, today's), the share of a boat's dodges taken (backlog 31). The meter should count a would-be dodge apart from a taken one, per team, so the next reading needs no estimate.

Proof: no code changed.

30. The meter by team, and LR 29 corrected, 19 October

Backlog 31's first part. LR 29 estimated the undodged rates from a meter that mixed the teams, on the assumption that each team draws a third of the shells. The meter now counts by the seat's group and whether the dodge was taken.

The instrument (no change to play):

  • meter::trial(mask): the census marks the trial team's seats; the meter keeps them apart from the rest. meter::seat(p), called by run_bots for each seat, names the seat the counts after it belong to.
  • Dodge::dropped: set by the Commander from dodges before each order_with. A dodge the brain drops (the dodge off) counts as would-be.
  • Meter::dodges is [group][taken or dropped][ashore or afloat] of Dodges { begun, hit, shell, killed }; Meter::add_dodges sums it for the census total. Proof: a_dodge_counts_by_its_seat_and_whether_taken.

The reading: -dodge, seeds 0 to 15 (LR 29's first range). The 16 per-seed differences are LR 29's to the hundredth, so play did not change. In the same matches, the trial team's would-be dodges against the other two teams' taken ones:

Taken (the rest)Dropped (the trial team)
Ashore, begun4,9821,899
Ashore, hit by the shell15.5%34.0%
Ashore, killed6.6%15.0%
Afloat, begun2,1481,029
Afloat, hit by the shell6.9%10.1%
Afloat, killed14.7%18.2%

Ashore, the dodge more than halves the dodged shell's hits and the kills, as LR 29 estimated. Afloat it is not nothing: it cuts the shell's hits by a third and the kills by a fifth. LR 29's estimate was wrong because its assumption was: the trial team without the dodge drew fewer dodgeable shells (1,899 ashore against about 2,500 for each other team), being dead more often. Taking the shares as equal hid the effect afloat.

What this changes. LR 29's "afloat it changes nothing" is withdrawn. Backlog 31's hypothesis is weakened: a boat's dodge saves a fifth of the boats it is taken for. dodge_afloat can still be worth less than 1 if the crossing time it costs is worth more, but the case is now a trade for the tuner, not a free gain. Backlog 31 stays, its expected gain smaller, below 30 (the landfall).

Proof: play unchanged (the per-seed differences). make test: brain 70 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

31. The run for the beach, 19 October

Backlog 30, from LR 27: 165 of 819 deaths on landfall, 132 of them a boat sunk by direct fire. The hypothesis: landfall_order prices a beach by the threats near it and the run's heading, not the run's passage through a gun's reach, so boats sail past guns.

The part: landfall_shy (weights.rs, 0 by Paul's choice, so play is unchanged; RuleBot::landfall_shy, set by the Commander). The run to each beach is cut into tile steps; each step within a hostile tank's direct range plus 4, or a pillbox's range plus 2, is exposed. The beach's score adds 4 x landfall_shy x the exposed tiles: at 1, a tile in a gun's reach costs as much as four tiles more sailing. Proof: a_shy_boat_runs_for_the_beach_clear_of_the_guns (a near beach whose run passes a gun, and a farther one clear: at 0 the near, at 1 the far).

The reading: landfall_shy = 1 for the trial team, 48 seeds, CENSUS_ACTS on.

d a seatDeaths (seeds 0 to 15)On landfallDirect fire, sunk, on landfall
Null (LR 27)0819165132
landfall_shy 1+0.65 +/- 0.69821169133

Not confirmed. The score is inside the noise and the deaths it was aimed at did not move. (LR 32 measured the explanation below and withdrew it: the boat had seen its killer in the 10 s before in 78% of landfall deaths.) Over all 48 seeds the landfall deaths were 548 of 2,522 (21.7%), as in the null's 20.1%. Choosing a beach clear of the guns the boat sees does not save it. The cause has to be the guns it does not see: sight and direct range are both 10 tiles (the duck's note in weights.rs), so a gun the boat could choose around is seen only once the boat is already in its reach. A price on what is seen cannot help against what is not.

What this changes. landfall_shy stays 0. Backlog 30 closes, not confirmed. Backlog 32: the run for the beach priced by the guns the team has seen (the duck's remembered guns, duck_memory, or the board's sightings), so a boat knows the beach a teammate saw covered.

Proof: play unchanged at 0 by construction (the exposure returns 0 before any work, and a score plus 0.0 is the same float; no null run was repeated). make test: brain 71 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

32. Who saw the gun that sank the boat, 19 October

Backlog 32's read, from LR 31: if the boats on landfall die to guns they never saw, the guns a teammate saw would help.

The instrument (no change to play): under CENSUS_ACTS, once a second, each living trial seat's observation records the hostile tanks it sees. At each trial death whose code held last was landfall and whose killer is named, the killer is classed as seen in the 10 s before by the boat, by a teammate, by both, or by neither.

The reading (16 seeds, null run, in ranges of 6, 5 and 5; 0.00 on every seed):

Seen in the 10 s before byLandfall deathsShare
both the boat and a teammate12878.0%
neither3521.3%
a teammate only10.6%
the boat only00
All with a killer164

(LR 35: sight is shared by the whole team, so the split between the boat and a teammate below differs only by when each was sampled, and says nothing.)

(LR 34: the killer stood a median 8 tiles from the boat at its death, inside its 10-tile range, so "seen" here means seen once the gun could already fire. The conclusion that boats are not blind is withdrawn.)

LR 31's explanation is withdrawn. The boat had seen the gun that sank it in nearly four landfall deaths of five, and a teammate had seen it too: they cross together. A teammate's sighting adds a gun the boat had not seen once in 164. Backlog 32's part, pricing the guns the team has seen, has nothing to work with. The "neither" fifth is an upper bound, since sight is sampled once a second.

What, then? The boat sees the gun, prices the beaches (with or without landfall_shy), and dies anyway. It sinks at one hit and cannot fire back. A beach is chosen once; a change waits 2 s and needs 6 better (landfall_order's change). Once a gun is in sight, the boat's choice is not which beach but whether to land at all, against standing off out of the gun's range until the beach is clear or the gun moves. That is an either/or, so a weight: backlog 33.

Proof: play unchanged (0.00 on every seed). make test: brain 71 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50.

33. Standing off a covered beach, 19 October

Backlog 33, from LR 32: boats on landfall die to guns they have seen. The hypothesis: the gun covers the beach, and a boat that waits out of its reach until it leaves lives.

The part: landfall_wait (weights.rs; 0 by Paul's choice, so play is unchanged; RuleBot::landfall_wait, set by the Commander). In landfall_order, once the beach is chosen: if a seen hostile tank is within its direct range plus 4 of that beach, the boat moves to 16 tiles from the gun, on the line from the gun through the boat, or holds where it is if already that far. It does so until landfall_wait seconds of the crossing are spent, counted from the first stand-off and not restarted (a gun flickering in and out of sight cannot hold a boat off for ever), then lands. The wait resets ashore, at a respawn, and under a hop. RuleBot::standing_off and a new code, stand_off (status.rs), let the census count it. Proof: a_boat_stands_off_a_covered_beach_for_its_wait (at 0 the boat lands; at 10 it backs off to 16 tiles and lands once the 10 s are spent).

The reading: landfall_wait = 10 for the trial team, 48 seeds, CENSUS_ACTS on.

d a seatDeaths (seeds 0 to 15)On landfallStand-off time
Null (LR 27)0819165
landfall_wait 10-0.51 +/- 0.63798184under 0.07%

Not confirmed. The score is inside the noise, and the landfall deaths did not fall (573 of 2,460 over 48 seeds, 23.3%). The stand-off hardly ever fired: under 0.07% of the trial team's seat-seconds (below the 40 commonest codes in every range), and it was the code held last at about 13 deaths. A gun the boat sees is rarely within reach of the beach it chose. The guns that sink boats on landfall are not at the landing. They are near the boat somewhere along its run.

Three parts on the landfall (LR 31 to 33) have now found nothing to price. Each assumed where the gun is. The next step reads it: at each landfall death, the killer's distance to the chosen beach and to the boat, and whether the killer is ashore (backlog 34).

landfall_wait stays 0.

Proof: play unchanged at 0 by construction (landfall_wait 0 skips the block). make test: brain 72 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

34. Where the gun that sinks the boat stands, 19 October

Backlog 34, from LR 31 to 33: three landfall parts each assumed where the gun is, and none moved the deaths.

The instrument (no change to play): Brain::beach, the beach a boat is running for (RuleBot::landfall; the Commander's inner RuleBot's). Under CENSUS_ACTS, at each trial death on landfall with a killer, the census records the killer's distance to the boat and to that beach, the boat's distance to its beach, and whether the killer was afloat.

The reading (16 seeds, null run, in ranges of 6, 5 and 5; 0.00 on every seed; 164 landfall deaths with a killer, every one with a beach chosen):

Range 0 to 5Range 6 to 10Range 11 to 15All
Killer to boat, median (quartiles)8.1 (7.4, 8.7)8.0 (7.5, 9.6)8.0 (7.0, 9.4)about 8
Killer to beach, median (quartiles)28.6 (4.1, 33.6)28.6 (5.0, 36.8)31.8 (6.8, 34.9)about 29
Boat to beach, median (quartiles)24.1 (7.3, 28.4)18.3 (6.9, 32.8)17.6 (8.1, 30.7)about 20
Killer within its range + 4 of the beach34.0%43.3%34.1%62 of 164, 37.8%
Killer nearer the boat than the beach60.4%53.7%68.2%98 of 164, 59.8%
Killer afloat9.4%11.9%4.5%15 of 164, 9.1%

Three things follow:

  1. The gun is seen when it can already fire. The killer stood a median 8 tiles from the boat, inside its 10-tile direct range, and sight is also 10 tiles. LR 32's "the boat had seen its killer in the 10 s before" is true, but the sighting came at or inside the gun's range, too late to act on. LR 32's reading stands; its conclusion that the boats are not blind is withdrawn in this sense: they are blind until it is too late.
  2. Two kinds of death. About two in five killers stood at the beach (within 14 tiles of it, the first quartile 4 to 7 tiles), a beach chosen a median 20 tiles off and so out of sight. That is why the stand-off (LR 33) found nothing to see. About three in five stood along the run, nearer the boat than its beach: guns on the coast the boat sailed past. Under one in ten were afloat.
  3. The limit is knowledge, not choice. A boat's own sight cannot price either kind in time, and teammates crossing together see the same. What the team does not use is what it knows of the coast: the team board's hostile influence (board.rs, built under board, read by nothing since LR 24). A beach and a run priced by that field could see a held coast before the boat is in range.

What this changes. Backlog 34 closes: the gun stands either at the beach or along the coast of the run, out of sight until in range. Backlog 35: price the landfall by the team board's hostile influence, at the beach and along the run (needs board). The parts of LR 31 and 33 (landfall_shy, landfall_wait) stay at 0; either can be fed the board's knowledge instead of the boat's sight.

Proof: play unchanged (0.00 on every seed). make test: brain 72 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

35. Known earlier, and lost, 19 October

Backlog 35's read, from LR 34: a boat sees its killer only once it is in range, so could the team board's hostile influence price the coast before then?

What the code says first. Two facts, read before measuring:

  • Sight is shared by the whole team (World::visible_to: any allied tank or drone within its vision). A seat's observation is the team's. LR 32's split between "the boat" and "a teammate" could only differ by when each was sampled; that split is vacuous and is marked so.
  • The board keeps no memory (TeamBoard::rebuild clears its influence and threat each second and fills them from what the team sees now). It cannot know a gun the team does not see at that moment, which is the case LR 34 found. Backlog 35, as written, cannot work.

The question that remains: had the team seen the killer earlier, and lost it?

The instrument (no change to play): under CENSUS_ACTS, each hostile tank in the trial team's sight is logged with its position once a second. At each landfall death with a killer, the killer is classed: seen 10 to 60 s before, then within its direct range plus 4 of where the boat died; seen 10 to 60 s before, elsewhere; or not seen then.

The reading (16 seeds, null run; 0.00 on every seed):

The killerDeathsShare
seen 10 to 60 s before, within reach of where the boat died7646.3%
seen 10 to 60 s before, elsewhere2615.9%
not seen 10 to 60 s before6237.8%
All164

Nearly half the landfall deaths were known earlier. The team had seen the gun standing near the place the boat would die, 10 s to a minute before, then lost sight of it (it moved behind the sight line, or the team's eyes moved on), and the boat sailed into it. Nothing in the brain keeps a hostile tank's last position for long. The duck keeps a seen gun for duck_memory (5 s) and only for ducking; the board for 1 s.

What this changes. Backlog 35 closes: the board forgets. Backlog 36: the landfall's guns (its danger, toward, and the landfall_shy and landfall_wait checks) include each hostile tank where it was last seen, for landfall_memory seconds (a weight, Paul's default to ask; 0 is today's). It reaches up to the 46% of landfall deaths whose killer was seen near there earlier.

Proof: play unchanged (0.00 on every seed). make test: brain 72 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

36. Guns remembered for the landfall, 19 October

Backlog 36, from LR 35: in 46% of landfall deaths the team had seen the killer near there 10 to 60 s before, and lost it.

The part: landfall_memory (weights.rs; 0 by Paul's choice, so play is unchanged; RuleBot::landfall_memory, set by the Commander). RuleBot::remember_guns, each tick in think_map, keeps each hostile tank the team sees where it is, and a tank out of sight where it was last seen, until the memory's seconds are up, the tank is seen again, or its spot is in a teammate's sight (within 0.9 of TANK_VISION) with no tank there. RuleBot::known_guns, the seen and the remembered, is what landfall_order prices: the beach's danger, the run's heading (toward), its exposure (landfall_shy) and the stand-off's cover (landfall_wait). At 0 the list is the seen tanks in the same order as before. Proof: a_gun_seen_and_lost_still_prices_the_run (a gun beside the near beach's run, seen and then lost: at memory 0 the boat takes the near beach, at 30 the far one, and the near one again after 31 s).

The reading: the three landfall weights on together for the trial team (landfall_memory 30, landfall_shy 1, landfall_wait 10), 48 seeds, CENSUS_ACTS on.

Null (LR 27, seeds 0 to 15)All three (seeds 0 to 15)All three (48 seeds)
d a seat0+1.84 +/- 1.13+2.31 +/- 0.58
Deaths8197472,298
On landfall (code held last)16582281
Killer seen near there 10 to 60 s before761659
Dead share of seat-seconds46.5%42.2%
Stand-off share of seat-secondsunder 0.07% (LR 33)0.17%0.16%

By range: +1.84 +/- 1.13, +3.07 +/- 0.91, +2.00 +/- 0.97.

Confirmed. The trial team scores 2.31 a seat more, four standard errors out, and every range is positive. Landfall deaths halve (165 to 82 on the same seeds), and the deaths the memory was aimed at, a gun seen near there earlier, fall from 76 to 16. The stand-off now acts, because remembered guns give it a cover to see. Of the earlier landfall parts, landfall_shy (LR 31) and landfall_wait (LR 33) read nothing useful on their own; with memory they have something to price.

What is not yet known: which of the three carries the gain. Memory alone also feeds the beach's danger and the run's heading, which were already on. Backlog 37 takes each away in turn. The defaults are Paul's: all three stay 0 until he moves them.

Proof: play unchanged at 0 (known_guns is the seen tanks in order; remember_guns keeps nothing). make test: brain 73 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings.

37. Which landfall weight carries the gain, 19 October

Backlog 37, from LR 36: landfall_memory 30, landfall_shy 1 and landfall_wait 10 together read +2.31 +/- 0.58. Each is taken back to 0 in turn, 48 seeds each, against the null run (the defaults for all), CENSUS_ACTS on.

Memory off (landfall_shy 1, landfall_wait 10, landfall_memory 0):

d a seat (48 seeds)Landfall deaths, seeds 0 to 15Killer seen near there earlier
Null (LR 27, 35)016576
All three (LR 36)+2.31 +/- 0.588216
Memory off-1.08 +/- 0.5316472

By range: -0.82 +/- 0.86, -0.71 +/- 1.06, -1.70 +/- 0.82.

The memory carries the gain. Without it, the two weights over the guns a boat sees cost about a point, just outside two errors, and the landfall deaths are the null's. That matches LR 31 and 33, each alone, with the same sign. The difference between this run and LR 36, about 3.4 a seat, is what remembering the guns is worth to the other two.

Shy off and wait off (each with the memory on):

All three carry part of it, and the memory is the base. Without the memory the other two cost a point. With it, the stand-off is worth about 1.6 a seat (all three against wait off) and the run's exposure about 0.8 (all three against shy off). Each of those differences is read across two runs against the null, not paired with each other, so they are rougher than the table's errors. Shy off alone is outside two errors (+1.50 +/- 0.67, 2.2 errors); wait off is not (1.1). The landfall deaths order the runs the same way as the score: all three fewest, then shy off, wait off, and memory off with the null.

The best measured setting is all three at once. Its numbers (30 s, 1, 10 s) were picked by hand and are not tuned. Moving any default is Paul's choice.

38. The landfall's three on by default, and the reverse reading, 19 October

Paul, after LR 37: "yes all three on". landfall_memory 30, landfall_shy 1 and landfall_wait 10 are the defaults (weights.rs). A Commander takes them from its Settings; a plain RuleBot (the lone aggro bot, and any RuleBot seat) keeps 0, as it keeps the dodge's constants. Every test passes with the new defaults.

The reverse reading. As proof of the switch, the census was run the other way round: the old defaults (all three 0) as the trial team, against two teams on the new, 48 seeds.

TrialBaselinesd a seat (48 seeds)
LR 36 (forward)all three onthe old defaults+2.31 +/- 0.58
Reversethe old defaultsall three on-0.08 +/- 0.45

By range, reverse: -0.35 +/- 0.82, -0.77 +/- 0.62, +0.86 +/- 0.86.

The two do not mirror. A team with the memory beats teams without it by 2.3 a seat; a team without it among teams with it loses nothing that 48 seeds can see. The gain is real against opponents that play the old way and not shown against opponents that play the new. One reading, untested: the stand-off and the run's price change where boats land and which coasts are contested for every team. A memory team's edge then depends on its opponents landing where the guns are, and a no-memory team among memory teams finds the coasts less contested. What the switch does to play when every team has it is measured by neither census: the census reads a team against others, not a team against itself.

The defaults stay as Paul chose. Backlog 38: what all three do when every team has them. That needs a fixed reference the census does not have: a team of the old defaults kept for comparison, or a score that is not relative (survival time, landfall deaths a seat, matches finished).

Proof: make test: brain 73 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings. 360 functions, 121 undocumented.

39. The landfall's three when every team has them, 19 October

Backlog 38, from LR 38: the relative census reads +2.31 forward and -0.08 in reverse. What do the three do to the game when every team has them?

The method. The census sets only the trial team's weights, but a null run is every team on the defaults. LR 27's null run (seeds 0 to 15) was every team on the old defaults, and play at the old defaults has not changed since (each new weight at 0 adds nothing). A null run now, on the same seeds, is every team on the new. Both with CENSUS_ACTS. These are absolute readings, not paired by seed, so they carry no standard error.

Seeds 0 to 15, every teamOld defaults (LR 27)New defaults
Trial team's deaths819848
On landfall (code held last)16589
In a dodge (code held last)354434
Seat-seconds (the matches' length)206,685216,000 (+4.5%)
Deaths per 1,000 seat-seconds3.963.93
Dead share46.5%41.9%
Living seat-seconds110,615125,471 (+13%)
Mean score a seat8.387.95

The deaths move; they do not fall. With every team on the new defaults, the boats lost on the run for the beach nearly halve (165 to 89), but the deaths in a dodge rise by about as many (354 to 434), and the death rate is the same. The tanks that used to sink on the way in now land, and die ashore under indirect fire. The matches run 4.5% longer and the team spends 13% more time alive, which is what goal 5 asks for. The mean score a seat falls (8.38 to 7.95). It is a weighted sum that counts kills and holdings, and it is relative to how the other teams play, so without per-seed pairing this change cannot be told from noise.

This explains LR 38's asymmetry. A team with the memory, among teams without it, saves its boats while its opponents' boats still sink: it gains. A team without it, among teams with it, still loses boats, but its opponents' saved tanks die ashore instead, so the relative score evens out.

What this changes. The defaults stay as Paul chose: the game is longer and as deadly, with the deaths moved off the water. The next lever is where the deaths moved to: the dodge ashore, now 51% of the deaths held last. LR 29 showed the dodge pays; its numbers (dodge_horizon, dodge_clear) have never been tuned (backlog 28's close), and the tuner (backlog 23) is the way to tune them.

Proof: no code changed. make test as in LR 38.

40. SPSA on the paired census, 19 October

Backlog 23, from CI-001 VI: the (1 + lambda) tuner selects noise. The recommended replacement is SPSA (Spall 1992), because the fair census already measures a paired difference.

The parts:

  • CENSUS_BASE_WEIGHTS (fleet.rs brain_census): a weights file for the two baseline teams, as CENSUS_WEIGHTS is for the trial. The header line prints both sides' changed weights. With it, one census plays theta + c delta against theta - c delta, and every team on some old defaults against the new is one run (LR 39 needed two).
  • scripts/spsa.sh (make spsa): SPSA in log space over the weights named in TUNE, each positive. Each step k draws a sign delta_i for every tuned weight and plays one fair census on SEEDS fresh seeds (SEED0 + (k - 1) SEEDS), the trial team on theta_i exp(+c_k delta_i), the baselines on theta_i exp(-c_k delta_i). The census's difference a seat d is f(+) - f(-); each log theta_i moves by a_k d / (2 c_k delta_i), capped at MAXSTEP (0.5). The gains follow Spall: a_k = A / (k + 1)^0.602, c_k = C / k^0.101 (A 0.6, C 0.4). SET plants values on the start; steps.tsv keeps every step with its difference and standard error. A result is promoted only after beating the defaults on 48 seeds the search did not use.

The planted test (CI-001 VI.D): a weight with a known large effect, detuned, which a working tuner must move back. w_wounded, named in CI-001, has never been measured, so a failure there would say nothing. landfall_memory has: removing it cost about 3.4 a seat (LR 37). It is set to 5 s (default 30) and tuned alone: 8 steps of 12 seeds. The first four steps, as committed with LR 40 (the whole run in LR 41):

kd a seatIts errorlandfall_memory after
1-0.131.804.69
2-0.871.243.27
3+0.551.333.99
4+1.211.432.68

Every step's difference is inside its error: at 12 seeds a step reads to about 1.4 a seat, and between memory of 2 s and 5 s the true difference is likely far smaller. The walk so far is noise. LR 41 gives the whole run.

Proof: make test: brain 73 passed and 8 ignored. A plumbing run (1 step, 3 seeds of 120 s) wrote both sides' files, and the header shows the trial on 4.48 s and the baselines on 2.01 s.

41. The planted test, first run: a flat slope, 19 October

LR 40's planted test, whole: landfall_memory from 5 s, tuned alone, 8 steps of 12 fresh seeds (1,000 to 1,095), A 0.6, C 0.4.

kd a seatIts errorlandfall_memory after
1-0.131.804.69
2-0.871.243.27
3+0.551.333.99
4+1.211.432.68
5-0.590.883.21
6-0.841.094.05
7+0.361.574.45
8-0.771.143.69

Not passed. The weight ended at 3.69 s, below where it started, after a walk in which no step's difference was outside its error. The mean of the eight differences, each signed by its draw, is no different from zero.

Why, most likely: the plant sat on a flat. With C 0.4 the two sides played memories of about 3.4 and 7.5 s around 5 s. The deaths the memory prevents were sightings 10 to 60 s old (LR 35). Below about 10 s, memory barely acts, so the slope there is near zero and each step reads only noise. SPSA was not shown to fail; the test did not give it a slope. A planted test has to sit where the effect is.

What this changes. The test runs again with C 1.0: the two sides then play about 13.6 s and 1.8 s, so every step straddles the range where memory acts. If SPSA climbs from there, the tool works when the slope is visible. If it does not, 12 seeds a step is too few for this effect, and the next try is 24.

Proof: no code changed.

42. The planted test, second run: it climbs, slowly, 19 October

LR 41's planted test again with C 1.0, so each step's two sides straddle the range where memory acts. landfall_memory from 5 s, tuned alone, 8 steps of 12 fresh seeds (the same seeds, 1,000 to 1,095), A 0.6.

Partly passed. The weight climbed from 5 to 11.8 s, toward the known better value. It is slow: after 96 seeds it is less than halfway to 30 s in log terms (ln 11.8 - ln 5 = 0.86 of 1.79). With Spall's decaying gains it would take about as many steps again.

What it says for the dodge. The planted effect was about 3.4 a seat between 0 and 30 s (LR 37), the largest part measured, and SPSA at 12 seeds a step only crawls toward it. The dodge's numbers act on something smaller. Tuning them is a long run: 24 seeds a step (errors about 0.8), A 1.0, and 16 or more steps, about 2.5 hours. The result then has to beat the defaults on 48 seeds the search did not use.

Proof: no code changed.

Step 9 is the first outside its error (2.7 errors): a horizon of about 1 s, with the narrower margin and the wider clearance, lost by 2.5 a seat to one of about 2.3 s. The early noisy steps had walked the horizon down to where a tank starts too late to get out from under a shell, and SPSA walked it back. Before step 9, no step was outside its error. The gain is too high for this noise: at A 1.0, readings inside their errors move every weight by up to the cap (steps 5 and 6 hit it). dodge_clear is the most consistent mover. Signed toward the side with the larger clearance, the first seven readings average +0.49 +/- 0.28 a seat (1.7 errors); with step 8, which read the smaller clearance better, +0.35 +/- 0.28 (1.3 errors), though each also carries the other two weights' perturbations. The run goes on; if it ends far from the defaults on noise-sized readings, it runs again at A 0.3, and any end point is confirmed against the defaults on 48 unused seeds before it can be a default.

Two steps of sixteen were outside their errors, 9 and 11. In both, the side with the larger margin won (1.26 over 0.57; 1.92 over 0.88). On the horizon they disagree (step 9: about 1 s lost to 2.3 s; step 11: 3.6 s lost to 1.6 s), and step 13 (inside its error) had about 1 s lose again. The end point: dodge_horizon 2.05 s, dodge_margin 1.52, dodge_clear 3.73 tiles, against the defaults of 4, 0.8 and 2.5. Start later, count more near misses, and clear further.

The confirmation: the end point as the trial team's weights, the defaults for the baselines, the 48 standard seeds (0 to 47, which the search, on 2,000 to 2,383, never saw), CENSUS_ACTS on.

Promising, not confirmed. +1.24 is 1.65 errors from zero, inside the two-error rule, though all three ranges are positive. The meter agrees in direction: with the end point a dodging tank is caught by its own shell more often (it starts later), but hit by anything less often and killed less often (6.4% against 7.4%). Starting a dodge 4 s out takes a tank off its errand and into other fire; starting at 2 s keeps it where it was until the shell is close.

The defaults stay. The decision rule, set before the next reading: a second fixed batch of 48 seeds (48 to 95); the end point is promoted if, and only if, the 96 seeds pooled read past two errors. No further seeds are added after that.

On the tuner. SPSA moved three noisy weights to a point that reads +1.24 on unseen seeds, from readings of which only two were outside their errors. With A 1.0 the walk was rough, and steps 5, 6 and 11 hit the cap. The next run should use A 0.3 and, for three weights, 32 seeds a step.

The second batch (seeds 48 to 95, the rule above set before it ran):

Not promoted. By the rule set before it ran, the pooled 96 seeds had to pass two errors, and they read 1.74. The defaults stay (4, 0.8, 2.5), and this end point gets no more seeds. If the dodge's numbers are worth something, it is under a point a seat, smaller than the census resolves at 96 seeds. A new search, at A 0.3 and 32 seeds a step, would start a new hypothesis with its own confirmation. Backlog 23 closes: the tool is built, it found a lead, and the lead did not hold.

That is every capability off by default, alone, against today's defaults (the landfall's three on, LR 38), 12 seeds of 900 s on naval.map, RESPAWNS_UNLIMITED (255; the roadmap's 254 was a slip). They are leads, to be confirmed on 48 fresh seeds before anything changes (backlog 39).

Backlog 29 answered. The late parts do not read differently given time: the base cycle +0.02 and -0.60, the escort +0.02 and -0.34, all inside the noise. The hypothesis that the census hid them by attrition is not confirmed. With today's landfall defaults the dead share is already lower (LR 39), and unlimited respawns did not uncover them either.

45. A frozen census binary, 20 October

Backlog 41, from Paul: "is there an improved and faster way for you to test ?" Every census ran cargo test, which rebuilds the brain if the source has changed. So no brain code could be edited while a census ran, and every long run was idle time for the work.

The part: census_bin DIR (scripts/common.sh) builds the brain's tests once, finds the test binary in cargo's JSON output, copies it to DIR/census-bin, and prints the path. switches.sh, spsa.sh and tune.sh run the copy ("$BIN" --ignored brain_census --nocapture). A run then plays the code as it was when it began. The copy sits with the run's logs, so the binary behind any result is kept beside it.

The proof: a 1-step SPSA plumbing run (SET="landfall_memory=3", 3 seeds of 120 s) started, and 20 s in, a line that is not Rust was appended to fleet.rs. The run finished. Its reading, d -0.4567 +/- 0.4054 with memory moved to 2.3940, is identical to the first plumbing run of LR 40, which drew the same sign on the same seeds and code. fleet.rs was then restored from a copy; git diff shows the tree clean.

The other ways to test faster (LR 44's companion, CI-020 V.3) stay on the list: metrics of the part's own counts, scenario files (backlog 24), more cores, a cheaper match (finding L2), sequential rules set in advance.

46. smooth as a weight, 20 October

Backlog 40, from Paul on LR 44's table: "smooth sounds like we should remove it.", then "ok. maybe not remove it. give it an insignificant coefficient". smooth was one switch over six behaviours, and it read negative in all four readings on record.

The part: the capability smooth is gone (its bit, 9, retired), and a weight, w_smooth, 0 to 1, default 0.05, takes its place. Read through Settings::smooth (clamped) and Settings::smooth_on (above 0.5).

BehaviourWhereHow w_smooth acts
The move's desire vectorCommander::gothe share of the new pull taken blends from 1 (at 0) to alpha (at 1): smooth_alpha; 0.97 at the default
The retreat thresholdsCommander::needs_homeblend from the constants (4 shells, 35 armour) to retreat_shells and retreat_armor; equal today, so no change until those are tuned
The rung's confirmationCommander::autoabove 0.5 (a count)
The turret's confirmationTactician.confirmabove 0.5 (a count)
The stance's advance scalarTactician.smoothabove 0.5: at any share above 0 it is a different rule (press above the band's middle), not a softer one, so it is yes or no
Resupply by the doctrine aloneRuleBot.own_resupplyabove 0.5 (yes or no)

A file or census that names smooth as a capability reads it as w_smooth 1, and -smooth as 0, so old weights files and make switches still work. Proof: smooth_is_a_weight_that_blends_and_thresholds (0.05 near off, the alpha blend in a line, the threshold strictly above 0.5, clamped at 1); a_weights_file_round_trips_and_names_its_mistakes (the old caps = ..., smooth reads as the weight).

The reading: today's play before the change was smooth off, w_smooth 0. As the trial team, against two teams on the new default 0.05, 48 seeds, from a frozen binary (LR 45):

TrialBaselinesd a seat (48 seeds)By range
w_smooth 0 (the old play)0.05 (the default)+0.47 +/- 0.60-0.96 +/- 1.21, +0.46 +/- 0.89, +1.91 +/- 0.92

Insignificant, as asked. At 0.05 only the desire vector acts, a move taking 97% of each new pull rather than all of it, and the census cannot tell it from the old play. The behaviours stay in the brain, reachable by one number, for the tuner or a slider.

Proof: make test: brain 74 passed and 8 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings. 363 functions, 121 undocumented.

47. The two leads on fresh seeds, 20 October

Backlog 39, from LR 44: bridge_jobs (+1.68 +/- 0.70) and duck (-2.26 +/- 1.05) were each outside two errors in one pass of 24 tests. The rule, set before the run: each is confirmed only past two errors, in the same direction, on 48 fresh seeds (96 to 143) alone. Each was run as its trial team against today's defaults (bridge_jobs with board on for every team), from a frozen binary.

LeadLR 44 (one pass)Seeds 96 to 143Verdict
bridge_jobs helps+1.68 +/- 0.70+0.07 +/- 0.41not confirmed
duck hurts-2.26 +/- 1.05+1.25 +/- 0.52not confirmed: past two errors the other way

Neither lead holds. bridge_jobs reads nothing on fresh seeds: its LR 44 reading was the chance finding the table predicted. duck reads past two errors with the opposite sign. Its readings on record: -0.51 (LR 21), +0.39 (LR 24), -0.51 and -2.26 (LR 44), +1.25 here. A part whose sign changes with the seeds has an effect smaller than the census resolves at 12 seeds, and possibly one that depends on the map's starts. "duck helps" is a new hypothesis, set by this reading, and needs its own fresh seeds before duck could be turned on (backlog 42). Both stay off.

The lesson repeats LR 43's: a reading from a table of tests, or a tuner's best point, regresses on fresh seeds. Of four leads confirmed this way since LR 36, one held (the landfall's memory, consistent in every run), and three did not.

48. Scenario files, 20 October

Backlog 24, from Paul (18 October): "is there a way to create description and try to attempt these via playtests faster", and his question of 20 October, "is there an improved and faster way for you to test ?". A census answers "does it win?" in 25 minutes. A single behaviour, such as "does the boat steer clear?", should be answerable in seconds, written as text rather than as a Rust test.

The part: scenario.rs, and scenarios/*.toml.

  • The form, a part of TOML read in the brain (no new crate): key = value, numbers, "strings", true and false, [lists], { inline = tables }, [expect], and [[brain]], [[pillbox]], [[depot]], [[shell]], [[event]]. Positions are anchors with offsets: "seat:0", "depot:0", "pillbox:0".
  • The setup: players, seconds, pillboxes = false. A [[brain]] is a rulebot or a commander with a goal (hunt_pillboxes, defend_depot:0, auto, ...); a seat without one sits still, as in the Rust tests. A [[pillbox]] is placed as one_pillbox places it; a [[depot]] is the nearest, given an owner; a [[shell]] lands on an anchor after lands_in seconds; an [[event]] moves a seat's tank at a second.
  • The checks ([expect]): hits_on, hits, moved, shells_spent, pillbox_owned_by (with by), pillbox_damage, pillbox_alive. A run reports every failed check in words, not the first.
  • The runners: every_scenario_holds runs every file under scenarios/ in cargo test; make scenario S=file runs one and prints each thinking seat's status code every 5 s, and when the match ends (the world stops then, as in the Rust tests; the trace now says so).

The proof (backlog 24: "three of today's tests rewritten as scenarios pass and fail as they do"). Three tests rewritten: a_bot_drives_out_from_under_a_shell (lib.rs), a_commander_takes_a_neutral_pillbox_without_a_shot and a_commander_defends_its_depot (commander.rs). Each Rust test and its scenario was run unchanged, then under two deliberate breaks of the code, the files restored by a trap on every path:

Rust testScenario
Unmutated: the threepasspass
A, the dodge never acts: shellFAILFAIL
B, the Commander never moves: neutral pillboxFAILFAIL
B, the Commander never moves: depotpasspass

They agree in every case. (Under B the defender still hits an intruder placed 5 tiles from its depot without moving, in both.) The three run in 0.02 s together: two tanks a world, so a step costs microseconds.

What this changes. A new behaviour can be specified as a scenario before it is built, run in a second, and kept as a test, and the census kept for whether it wins. The Rust tests stay for now; whether to retire the ported three is Paul's call. Next for the form: a [[gun]] or [[tank]] placement for the landfall's situations, and a census-sized mode, a scenario over many seeds with its rate and error.

Proof: make test: brain 76 passed and 9 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings. 18 files, 374 functions, 121 undocumented.

49. The landfall as a scenario, 20 October

LR 48's next step: scenario placements for the landfall, so its situations can be tested in seconds rather than by census.

The form grows (scenario.rs):

  • teams = [0, 1, 0]: a team for each seat.
  • [[terrain]]: a rectangle of tiles around an anchor, x and y in tiles from it, set to water or grass. A sea and its coasts are carved this way.
  • [[tank]]: a seat's tank put at an anchor and offset at the start, afloat with boat = true.
  • "centre:0": the map's centre, as an anchor.
  • [[brain]] weights = { name = value }: a Commander's Settings with those changed; on a RuleBot, which reads no Settings, the landfall's three, its only weights held as fields.
  • [[event]] gains boat = true (put to sea) and drones = "recall".
  • [expect] gains ends_near (where a seat finishes) and lands_near (where it first comes ashore).
  • The outcome keeps every order the world refused a thinking seat, and the trace shows a RuleBot's status text beside its code.

The scenario, a-boat-remembers-the-gun-on-the-near-beach.toml: a RuleBot ashore beside a hostile tank sees it; at 1 s it is put to sea between a near coast (12 tiles east, by the gun, now out of sight) and a far one (22 west); its drones are recalled. With the landfall's defaults it must come ashore within 10 tiles of the far coast.

landfall_memoryCame ashore atVerdict
30 (the default)(42.8, 64.5), the far coastholds
0(76.2, 56.3), the near coast by the gunfails: 35.1 tiles from the point

Three false starts, each a finding. The first two versions held with and without memory, so they tested nothing:

  1. ~~A Commander on Auto that starts at sea never prices a beach.~~ Withdrawn (LR 50): it does. The trace sampled every 5 s, and the boat had landed before the first sample, so every sample read the rung it took ashore. What was true: a Commander built by Commander::new ran the landfall's weights at 0 while its Settings said the defaults (LR 50).
  2. The first choice binds. With the gun seen at t = 0 through a teammate, the boat chose the far coast at once. The landfall changes beach only after 2 s and for one 6 tiles better, and by then the distances had closed. Memory can only matter when the gun is seen and lost before the boat chooses.
  3. A drone sees for its tank. A tank moved to sea by hand left its recon drone ashore, still seeing the gun, so even at memory 0 the boat knew it. Recalling the drones made the test honest.

One oddity is kept, not chased: at memory 0 the boat first headed west and later turned east. The landing separates the two cases cleanly.

What this changes. The landfall's memory now has a test that runs in a second and fails when the memory is off, beside its census result (LR 36). The three false starts are why a scenario must be run broken before it is trusted, and the first of them (withdrawn in LR 50) is why a trace must sample faster than the thing it watches.

Proof: make test: brain 76 passed and 9 ignored. No warnings.

50. The circle at the edge of sight, 20 October

Backlog 43, from LR 49: "a Commander on Auto at sea never prices a beach". The scenario runner gained trace_every (seconds between trace lines) and each seat's position in the trace. At one line a second the claim fell at once: the Commander's afloat branch runs before the ladder, and the boat had landed in under 5 s, before the first 5 s sample. LR 49's finding 1 and backlog 43 are withdrawn.

What the fine trace found instead.

  1. Commander::new held the default Settings but did not apply them. Its inner RuleBot kept the landfall's weights at 0 while settings said the defaults. Dealt bots were not affected: equip (fleet.rs) and the window's tuning page (tune.rs) call set_settings. Tests, scenarios and any Commander built directly were. Fixed: Commander::new ends with set_settings(Settings::default()). The brain's tests all pass. A census re-run of LR 46's first range checks dealt play is unchanged.
  2. Without memory, a boat circles at the edge of sight. A Commander on Auto at sea between a coast 12 tiles east, by a hostile tank it saw and lost, and one 22 tiles west, traced every second at landfall_memory 0:
sBoat atMaking for
1.9(68.5, 63.9)the near beach (76, 64)
3.0(74.2, 64.3)(76, 64)
4.0(73.8, 67.1)the far beach (42, 64): the gun seen at about 8 tiles
5.0(68.1, 66.7)(42, 64)
6.0(68.6, 63.9)(76, 66): the gun out of sight, forgotten
8.0(72.7, 68.5)(42, 66)
......the same, every 4 s, drifting south

It sails toward the near beach, sees the gun, turns away, loses it at 10 tiles, forgets it, and turns back. It is ashore at 40 s, beside the gun. With the default memory the same boat keeps the gun in mind, sails straight for the far coast, and is ashore at 6 s. This is the mechanism behind LR 36's gain and LR 35's finding that the killer had been seen near there earlier: without memory, boats circled where the guns could reach them.

The test: a-commander-boat-does-not-circle.toml, the Commander form of LR 49's scenario. It holds at the defaults, and with landfall_memory = 0 it fails ("came ashore 35.8 tiles from the point").

The check of dealt play (LR 46's seeds 0 to 15, the same weights, the new binary) is LR 51.

Proof: make test: brain 76 passed and 9 ignored. No warnings.
