52. Scenarios over many seeds, and a dodge into water, 20 October

The mode. scenario::many runs a scenario over many seeds: the world's seed (Scenario::seed, so the map outside what the file carves) and every brain's (its seed xor the run's). It reports how often it held and the binomial error, sqrt(p (1 - p) / n). SCENARIO_SET="name=value" sets a weight on every brain (set_weights), so two settings run on the same seeds. make scenario S=file RUNS=100 SET=...; SEED=n traces one seed. Proof: a_weight_is_set_on_every_brain. Brains in a scenario now think with the map (think_on), as run_bots gives it in a match. That changed no result.

Every scenario over 100 seeds (each takes 0.2 to 2.5 s):

ScenarioHeld
a boat remembers the gun on the near beach100
a bot drives out from under a shell97 (before the fix below)
a commander's boat does not circle100; with landfall_memory 0, 0
a commander defends its depot99
a commander takes a neutral pillbox100

The memory's curve, the circling scenario at each memory, 100 seeds: 0 s holds 0 times; 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 15, 20, 30 and 60 s each hold 100 times. In this geometry it is a step, not a slope: any memory longer than the landfall's two-second commitment breaks the circle. That is also why LR 41's planted test found nothing to climb between 3 and 7 s. The census's view (LR 35: killers seen 10 to 60 s before) still argues for a long memory in matches, where guns move and boats cross far.

The dodge into water. On 3 of 100 seeds the shell scenario failed. SEED=8 traced at a quarter second: the dodge's point, across the shell's line on the near side, was water. The world refused the move ("refused: water"), the shell was already marked dodged, and the tank sat under it for 3 s. Dodge::order_on (lib.rs) now takes the map, which both callers hold. It tries the near side, the far side, back along the line, and forward, and takes the first point the tank can reach: passable ground ashore; afloat, water or a beach (a boat that runs ashore lands). Without a map it acts as before (order_with). The shell scenario then held on 1,000 of 1,000 seeds.

Its effect in matches. A change to a reflex every team has cannot be paired against itself in the census, so the same 48 seeds were run as a null before and after, from two frozen binaries (the tree without the fix by git stash, then with it), CENSUS_ACTS on. The first version required water afloat; it made boats worse, so it was corrected to water or a beach.

Same 48 seeds, every teamBeforeWater only afloatWater or a beach afloat
Deaths (the trial seat's team)2,4722,3902,297 (-7.1%)
Dead share of seat-time42.5%40.9%40.6%
Ashore dodges: hit / by the dodged shell / killed32.3% / 15.7% / 6.5%24.0% / 10.6% / 4.1%25.5% / 10.4% / 5.1%
Afloat dodges: hit / by the dodged shell / killed16.3% / 7.1% / 14.8%17.4% / 7.9% / 16.6%15.4% / 6.6% / 14.0%

Ashore, a third fewer dodges are caught by their own shell. Afloat, the beach restores and slightly betters the old rate. 175 fewer deaths on the same seeds. These are absolute counts from two unpaired runs: no error is given, and the mean score a seat (8.70, 8.81) is relative to how every team plays, so it says nothing here.

What this changes. The census needed an hour to show the memory's gain; a scenario over 100 seeds shows its mechanism in under two seconds, and its first use found a bug that had been in the dodge since it was written. A fix to a reflex every team shares is read by before-and-after nulls on the same seeds, with absolute counts.

Proof: make test: brain 77 passed and 10 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings. 378 functions, 121 undocumented.

53. The dodge planned by the tank's own motion, and a hold that lets go, 20 October

LR 52 left ashore dodges caught by their own shell at 10.4% after the water fix. The dodge picks its point from the shell's line alone: 5 tiles from the impact, across the line, on the side the tank is on. It reads neither the tank's heading nor its speed. Since the scatter is drawn at launch (treadcore-sim impact: *at + scatter), the impact a bot reads is exactly where the shell lands, and the line it came in on says nothing once it is fired.

The instrument. Two scenario families, each one file that draws its situation from the run's seed (scenario.rs): [[tank]] gains speed, heading (degrees, or "any") and a goal drive tiles on, so it starts rolling; [[shell]] gains offset, scatter (up to that many tiles off, by the dice), from_any (the direction by the dice) and lands_in = [lo, hi]. A Commander on Auto, a shell from 30 tiles landing within 2.5 tiles of its tank in 1.5 to 3.5 s, rolling at full speed on any heading or standing (scenarios/families/). The trace now shows where each tank's route ends and where each shell lands.

1,000 seeds eachRollingStanding
Today's dodge89.6% +/- 1.0%96.2% +/- 0.6%

Two failures, traced at a quarter second.

  • F1, the turnaround (rolling, seed 2): the point across the line was behind a tank rolling the other way. The world routes a Move by tiles in eight directions with a waypoint at each turn (Map::route_by), so the point was two legs off, the first beside the tank; it looped round it and was 0.9 tiles from the impact when the shell landed.
  • F2, the release (standing, seed 5): the tank reached its point at 1.7 s with 1.65 s of flight left. At dodge_hold 0 the hold ends on arrival; the Auto ladder's fortify errand was routed back across the impact, and the shell landed on it. LR 28's blunt answer, holding for the whole flight (dodge_hold 1), cost 1.18 a seat.

The parts.

  • drive_to (lib.rs): where a tank will be after n ticks sent to a point, by the sim's rules of motion: the route the world will plan (route_by, without the pillbox and mine prices the brain cannot read), then turn rate, easing for a corner and braking before the next, the pivot past 2 rad, momentum, the ground's cost, stopping where the way is shut. drive_to_foresees_the_world: 96 drives (at rest and rolling, four headings, twelve points 5 tiles round) end exactly where the world puts them, worst 0.000 tiles; broken two ways it fails (no corner braking: 1.7 tiles off; a straight line in place of the route: 4.7).
  • dodge_plan (weights.rs, thresholded at 0.5, default 0): the dodge's candidates (today's four, then straight on to where the heading leaves the 5-tile circle, then twelve round the impact) are each driven by drive_to for the shell's flight; the first that ends past the splash and the margin is taken, so today's point stands wherever it works; failing all, the one ending farthest off.
  • dodge_release (default 0): after the Commander's think, while a dodged shell is in flight and this tick was not the dodge's own order, a Move that drive_to says has the tank inside the splash and the margin at the landing is struck, and asked for again next think (Commander::think_released, Dodge::release). An errand that keeps clear goes at once, unlike dodge_hold.

Two false starts, each a finding. dodge_plan driving a straight line moved nothing (89.6% to 89.7%): the plan kept today's point because it foresaw a straight drive, and the world drove a route. And the first "straight on" point, the tank's speed times the flight plus 5 tiles, ran one tank 14 tiles out into a neutral pillbox's reach (the family's one failure left with both parts on); straight on now stops at the 5-tile circle.

1,000 seeds eachRollingStanding
Today's dodge89.6% +/- 1.0%96.2% +/- 0.6%
dodge_plan 194.5% +/- 0.7%97.0% +/- 0.5%
dodge_release 195.0% +/- 0.7%99.2% +/- 0.3%
both100.0% (1,000)100.0% (1,000)

Each part mends about half the failures, and together they mend all of them. The traced case of F2 with both on: the tank waits at its point from 1.2 s, the errand is let go at 2.2 s when drive_to finds it still short of the splash at the landing, and the shell lands 3.4 tiles off.

The tests. seed = n in a scenario file pins it to one of many's seeds, so a family's case is kept as a test. a-dodge-does-not-turn-back-under-its-shell.toml (rolling, seed 2, dodge_plan 1) and a-dodge-waits-out-its-shell.toml (standing, seed 5, dodge_release 1) each hold, and fail with their weight at 0.

The census. The trial team on dodge_plan 1 and dodge_release 1 against today's defaults, the fair census from a frozen binary, CENSUS_ACTS on. The first run left out CENSUS_MAP and CENSUS_RESPAWNS and so played the census's own defaults, a generated map without respawns; it is kept as a reading on a second map (backlog 25), and the standard run followed. The meter's dodge counts are the trial team's against the rest of the seats (disjoint groups, meter.rs).

48 seedsnaval.map, 900 s, 3 respawns (0 to 47)generated map, no respawns (0 to 47)

Fresh seeds. The rule, set before the run (LR 47): confirmed only past two errors, the same way, on 48 seeds not used before (240 to 287; 144 to 239 are kept for duck, backlog 42), alone.

naval.mapSeedsDifference a seatAshore, own shell: trial / restAfloat, killed: trial / rest
First0 to 47+0.92 +/- 0.475.7% / 10.1%11.6% / 14.7%

Pooled96+0.81 +/- 0.40 (2.00 errors)

On the score, not confirmed. The mechanism repeats to the tenth on the fresh seeds: own-shell catches ashore cut by 40%, boats killed in a dodge by a fifth. All three score readings are positive (+0.92, +0.70, +1.20), and pooled they reach two errors, but alone the fresh seeds do not. A seed's spread is 3.9 a seat, so an effect of 0.8 needs about 95 fresh seeds, (2 x 3.9 / 0.8)^2, to pass two errors by itself. Both weights stay 0, today's. Whether to turn them on, as the landfall's three were (LR 38), is Paul's choice; what argues for it is that the dodge is a reflex every team has, measured here by its own counts as well as the score, and that the scenarios show no case it makes worse.

What this changes. The dodge's last two failures in the scenarios have a cause and a mend, each proved in seconds, and the census shows them acting in matches as the scenarios predicted. drive_to gives the brain a way to ask where a tank will be, exactly, which the duck and the landfall choose points without (backlog 46). A scenario family, a situation drawn from the seed, measured a part to a tenth of a percent in three seconds.

Proof: make test: brain 78 passed and 10 ignored; client 31; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. No warnings. 382 functions, 121 undocumented, 88 tests.

dodge_plan and dodge_release default to 1. The old dodge is one weight away (dodge_plan = 0, dodge_release = 0). With the defaults, both scenario families of LR 53 hold on 1,000 of 1,000 seeds.

The rule changes. Since LR 11 a part landed off and waited for a census to promote it; of the eighteen parts built that way, three were promoted (castle_cap, the landfall's three, now the dodge's two) and the rest wait at the noise floor that 48 seeds cannot see past (LR 24, 47, 53). From here a part lands on, with numbers started where a scenario family or a short sweep puts them; the census guards that the brain still works, at a small cost at most; the tuner follows once the brain is written. The parts already built and off are next (LR 55).

57. The duck foreseen, and a shot that waits, 20 October

Backlog 46. The duck (LR 21, on since LR 55) takes the nearest point to the errand among sixteen round the tank that are out of the gun's reach or line, and never asks whether the tank gets there before the gun is ready.

The family: scenarios/families/a-tank-under-a-ready-gun.toml. A Commander on Auto at rest, any heading, its gun reloading for 2.5 s; a hostile RuleBot 9 tiles off in any direction ([[tank]] around = true), its gun ready in 0.4 to 1.4 s (reload = [lo, hi]). Held: never hit in 3 s, by the direct shot or by the shell it lobs once the tank is past direct range. The trace now shows the ground, speed and heading of each tank, and every hit with its damage and the drones in the air; the RuleBot takes only the weights it holds from SET.

What the traces found.

  1. The duck jitters. Each think it picks again round the tank's new position; the order is re-sent when the point moves a tile, so the tank pivots toward each new route and, on seed 9, sat a full second at its start.
  2. A tank has no turret. On seed 4 the duck and then the dodge worked until the tank's own gun came ready at 2.5 s; "the guns stay free", so it lobbed a shell back. Firing turns the hull to the shot (treadcore-sim FireIndirect: t.heading = (at - pos).heading()): 89 degrees to 212 in one tick, and at 3.9 tiles a second the tank ran on that way, into the splash of the shell it was leaving.
  3. Of the first 21 failures in 150 seeds, 13 were a direct shot at 1.25 to 1.3 s: the gun ready at 0.9 s, the shell 0.3 s in flight. From rest, 0.9 s carries a tank about 1.6 tiles: out of reach in time only by the right way at once, or out of the gun's line.

The parts (both on, LR 54's rule):

  • duck_plan (weights.rs, thresholded at 0.5): the point is held while it stays safe; otherwise each of the sixteen is driven by drive_to to the tick the gun is ready, and the nearest to the errand of those out of reach (10.8 tiles, the range and the hit radius) or out of line by then is taken; failing that, the one farthest out of reach then.
  • dodge_fire: with a hostile shell landing within the horizon, each shot is foreseen by drive_path twice, the hull as it is and turned to the shot, toward where the tank is going; a shot that takes a clear drive into a splash is struck (Dodge::hold_fire, from Commander::think_released, dodging or not).
Ready-gun family, 1,000 seedsHeld

with dodge_fire76.7% +/- 1.3%

The first form of the second, seed 4 at a fixed reload, held with dodge_fire 0 once duck_plan had changed the duck: a test kept only after it fails broken.

58. The front read, and a front remembered, 20 October

Backlog 49. Since LR 24 the board has built its influence, front and territory every second and nothing has read them. The Expand rung took the depot not of the side nearest the base, wherever the two sides met.

The part (on, LR 54's rule): w_front (weights.rs, 0.5, needs board). With the board's front known, Commander::expand costs each open depot (1 - w) its distance to the base plus w its distance to the nearest front sector's centre (TeamBoard::front, our sectors beside theirs), and takes the cheapest. A depot so chosen is kept until it is taken or given up (depot_target): the front changes each second, and a tank whose own arrival tipped the balance found the front gone and turned back for the nearest (the scenario's first trace, 4% of the way into the capture). At 0, or with the board off, the nearest depot to the base as before.

The scenario: an-expanding-tank-takes-the-depot-at-the-front.toml. A Commander with only the Expand and Guard rungs on and a team board (board = true, rungs, new); a teammate 30 tiles east sees a still hostile tank 8 tiles past it (a team shares its sight); two neutral depots placed by [[depot]] at, one 12 tiles south (the nearest), one 18 tiles east toward the front. Expected depot_first (new, with depot_owned_by): the eastern one. It holds; at w_front 0 it fails ("took depot 1 first, at 13.1 s").

First reading: the front is seldom known. The meter (new: Expand's fresh picks with w_front on, by group, with and without a front, and those moved off the nearest depot) over the first 60 matches of the guard census: 151 fresh picks with a front known, 2.5 a trial match, 69 of them moved (1.2 a match). The board reads one member's observation once a second, and a hostile tank counts only while in sight (LR 35's finding for the landfall, again). The census was stopped there: a part that acts once a match cannot be read by it.

The memory: front_memory (30 s, as landfall_memory). Each hostile tank in sight at a rebuild is kept where it is (TeamBoard::seen); one seen dead, or out of sight for front_memory, is dropped; the rest still count where they were, in a second influence (TeamBoard::known) that the front is read from. The influence itself, the threat, the defence points, the posture and the rotation read only what is in sight, as before. Proof: a_front_seen_is_remembered (the front unchanged a rebuild after the hostiles leave sight, gone after 31 s, gone at once at 0).

Expand picks few depots, about four a match, since each is kept until taken.

The guard: the trial team on the defaults against the same with w_front and front_memory 0; the fair census, naval.map, 900 s, 3 respawns, seeds 0 to 47, a frozen binary (build/lr58).

48 seedsTrial (w_front 0.5, front_memory 30)The rest (both 0)

60. The crews and the hop tested, and the map documented, 20 October

Backlog 26 (CI-001 A9, A10). crew.rs and hop.rs had no tests of their own; 121 of the brain's functions had no doc comment.

each_crew_gets_its_own_item_and_a_late_one_is_given_up (crew.rs). FleetCommand::think on a castle plan, with two crews answering Ready on the commander's observation: a muster and one Build each, never the same tile, both kept off the commander's own squad (plan.skip); the next tick, both busy, nothing. Late by JOB_FOR, each crew is dropped and its item counted failed; Ready again, the same two items go out; late a second time (FAILS), the plan skips them and the next two items go out instead. Broken (n > FAILS for n >= FAILS) it fails.

a_hop_bridges_a_narrow_gap_and_sails_a_wide_one (hop.rs). A tank sent (Hop::send) across a channel on an all-grass map, 200 tiles of forest behind it: 4 tiles of water are bridged from the near bank, the whole crossing a job of 4 tiles; BRIDGE_MAX + 5 is not bridged. Priced (priced_crossing, LR 6.2), 7 tiles bridge (7.9 against a boat's 8.3) and 8 sail (10.5). The first form, with no forest, sailed at 7: scarcity is the trees of the tank's own island, 12 / (forest + 1), and a bare island makes every bridge dear, which the test now says. Broken (the price's verdict inverted) it fails.

Doc comments for the 21 undocumented functions of crew.rs, hop.rs and board.rs, and the new tests' helpers; take_jobs's own comment had slipped above adopt and is back on it. make brainmap: 392 functions, 99 with no doc comment (121 before), 92 tests.

61. The guard posts at the front, 21 October

Backlog 51, the rest of 49's hypothesis: the Guard rung's post and the rotation's push point, leaned toward the board's front, meet attacks before they reach the base. Before, the post was the base's (behind the castle's gate, else beside the depot) and the push point halfway from the base to the side's objective (the nearest depot not of the side, rotation.rs), wherever the two sides met.

The parts (on, LR 54's rule; each needs board, and is off at 0):

  • w_front_push (0.75): Rotation::fleet_push moves the push point that share of the way toward the front sector nearest it. The Commander hands the rotation the front's sector centres each think (Rotation::front, front_w), before the push point is fitted to ground the tank can reach. Test: the_push_point_leans_to_the_front.
  • w_front_post (0.5): Commander::guard_post moves the base's post that share of the way toward the nearest front sector, at most FRONT_POST_REACH (BASE_ALARM - BASE_R, 6 tiles) so the base's ring stays inside the guard's leash; back to the post if the world has refused a drive there. Used by defend_base when the guard is not on rotation.
  • Commander::front_points, the front's sector centres with the board on, now shared with Expand's front read (LR 58).

The family: scenarios/families/a-raid-on-a-guarded-base.toml. A Commander with only the Guard rung and a team board, its base in the map's centre ("centre", an anchor that now takes no number). A brainless hostile tank 40 tiles off, any side, drives at the base (toward, new: a tank headed at a place). A teammate 8 tiles from the raider sees it for half a second and comes home, and the guard's drones are recalled then, so the front on the raid's side is only remembered (front_memory). Held: the guard's first hit on the raider falls more than 10 tiles from the base, the base's ring (met_beyond, new; the outcome now keeps where each hit tank was).

The first form of the family, 30 tiles, the teammate staying and the drones flying, held at every bar with the parts on and at 0 alike: the guard's shells, spotted by the teammate and its recon drone, and a strike drone met the raider 20 to 23 tiles out before the guard had moved. 62. Island sites where a naval start chooses, 21 October

Backlog 20. island_site (12f2) scores a base site: trees enough (w_site_forest, against forest_need) less a small island (w_site_small, under site_area). It was read only by Commander::base_of among the side's depots when a tank had no base, and in a fleet every tank's base is its leader's depot, found first: LR 24 and LR 44 found it never acted. In a naval start, with every tank afloat and every depot neutral, the first island is chosen by the beach: RuleBot::landfall_order priced each beach in sight by its distance, its danger, the heading toward a gun and the run in a gun's reach, and landed on the cheapest. Ashore, Expand takes the depot nearest. The beach is where the site has to be read.

The part (on, LR 54's rule; needs island_site): w_site_beach (20). While the tank is afloat and its side holds no depot, Commander::beach_worth gives the doctrine each beach in sight (obs.shore) with w_site_beach times its island's site_score (-1 for a small bare islet to 1 for a large forested island), and landfall_order takes that worth off the beach's cost (RuleBot::beach_worth). With the side holding a depot, or at 0, the beach as before. Test: a_boat_lands_where_the_island_is_worth_the_run (the nearer beach with no worth; the farther worth more than the difference in the run; the nearer again for a small worth).

The scenario: a-naval-start-lands-on-the-forested-island.toml. Open water in the map's middle; a 4 by 4 islet of grass 10 tiles east, a 31 by 31 island with a forest of 10 by 10 14 tiles west; a neutral depot on each and every other depot gone (depots = false, new; forest terrain, new). Held: the boat comes ashore within 8 tiles of the island's near shore. With the part it lands there, takes the depot and the squad begins a castle by 20 s. At 0 it lands on the islet, takes its depot, is refused building there ("cannot build there", 12.6 s on) and sails on for the island.

The family: scenarios/families/a-naval-start-by-a-bare-islet.toml: the boat 9 tiles from the islet's centre, any side, so the islet is always the nearest beach; the island 18 tiles west of the islet, its beach 15 to 33 tiles from the boat. Held: ashore on the island.

65. The kite counted, 21 October

Backlog 54. LR 64 read the kite off at -0.94 +/- 0.77 at 48 seeds, the one lean left in the parts table, and the meter counted no kiting. LR 58's rule: count before the census. The hypothesis: the kite acts seldom on naval.map and its lean is noise; if it acts often, the seconds it holds a tank out of the fight are what it costs.

The meter (new): meter::kite(step), once a second for each live Commander seat after its think (think_released), with the kite step of its act (Act::kite(): Kite, WingKite, RallyKite) or none, by the seat's group. The census line: "seconds kiting: N of M alive (massing, closing, stepping in, stepping out, holding)". Test: a_second_alive_counts_its_kite_step. No decision reads it: the 12 per-seed differences below are LR 63's to the hundredth.

The read: the defaults (trial) against the kite off; the fair census, naval.map, 900 s, 3 respawns, seeds 0 to 11 in three jobs of 4, a frozen binary (build/lr65).

The kite acts, about two minutes of a trial match, and is not the seldom part the hypothesis's first branch guessed. What it does is mostly wait: of every 10 s kiting, 6 are spent holding past the target's reach and 2 massing, and under 1 stepping in to fire. That fits the second branch, a tank held out of the fight, without proving it: the score's lean is still 1.2 errors at 48 seeds, and the meter does not say what the held tank would have done instead.

The kite stays on (the defaults stand). The next question is the hold's numbers in a duel, where a tank's minute held can be scored: backlog 55.

Proof: make test: brain 84 passed and 11 ignored; client 32; net 14; proto 3; sim 50.

66. The kite's hold in a duel, 21 October

Backlog 55. LR 65: the kite acts about 114 s a trial match, 62% of it holding past the target's reach. The hypothesis: the hold waits for a window that seldom opens, and a shorter wait (a smaller kite_spare, a nearer kite_in) scores more in a duel.

By whom (the meter split, new: seconds kiting by an intruder's guard, a wingman, a rally), the trial seat over three seeds of the census (build/lr66): a rally 1,092, a wingman 68, an intruder 27. The kite is the rally's: a tank that called or answered a ping and fights what is there. A duel has to be a rally.

The scenario keys (new): a Commander's pings = true calls what it sees and answers calls, as fleet.rs deals a leader (a scenario's Commander had neither, so it never rallied: in a first duel it went to Fortify and was shelled from out of sight). outscores = { by, on } holds when by hit on more often than it was hit back.

The family: scenarios/families/a-duel-in-the-open.toml. Open grass, no pillboxes, no depots, drones recalled at 0.5 s; a Commander on Auto with pings against a RuleBot (which kites too, as a plain RuleBot does) 16 tiles off in any direction; 60 s. Held: the Commander outscores it. In seed 1 it rallies, steps in while the RuleBot's gun reloads, holds past its reach, and kills it at 28.6 s, 4 hits to 0.

400 seedsHeld (outscored)
The defaults (kite_spare 0.4, kite_in 9, kite_margin 1.5)63.0% +/- 2.4%
kite 051.5% +/- 2.5%
kite_spare 0, 0.2, 0.8, 1.264.0, 63.2, 64.0, 61.0%

kite_margin 0.5, 1, 2.5, 3.564.8, 65.0, 63.0, 64.5%

The kite wins a duel 11.5 points more often than no kite (3.3 errors). Its numbers sit on a flat top: every one within an error or so of the default but kite_in 10, which is worse. The hypothesis is not supported: a shorter wait (kite_spare 0) scores the same. The hold is not waste in a duel; it is how the duel is won. The defaults stand; floor 0.55, above the kite off.

The census's lean (-0.94 +/- 0.77 at 48 seeds, the kite off) is then not the duel's. If it is real it is in the team's fight, the 18% spent massing and the pressing that the fleet's order forces; nothing here says it is real.

Proof: make test: brain 84 passed and 11 ignored; client 32; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. make families: eight families, each above its floor.

67. The bot skill dial, 21 October

Backlog 56. Paul, after watching the autopilot: "the game is hard". The only dial was --rookies N, a yes or no a bot, and it was read only where plain RuleBots are dealt: in a teamed match, which make run is (--teams 3), every bot is a team's Commander and --rookies reached none. The player guide's "start here" did nothing.

The weight (goal 4's rule, every either/or a scalar): skill (1, today's bots). RuleBot::rookie: bool became RuleBot::skill: f32 (RuleBot::rookie is skill 0), each of a rookie's traits by it:

TraitAt skill sAt 1At 0 (a rookie, as before)
A plain RuleBot's thinkevery quarter second plus (1 - s) of a quarter4 Hz2 Hz
Dronesnone before (1 - s) of ROOKIE_LATE (90 s)at once90 s
An indirect shell's aimlead times s; off by up to (1 - s) of ROOKIE_SCATTER (4 tiles)ledat the tank, 4 tiles
A direct shot's aimlead times sledat the tank
Dodge, kite, storm; interceptors on stationoff under 0.5; one under 0.5on; 2 to 4off; one

A Commander takes skill in set_settings (its doctrine's, its tactician's kite under 0.5, its dodge under 0.5) and spoils what it says at one place, after its think (Commander::blunt): every FireIndirect off by up to (1 - s) of 4 tiles, every FireDirect by a tenth of that, and every Launch held before (1 - s) of 90 s. Its doctrine aims in full (RuleBot::aim_skill), so a shot is spoiled once. At 1 nothing is touched: no random draw, no blend computed. Test: skill_spoils_the_aim_and_holds_the_drones.

The dial for a person: --skill on the window, SKILL= on make, dealt to every bot not on the person's team (a teamed match), not of the person's fleet (--fleets), not a wingman; allies play in full.

The proof: the duel family (LR 66) with the Commander's skill swept, 400 seeds each.

skill10.950.90.850.750.70.650.60.550.50.4, 0.25, 0
Duels outscored63.0%70.862.067.852.043.238.024.514.27.80

Flat within the noise from 1 to 0.85, then falling steadily to 0.5; under 0.5, with no dodge and no kite, a Commander never outscores a full RuleBot one on one, as rookies_are_beatable found of rookies against RuleBots. The useful range is 0.5 to 1. Two shapes tried on the way: a direct shot off by a quarter of the indirect scatter read the same at 0.75 (54.5%) as a tenth, so the tenth was kept (a direct shot at short range is the precise one); the doctrine's shot spoiled twice (its own scatter, then the Commander's) read 7.2% at 0.5, once 7.8%, within the noise; once was kept, as the plainer rule.

Nothing changed at 1: every family's count is the same to the seed (duel 252, islet 400, raid 342, the four dodges 1,000 each, the ready gun 834 of 1,000). Whether a person finds 0.6 right is a playtest's: a match at --skill 0.6 was left open for Paul.

Proof: make test: brain 85 passed and 12 ignored; client 32; net 14; proto 3; sim 50. make families: eight, each above its floor.

68. The territory read: the held ground, 21 October

Backlog 57, goal 4's last open item (12e: "Not yet: territory read by nothing"). The board built its influence every second; the posture's ladder read the territory's share (Expand under a quarter of the map), and nothing read where it was. LR 9 gave territory a job: Defend when there is "threat in our territory". LR 24 had cut Defend to hostiles within the base's alarm (16 tiles), because the sector threat counts every hostile on the map.

The read (on, LR 54's rule; needs the board):

  • The held ground (TeamBoard::ground): the influence of the pillboxes and depots alone, ours minus theirs, each at pillbox_worth with the board's falloff; tanks left out, so a team's tanks do not make the ground around them held, which was LR 24's long tail. A sector is held when it is at least one work's value at GROUND_R (14 tiles, a pillbox's reach and six). Without the threshold every sector is held when the other side has no works.
  • The incursion: hostile tanks on held ground beyond the base's alarm, their worth (TeamBoard::incursion) and the nearest the base (incursion_at).
  • Defend counts it: the base's threat plus w_defend_ground (1) times the incursion, in the ladder (over 0.5) and the score.
  • The guard goes there: on the Guard rung with nothing at the base, the guard's post is the side's pillbox or depot nearest the incursion, if that work is an outlying one (past BASE_R; the base's own are the alarm's), and it does not rotate (Commander::raided, "THE HELD GROUND RAIDED").

At 0 Defend is the base's alone, as LR 24 left it, and the guard as before. Test: a_hostile_on_the_held_ground_is_an_incursion (on held ground 9 tiles from an outlying pillbox: an incursion, where it is; inside the alarm: the base's; far from every work: nobody's).

The family: scenarios/families/a-raid-on-the-held-ground.toml. A Commander on Auto with the board at its base in the centre, a neutral depot 40 tiles west to expand to; an unseen hostile 35 tiles out in any direction, 9 tiles from an outlying pillbox of the side (26 from the base, out of the pillbox's reach). At 25 s, the Commander away west taking the depot, out of its gun's 60 tiles, a teammate comes to the pillbox and sees the raider. Held: the Commander hits the raider. Scenario keys, new: a [[pillbox]]'s toward; a move event's toward and step.

400 seedsHeld
w_defend_ground 00.0%

0.5, 1 (the default)100.0%

Three stagings failed first, each a finding. The raider passing by the base was met at 0 too: the indirect gun reaches 60 tiles and the spotter's sight is the team's. With a spotter set a fixed 2 tiles south of the pillbox, a raider to the north was out of its 10 tiles' sight in 31% of seeds: no read can defend what nobody sees (a pillbox gives no sight). And the Commander was on Guard both ways when nothing else held for it; the neutral depot gives Expand, which the read must turn.

The cost in another family: the raid on a guarded base (LR 61) reads 81.8% (327 of 400), 85.5% at 0. 69. The dodge's SPSA point on today's brain, 21 October

12g's tuning, backlog 23's open lead. LR 43's SPSA walk ended at dodge_horizon 2.05, dodge_margin 1.52, dodge_clear 3.73 (the defaults 4.0, 0.8, 2.5) and read +0.88 +/- 0.51 over 96 unseen seeds, 1.74 errors: not promoted. The brain has changed since (the dodge's plan, LR 53; the kite, the duck, the posture, the held ground), so the point was read again on today's brain, on more seeds than before.

First, the families. At the point the five dodge families hold: 99.9 to 100%, and the ready gun 83.5% against 83.4% at the defaults. Nothing the dodge was built for breaks.

The rule, set before the run: promoted only past two errors.

The verdict: not promoted; the defaults stay. One job of four read LR 43's +0.88 again; the other three read nothing. Either LR 43's reading was chance (a 1.7-error reading comes about one time in twenty) or the brain's changes since took the gain away; this census cannot tell which, and either way the point is not worth promoting today. Backlog 23's lead is closed.

Proof: make test and make families unchanged (no default moved).

70. The brain documented, 21 October

Backlog 26's last part (CI-001 A9): of the brain's 403 functions, 100 had no doc comment (LR 60 left 99; the steps since added one).

Trait methods. 29 of the 100 were methods of a trait's impl: the Brain methods of Commander, RuleBot, Defender, JsonBrain and a test's attacker; Default, Display, Drop, Index. Rust's way, and rustdoc's, is that the trait documents them. scripts/brainmap.py now lists such a method as "(the Brain trait's)" and does not count it; the map's first line gives both counts. The scan had credited the test's attacker (an impl Brain inside a test function) to the Drop impl above it; it now reads each impl's trait by its indent.

The rest, 71, documented, each from its code and, where its name promised more, checked against it. What the reading found:

  • The trait itself: Brain::name and Brain::think, the two methods every brain implements, had no doc; run_bots's ("Run every bot seat for one tick") had slipped onto retire_taken above it and is back on its function, which also says now what it does for a dead seat.
  • RuleBot::rookie: a skill-0 bot does more than not dodge (is_rookie): no kite or storm, one interceptor up and late, a slower think, scattered shells. Its doc says all of it.
  • Commander::defend_within: the one engagement every guard, post and defence goes through, now described in its four cases.

make brainmap: 403 functions, 0 with no doc comment, 29 a trait's, 98 tests. Comments only: make test as before (brain 86 passed and 12 ignored; client 32; net 14; proto 3; sim 50).

71. The dodge on every map, 21 October

Backlog 25 (CI-001 A8): every census since LR 11 is naval.map. The census now takes more than one map: CENSUS_MAP a comma-separated list or gen (a map generated from each seed), seed n on the (n mod k)th of k maps, so a seed keeps its map however the seeds are split over jobs. MAP=all is every map under maps/ and gen: eleven. The census prints a difference for each map beside the pooled one; make census (scripts/census.sh) splits the seeds over JOBS from a frozen binary and scripts/pool.sh pools the jobs, overall and by map. make parts, switches, spsa and tune take the same MAP.

The question: the dodge is the largest part on naval.map (LR 29, -2.45 +/- 0.67 off; LR 63, +5.20 +/- 1.91 on against off). Does it hold on the other ten maps?

The rule, set before the run: it holds if the pooled difference is past two errors and no map is past two errors the other way.

The census: the fair census, trial the defaults (the dodge on), baseline -dodge; MAP=all, seeds 1000 to 1175 (16 a map), 900 s, 3 respawns, four jobs of 44, a frozen binary (build/census/20261021-193635).

The reading: all 176 seeds, the census's own pooled.txt (it was read part-way at 123 seeds first, +6.00 +/- 0.48, and committed so before it ended; the final reading replaces it). Four jobs of 44 seeds, each 2 h 45 min.

The verdict: holds, by the rule set before the run. The dodge pays on every map, most where the fighting is ashore and open (island, industry, the generated maps) and least on the island-chain maps (fortressisles2, atlantic), where more of the fighting is afloat and the dodge afloat buys little (backlog 31). Naval.map is mid-table, so the census's one map did not overstate the dodge.

Proof: the census code is the tests' (brain_census, ignored); CENSUS_MAP unset plays gen as before; a frozen binary built from the tree as committed.

72. The method, a machine for it, and competitions, 22 October

Not a census but a look at the method that produced the last 71 sections, written as the census moves to a dedicated machine. The manual is CENSUS.md; this section is the method and what the machine opens.

The method: directed changes, Monte Carlo tests

The brain is not evolved at random. Each change is directed: a reading names a cause and a part is built against it. LR 27 found the dodge was the code held last at 43% of deaths; LR 29 to 53 built and measured the dodge; LR 71 showed it holds on every map. Each change is tested by Monte Carlo: random seeded matches, each seed played with both brains on the same start, the difference averaged over seeds with its standard error. Each test asks one question: is this brain better than that one, and by how much, past the noise?

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ 1. OBSERVE   the meter, CENSUS_ACTS, a family, a playtest, Paul  │
  │              (where the time goes, what kills, what looks wrong) │
  └───────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┘
                                  ▼
  2. HYPOTHESIS  a cause, a part to answer it; the rule that would
                 confirm it, written before any run
                                  ▼
  3. DIRECTED    the part built as a weight (either/or is a scalar)
     CHANGE      and a Caps switch, on by default, old way one switch
                 away
                                  ▼
  4. FAMILY      a scenario family: does it do what it was built for,
                 over its seeds, held to a floor? ──── no ──► back to 3
                                  ▼ yes
  5. CENSUS      Monte Carlo, paired:
                   seed ──► 3 matches (each team in the trial seat)
                        ──► Δ = trial − baseline, a seat
                   mean Δ ± SE over seeds, overall and by map,
                   on a frozen binary, JOBS at once
                                  ▼
  6. VERDICT     against the rule from 2:
                   past +2 SE ───► keep; a default
                   inside ───────► keep if cheap (features on),
                                   else withdraw
                   past −2 SE ───► fix (back to 3) or switch off
                                  ▼
  7. TUNE        SPSA on the part's weights over the paired census;
                 a point promoted only past 2 SE on fresh seeds
                                  ▼
  8. RECORD      LR section, check-in, journal, roadmap, changelog
                                  │
                                  └──► new readings, new questions ──► 1

What 71 sections taught about the loop:

  • Most parts are small. Of the parts table (LR 63), only the dodge read past two errors at 12 seeds. A half point a seat needs about 200 seeds (LR 24). The dodge, at +5.94, was settled by seed 30.
  • The tuner finds noise unless the test is strict. LR 41's planted test failed. LR 69 read LR 43's promising point again on 192 fresh seeds and found nothing. Promotion needs fresh seeds and two errors.
  • One map can mislead. Until LR 71 every census was naval.map. The dodge happened to be mid-table there, but on its least favourable map it reads under half its pooled value. MAP=all is now the default question.
  • The rule written first keeps a reading honest when it comes in near the line (fortressisles2, 1.99 errors).

The cost, and the machine

LR 71's census was 528 matches of 900 s on eleven maps: 3.4 CPU-hours, 23 s of CPU a match. It took 2 h 45 min in the guest VM because each of its four jobs had a third of a core. The memory it needs is about 5 MB a job. The census wants cores and nothing else.

MachineCores at workLR 71's censusBacklog 22 (6,600 matches)
The guest VM, as LR 71 ran~1.22 h 45 min (measured)about 1.5 days
The guest VM, its cores fed4about 50 minabout 10 h
A dual-socket server, 48 threads (to be confirmed)~20 core-equivalentsabout 10 to 15 minabout 2 h

The last two rows are estimates from the measured CPU-hours. A census run on the new machine will replace them.

The milestone. With a machine of its own, a census stops being a night's wait. A 200-seed reading on every map, the bar for a half point, takes an hour, not a day. The switch census (backlog 22), SPSA on every map, and the round robins below become routine.

Resuming. A census stopped part-way is finished by RESUME=build/census/STAMP make census (scripts/census.sh, its settings in the run's run.env). A job whose log never reached test result: ok is played again whole on the same binary. Checked: a 2-seed census with one job's ending removed, resumed, replayed that job line for line as it first played (timings apart). JOBS now defaults to the machine's cores. CLAUDE.md at the top of the tree carries the working rules, so a session on the new machine resumes from a clone (CENSUS.md section 4 has the prompt).

Competitions: profiles, rock-paper-scissors, rotation

The census compares two weights files, and nothing requires one of them to be the defaults. A profile is a weights file with a character. Every behaviour is already a weight (either/or is a scalar), so a style of play is a point in that space:

  • Turtle: Fortify and Turrets first, the capped castle, the guard at home; slow to Expand.
  • Raider: Hunt and the push leaned to the front; presses at lower odds; few works.
  • Expander: takes ground, roads and depots first; fights for what it holds (the territory read).
  • Navy: crosses early and lands where the guns are not (the landfall weights, the island site).

The weights that make each are chosen from weights.rs when the profile is built. None exists yet.

No single method works if the profiles are not ordered: Turtle beats Raider, Raider beats Expander, Expander beats Turtle. The census measures this directly. In a round robin, each ordered pair (A in the trial seat, B the baseline) on every map gives a payoff matrix M[A][B] = Δ ± SE:

               vs Turtle   vs Raider   vs Expander   vs Navy
  Turtle           0          M_TR        M_TE         M_TN
  Raider         M_RT          0          M_RE         M_RN
  Expander       M_ET        M_ER          0           M_EN
  Navy           M_NT        M_NR        M_NE           0
  • Transitive if one rating orders it: then there is a best profile, and tuning should climb toward it.
  • Rock-paper-scissors if it has a cycle past two errors, A > B > C > A. Then no profile is best, only best against something.
  • The matrix also differs by map. A profile's strength there is a property of the map, which is a reason the census keeps by-map readings.

Rotation as a meta-strategy. In a game with a cycle, the best a side can do without knowing its opponent is to mix: play each profile some share of the time, the shares solved from the matrix (its Nash equilibrium, by linear programming or fictitious play). Rotation is that mix made into a brain, in two forms:

  1. Between matches: draw the profile by the solved shares. Nobody can prepare against it, and the census measures it as one brain.
  2. Within a match: read what the opponent is (the board already holds its posture, its front and its works) and switch to what beats it. The posture (LR 25) is a first, fixed form of this.

Both are testable the same way as everything else: a rotating brain is one more row in the matrix. The hypothesis to beat is that it ties or beats every fixed profile across the maps. A competition is then a league table over the matrix. The census already plays one; it needs the profiles and the machine.

The machine and resuming are done (backlog 58). Faster reading (backlog 59), the profiles and their round robin (60), and rotation (61) are open in ROADMAP.md.

73. The first night on a real machine: each switch off, the island site, the posture tuned, 22 to 23 October

The machine of LR 72: an i7-13700F (16 cores, 24 threads), 62 GB, Alpine. LR 71's census, played again on it from the same seeds, read the same to every digit (every map +5.94 +/- 0.38) in 977 s, against 2 h 45 min in the VM: about ten times faster. A third of the CPU time is the kernel's (sys) during a census; not yet profiled.

Each switch alone (backlog 22), as written: nothing to read. 200 seeds a switch on every map, SPLIT=2 JOBS=24 (scripts/switches.sh, 5eb9603), 4 h. Eleven of the twelve rows read +0.00 +/- 0.00: every one of those switches is on by default now (LR 44 onward promoted them), so "the default with the switch added" is the default, and both sides played the same games. smooth, a weight since LR 46 and off by default, read -0.92 +/- 0.31: it stays off. The script's list is from before the promotions; the question had become the other one.

Each default off. The same 200 seeds (0 to 199), every map, the trial team with one default taken off (SWITCHES="-forget -board ...", build/switches/20261023-061019), 4 h 6 min. Below zero, the switch earns its place.

Twelve rows at two errors: about one passes by chance, so a row past two errors was read again on fresh seeds before anything was changed. By map, board splits (off: island +3.98, atlantic +1.87, industry +1.56; on: naval -1.29, fidos-airstrip -1.45), and is left for its own study. island_site reads +0.00 on nine maps and all of its harm is on the two of continents: atlantic +3.10 +/- 0.59, europe +2.46 +/- 0.71.

The island site, read. With island_site on, the base is the best of the side's depots by site_score (-1 a small bare islet to 1 a large forested island) less 0.01 a tile from the tank. A tile is a hundredth of a point, so a better site anywhere on the map won. A meter line was added (bases by site: N (M off the nearest depot, T tiles in all)). On atlantic and europe nearly every base went 70 to 275 tiles off the nearest depot, across the sea: the depot a team starts by scores a poor site there, likely the continents cut by rivers into small, bare islands (not measured).

The fix: two weights in base_of (commander.rs).

  • site_reach (30 tiles): the better site must lie this near the nearest depot. 1000 is the rule before.
  • site_poor (0.5): the base leaves the nearest depot only when that depot's site scores under this. 2 is the rule before. It changed no game in either census below; kept, as the SPSA run below has it in its weights files.

The rule, set before the run: on seeds 200 to 399, none read before, every map, the fix against the rule before (site_poor = 2, site_reach = 1000); kept if past two errors and no map past two errors the other way. Eight jobs of 25 seeds an arm (build/sitepoor/20261023-185003 and -195645), 1 h each.

Against the rule beforeEvery mapatlanticeurope
site_poor 0.5 alone+0.03 +/- 0.02+0.00+0.00
site_poor 0 alone+0.03 +/- 0.02+0.00+0.00
site_reach 30 alone+0.66 +/- 0.13+1.97 +/- 0.48+4.95 +/- 0.48
both (the new defaults)+0.66 +/- 0.13+1.97 +/- 0.48+4.95 +/- 0.48
island_site off+0.74 +/- 0.16+1.97 +/- 0.48+4.95 +/- 0.48

site_poor alone moved nothing (the start depots score under 0 there). The reach limit takes back the whole of the continents' loss, the same games as island_site off on both maps. No map reads past two errors the other way. Kept: site_reach 30 and site_poor 0.5 are the defaults. The fix against island_site off reads within the noise (+0.66 against +0.74); the switch stays, for a small island's neighbour.

The posture, tuned (running). After the dodge, the posture is the largest part the census read off (-0.58), and it splits by map (industry -1.67, generated -2.37 off; naval +1.81). Its numbers were set by hand in LR 24 and 25 and never tuned.

  • Intention: find posture weights that score more a seat across every map, from the new defaults, by SPSA (LR 40).
  • The weights: w_posture, w_pos_defend, w_pos_economy, w_pos_fortify, w_pos_connect, w_pos_expand, w_defend_ground.
  • The run: 64 steps of 48 fresh seeds (5000 on), every map, 24 jobs, a 0.6, c 0.4 (build/spsa/20261023-220502), about 8 h, from 22:05 on 23 October.
  • The rule, set before the run: the point SPSA ends on is promoted only if, on 528 seeds none of the search used (20000 on), every map, it beats the defaults past two errors and no map reads past two errors the other way (ROADMAP goal 4). The census is queued to start when the search ends.

The result and the verdict are a later section.

The reading. The weights moved far, the posture took over the ladder, and the score a seat did not move: across every map the posture's weights are a ridge, not a peak. What they change is which maps a team does well on (atlantic and naval up, big-lizard down). One set of numbers for every map averages those away. That is LR 72's case for profiles (backlog 60): a posture fitted to a map, or read from it, could keep the gains without the losses. SPSA on one map at a time would find each map's point; a census of each against the defaults on its own map would say whether the maps differ past the noise.

75. The board's parts, and the front read off, 24 October

LR 73 read the team board off as +0.49 +/- 0.26 a seat, inside the noise but split by map. Three runs on the PC, each 400 fresh seeds, every map, 900 s, 3 respawns, a frozen binary.

The board study (build/board/20261024-070936, seeds 30000 to 30399). Each arm against the defaults, a seat:

The board carries both a cost and a gain: its dependents (bare) are worth a little, and its front read costs more than a seat. The front read is the cause of the split.

The front read confirmed (build/front/20261024-105130, seeds 40000 to 40399). Which of the three weights does it:

A fault under both runs. Their binaries came before 0828d4a: the team board folded every map into a 128-tile 16 by 16 grid, so on the seven larger maps the front it read was the wrong place. The study read a broken front read, not the front read.

The front read on the fixed board (build/front2/20261024-140954, seeds 41000 to 41399, a binary from 0828d4a, 14:09 to 16:13):

No map in any arm reads past two errors the other way (nearest: naval -0.59 +/- 0.44 in nofront).

The rule, set before the run: an arm passes past two errors over every map with no map past two errors the other way; of arms that pass, the fewest weights changed (the higher read breaks a tie) unless nofront reads past two errors above it, the error of the difference the two added in quadrature. All three pass; nopush beats nodepot on the tie; nofront less nopush is +0.55 +/- 0.26, past two errors.

The verdict: w_front, w_front_post and w_front_push are 0 by default. The front read is still in the code and still tested: the scenario an expanding tank takes the depot at the front sets w_front to its old 0.5.

The reading. Fixing the grid made the front read no better: off reads +1.13 on the fixed board against +0.88 on the broken one. The two single weights add to about nofront's gain, so neither part is the whole fault; aiming Expand and the rotation at the front draws tanks off cheap nearer depots and toward the enemy's strength. Of note: in the scenario, w_front at 1 takes no depot in 40 s, where 0 and 0.5 each take one. A depot "kept until taken or given up" may hold a tank on a pick it cannot reach in time; worth a look before the front read comes back in any form.

77. The play styles' round robin, 24 October

Backlog 60. Paul: "there is no single method that works", play styles "in a rock paper scissors like game". Four profiles as weights files (profiles/, d0c5a7a): turtle (the posture toward Defend and Fortify, a bigger castle, home sooner), raider (toward Expand, closes at even odds, picks off the wounded), expander and navy, with the defaults as a fifth. Are they ordered, or do they beat each other round a cycle on some maps? LR 74 found the posture's weights trade maps against each other, which is what a cycle by map would look like.

The rule, set before the run (build/rr/NOTES.txt): a style is better than another if its row reads past two errors above zero against it. The styles are ordered if those relations have no cycle over every map; a cycle on every map, or on any one, is what backlog 61 builds on. A style past two errors above the defaults over every map, with no map past two errors the other way, is read again on 400 fresh seeds before it becomes a default.

The run: make roundrobin, the fair census for every ordered pair, one team of the row's style against two of the column's: 20 censuses, the same 110 seeds each (60000 to 60109), every map (10 a map), 900 s, 3 respawns, 24 jobs, a frozen binary each (build/roundrobin/20261024-211723), 21:17 to 23:18, 2 h 1 min.

The matrix (the row's style less the column's, a seat; * past two errors):

defaultsexpandernavyraiderturtle
defaults.+0.70 +/- 0.30*-0.30 +/- 0.25+0.82 +/- 0.39*-2.10 +/- 0.33*
expander-1.02 +/- 0.31*.-1.22 +/- 0.35*+0.37 +/- 0.39-3.06 +/- 0.36*

The two cells of a pair are two censuses (one team of A against two of B, and the other way); wherever either is past two errors, the other reads the same way round (A over B in one, B under A in the other).

No cycle. The relations past two errors over every map: turtle over every other style; the defaults over expander and raider; navy over raider. An order, turtle first, raider and expander last. Map by map (10 seeds a map, so few relations pass: none on big-lizard, eight on generated), no map has a cycle of three, four or five either. The posture trades maps (LR 74), but no style trades them in a ring.

Turtle against the defaults, by map: europe +2.28 +/- 0.99, the generated maps +3.25 +/- 1.28, industry +2.57 +/- 1.14, island +3.66 +/- 0.93, past two errors; naval -0.30 +/- 0.95 and fidos-airstrip -0.27 +/- 1.05 the only ones below zero, inside the noise. By the rule it is read again on 400 fresh seeds before it becomes a default: build/census/20261025-075856, seeds 80000 to 80399, the rule in build/turtle/NOTES.txt: +1.40 +/- 0.18 a seat, 7.8 errors, every map above zero (naval, its lowest in the round robin, +1.58 +/- 0.58; the generated maps +3.56 +/- 0.55, island +2.44 +/- 0.50). By the rule turtle becomes the defaults.

The two changes together. Turtle was read with team_rotate on; team_rotate's own re-read (LR 76) ends with it off. The pair against the old defaults (build/newdefaults, seeds 90000 to 90399, the rule in its NOTES.txt: past two errors, no map the other way, not below turtle alone by more than two errors): +1.75 +/- 0.19, 9 errors, no map below zero; atlantic +5.03 +/- 0.32, island +2.70 +/- 0.54, europe +2.25 +/- 0.48. Both go in.

The new defaults (weights.rs): w_posture 0.5 to 0.95, w_pos_defend and w_pos_fortify 1 to 3, w_pos_expand 1 to 0.4, w_defend_ground 1 to 1.5, castle_inside 2 to 4, castle_road 1 to 2, posts_max 4 to 6, retreat_armor 35 to 50, press_odds 1.5 to 2 (and Tactics::default's), team_rotate off. profiles/turtle.weights is gone, being the defaults; profiles/classic.weights is the old defaults, kept as a style. The other profiles name what they change "from the default", so raider, expander and navy now inherit turtle's values where they name none (w_defend_ground 1.5 in each, team_rotate off). Three tests of a mechanism pinned the old values they were written for (the posture's scores, the ladder's lean, the castle's cap); the tests pass, 87. make families: as before the change, every family holds but a-raid-on-a-guarded-base (backlog 62).

The verdict: the styles are ordered, not a rock paper scissors. Holding what the base has beats going to fight, against every style here: the defaults leaned too far toward Expand, and now lean the other way. Backlog 61 (rotation between styles) has no cycle to use; a mix can only do as well as its best member here, and is shelved until a style beats turtle somewhere.

78. Read sooner: a sequential census, replayed, 24 October

Backlog 59. LR 72 noted the dodge was settled by seed 20 of 176: a census of fixed length spends as many seeds on a clear call as on a close one. Chess engines' testing frameworks stop when the reading passes a bound. The question: does a sequential test reach the fixed census's verdicts with fewer seeds, its false alarm rate held where it is? (LR 77 is kept for the profiles' round robin, still running: its notes named the number before it started.)

The tool: scripts/sequential.py DIR... reads a census's logs as pool.sh does, interleaves its jobs' seeds by round (the order in which parallel jobs finish them), and looks K = 8 times, at each eighth of the seeds, stopping at the first look past its bound. Two bounds, each with c found by simulation (200 000 paths) so that at a true difference of zero the chance of stopping at any look is P(|Z| > 2) = 0.0455, the fixed census's own false alarm rate:

TestBound at look k of 8First lookLast
O'Brien-Fleming2.117 sqrt(8 / k)5.992.12
Pocock2.5572.562.56

The rule, set before the replay: the sequential census holds if it reaches the fixed census's verdict (outside or inside two errors, and the sign) on every census replayed, or differs only where the fixed reading is within 0.1 errors of the bar, with a clear saving of seeds.

The data: LR 63 and LR 71's logs were on the Mac and are gone. In their place, every finished census on the PC, LR 72 to 76: LR 72's reading of the dodge on every map (176 seeds), the census of the SPSA point (528), the board, front and island site families (200 or 400 each), and the switches of LR 73 and LR 76 (200 each). 44 censuses, 11 304 seeds. Left out: the SPSA steps (a census each of a few seeds), the round robin under way, and LR 73's twelve switches turned on that were on already (every seed 0.00: the same brain on both sides). Two pairs repeat a census on the same seeds and binary and read the same, seed for seed (sitepoor off; board noboard and noground); they are counted as they ran.

The reading (z the fixed census's difference over its error; a star past two):

CensusSeedsFixed zO'Brien-Fleming stopsPocock stops
LR 72, the dodge on every map176+15.45*44, out22, out
LR 76 -dodge200-16.80*25, out25, out
LR 73 -dodge200-15.37*25, out25, out
front2 nofront400+5.96*150, out100, out
board nofront400+5.55*150, out50, out
LR 76 -posture200-3.90*100, out75, out
LR 76 -board200-2.72*125, out100, out
front2 nodepot400+2.49*350, out400, in
LR 73 -posture200-2.31*200, out200, in
LR 76 -priced_crossing200-2.24*175, out200, in
LR 73 -priced_crossing200-2.16*200, out200, in
LR 76 -team_rotate200+2.08*75, out50, out
LR 73 -column200-1.02100, out100, out
21 more inside two errors5 328-1.90 to +1.87every seed, inevery seed, in
10 more outside (front, front2, sitepoor, smooth, LR 73 -island_site)2 8001 500, out925, out
Every census11 3048 547 (76%)7 800 (69%)

O'Brien-Fleming agrees with the fixed census on 43 of 44 and spends 76% of the seeds. Most of the saving is the large effects: the dodge is settled by seed 25 of 200 at either bound. Pocock spends 69% but loses four of the narrow calls past two errors (its last bound is 2.56, not 2.12) as well as -column.

-column (LR 73): at 100 seeds it read -0.39 +/- 0.13 a seat, z -3.08, past the bound at that look (2.99); by 200 it was back to -0.10, -1.02. A run of bad seeds early, then regression to the mean. Of the 22 censuses inside two errors, about 1.0 would stop wrongly at this design's 4.55%; one did. The test did what it was built to do. The fixed census has the same chance of a false "out"; it pays it at the end, where the sequential one pays it early, and a single stop can't be told from a real one.

The verdict: by the rule set before the replay, not adopted: one census of 44 got a different verdict, and its fixed reading (-1.02) is far from the bar. O'Brien-Fleming would save a quarter of the seeds; the cost is that an early "out" is final, where the fixed census would have had 100 more seeds to recover. A form that keeps the saving and the rule: an early stop marks a reading for a re-read on fresh seeds (as LR 76 did for team_rotate) before any default changes on it. That is Paul's call; backlog 59 stays open with this reading.

79. The sequential census with a re-read, 25 October

LR 78's sequential census saved a quarter of the seeds, but its one false early stop (LR 73's -column) was final. The form LR 78 left open: an early stop is no verdict; the census is read again, sequentially, on fresh seeds, and is outside only if the re-read is outside the same way. A census that reaches its last look is the fixed census, read as before.

The rule, set before the build: it holds if (a) the census's seed differences can be read as it plays, matching its own lists; (b) its false alarm rate, by simulation, is at or below the fixed census's 4.55%; (c) over LR 78's 44 censuses, a re-read drawn from each census's own seeds (with replacement, 200 times) reaches the fixed verdict as often as not on every one, on fewer seeds than the fixed census.

The tool: LOOKS=8 make census. scripts/sequential.py watch follows the jobs' match lines, rebuilds each finished seed's difference, and at each look writes the reading to DIR/sequential.txt; at an early stop the jobs are stopped and a second sequential census plays SEEDS fresh seeds from SEED0 + SEEDS into DIR/reread; sequential.py verdict writes DIR/verdict.txt. Without LOOKS, make census is as it was. sequential.py twostage runs (b) and (c).

(a) Read as it plays: on four finished censuses (976 seeds), the seeds rebuilt from the match lines match each job's "a seed:" list to 0.01, the rounding of the printed scores.

(b) and the power, simulated (200 seeds a stage, 4 000 censuses a row; expected z is the fixed census's, the effect over its error):

Expected zFixed, outsideTwo stages, outsideSeeds, two stages
04.8%2.8%102%
116.6%8.8%108%
250.6%34.4%123%
2.567.7%52.9%129%
384.7%74.9%130%
497.5%96.0%117%
6100%100%85%
10100%100%55%

The false alarm rate falls to 2.8%, below the fixed census's. The cost is power: a true effect at z 2.5 is read outside 53% of the time, not 68%. And the seeds: an effect large enough to stop early but not large enough to stop at once pays for a whole second census.

(c) The 44 censuses: every fixed verdict is reached as often as not (LR 73 -column, LR 78's false stop, now 81%), the closest LR 76 -team_rotate at 52% and -priced_crossing at 68%. Seeds: 11 969 against the fixed census's 11 304, 106%. The dodge is read in 61 to 81 seeds of 176 to 200; a mid-sized effect (front nodepot, z 2.89) in 568 of 400.

The re-read's size (explored after the verdict, not a rule; 3 000 censuses a cell, outside and seeds):

Re-readz 0z 2z 3z 4z 6
a quarter2.3%, 100%21.8%, 98%38.8%, 92%53.3%, 82%84.3%, 61%
a half2.7%, 101%26.7%, 106%53.6%, 107%79.8%, 98%98.9%, 71%
whole2.4%, 102%34.8%, 122%74.0%, 129%96.2%, 118%100%, 86%
fixed census4.8%, 100%50.4%, 100%84.8%, 100%97.3%, 100%100%, 100%

No size beats the fixed census on both counts at the effects the census mostly reads (z 2 to 4): fewer seeds buys less power.

The live test (the Mac, naval.map, 120 s, 32 seeds from 900, LOOKS=4 JOBS=8; looks at 8, 16, 24 and 32 seeds). Every capability off (caps =) read +0.54, +1.15, +0.92 and +0.54 errors: no stop, the census to its end, its last reading the same as pool.sh's (+0.11 +/- 0.21). A rookie (skill = 0): z -11.80 at 8 seeds, past the first bound (4.14); the eight jobs stopped, none left; the re-read on seeds 932 to 963, z -5.54 at 8 seeds, past it again; the verdict outside, in 16 seeds of 32. Played again, it read the same to the digit. Defaults against defaults without LOOKS: 0.00 over 8 seeds, make census as it was.

The verdict: by the rule, not adopted as the default: (a) and (b) hold, (c) does not (106% of the seeds). The fixed census stays the census. LOOKS stays as an option for a screen where a large effect is expected (z past 5: a part thought to carry the brain, a broken change), where it reads in a third to a half of the seeds and keeps the false alarm rate below the fixed one. Backlog 59 is done.

82. Why atlantic: where the switches move the time, 25 October

Backlog 64. In LR 81, atlantic is the map where the board (-1.80 +/- 0.47 off), the posture (-1.90 +/- 0.47 off) and team_rotate (-4.13 +/- 0.70 on) each count most. Why there? No new run: LR 81's census (build/switches/20261025-131009) prints for every match each team's score parts and the trial team's seat-seconds on each rung. scripts/rungs.py reads them: map by map, the trial team's Expand, Turrets, Hunt and Fortify seconds and its held ground, built and bridges less the other two teams'. -forget (+0.04 +/- 0.06 off) stands for the defaults. A reading only, set before reading: what the trial team does differently on atlantic. No default changes on it.

On atlantic (54 matches a row):

RowExpand sTurrets sHunt sFortify sHeldBuiltBridges
-forget (the defaults)30967194-0.12-0.5+0.0
-board2111942997-1.33+20.2+4.6
-posture2101942997-1.35+20.0+4.6
team_rotate on11450281-2.22-1.5-0.4

Two mechanisms, not three. The board's row is the posture's to the second: the posture needs the board, so -board takes it too, and on atlantic all of the board's worth is the posture's.

Not that atlantic pays Expand more. Over every row pooled, held ground a 100 Expand seconds is +0.69 +/- 0.05 on atlantic, in the middle (island +2.08, fidos-airstrip +1.86, europe +1.51; industry and generated about 0, where Expand runs 1000 s a match and the ground is already held).

team_rotate takes atlantic's seats off the ladder. The trial team's seconds on the four rungs, the defaults against team_rotate on: atlantic 471 to 247, -48%, Expand alone 309 to 114. Elsewhere -3% to -22% (big-lizard -22%, fidos-airstrip -19%, island -16%, the rest -13% or less). Rotation pulls a seat out to push toward the objective, and on atlantic, an ocean map, that seat is away from the ladder half the match. The held ground falls with it (-2.22). team_rotate is off by default since LR 76, so this is the why of a decision already made.

The posture keeps atlantic's teams from building. Without it the trial team on atlantic builds 20 more things than the others and 4.6 more bridges (elsewhere +2.5 at most, naval), and spends Turrets seconds (67 to 194) where it spent Expand (309 to 210). It loses 1.35 held for 99 Expand seconds: more than the slope's 0.69, so the loss is not only the Expand time. On atlantic the bridges and turrets cost what they took. Elsewhere the posture moves more Expand time (generated 1114 to 595 s) for less held (-0.65), because the ground there is held either way.

What the meter cannot say: when in the match the seconds go, and what a bridge on atlantic costs in builders' time. A meter line for build time by kind would read the second.

The verdict: a reading; no default changes. Atlantic carries the three switches through two mechanisms: rotation takes its seats off the ladder, and the posture stops it spending on bridges and turrets. The first is already off; the second already pays. Backlog 64 is done.

83. The squad's time by task: a meter line, 25 October

Backlog 65. LR 82 could not say what a bridge on atlantic costs in the builders' time: the posture off builds 4.6 more bridges there, and the meter counted what was built, not how long it took. A new count: each second a seat is alive (where the kiting seconds are counted, Commander::think_released), whether its squad is out and on what task (meter::SquadTask): a Build by kind (road; bridge, a road whose site is water or a bridge on the map; wall and concrete; pillbox; mine; structure; boat), each covering the walk to the site and the work; harvest; capture; repair; lift; the walk home; out with no task. By group, the trial's apart, as the other counts. The match line:

seconds the squad out (trial): 491 of 1331 alive (road 0, bridge 16, wall 25, pillbox 56, mine 0, structure 0, boat 5, harvest 41, capture 173, repair 7, lift 11, home 157, idle 0)

Checks. The meter decides nothing. The same census at a6c71f3 and with the count (atlantic, 4 seeds from 500, 300 s, defaults on both sides) prints the same 12 match and 36 team lines, to the digit. a_squad_second_counts_by_its_task tests the group, the bridge and the line; the brain's tests, 88 passed.

A first look, not a reading (those 4 seeds, every seat): the squad is out 37% of the seconds alive. Of that, the walk home 39%, captures 28%, pillboxes 11%, walls 9%, harvest 5%, bridges 1.5%. If it holds over a census, the posture's cost on atlantic is not the bridges' own work but the walking that each job adds. The reading is LR 82's rows on atlantic with this line, its rule written first.

84. Rewards in the census score, 25 October

Backlog 66. Paul: "we want to improve Census with a reward system ... Big points for capturing all neautral pillboxes . Good points for taking enemy pillbox. Good points for doing damage to enemy pillbox. Getting a direct hit with the main tank weapon is a very good amount of points". And pillboxes "taken, defended". The census score (LR 8, ledger::score) judged the win, kills less deaths, ground held over time, works built and road laid. A pillbox counted only by being held.

The terms (ledger::Tally::hear, from the sim's events; a seat's count, a team's score the mean of its seats'):

RewardCounted fromWorth
a direct hitShellImpact of a Direct shell (its owner from ShellLaunched) on a hostile tank1.5 (about three kill, a kill 1)
pillbox damagePillboxDamaged to a hostile-owned pillbox, in pillboxes' health1.5 a whole pillbox
a neutral pillbox takenPillboxCaptured, from none3
a hostile pillbox takenPillboxCaptured, from a hostile6
a defencea kill of a hostile tank within DEFEND_R (16 tiles, twice a pillbox's reach) of a live pillbox of the killer's team1.5
every neutral pillboxthe team itself took as many neutral pillboxes as began neutral (counted, since a pillbox lifted and set down is another id)15, once

The switch: on by default; CENSUS_REWARDS=0 scores as before. The census prints brain_census: rewards on (or off) and each team line ends with the rewards' part and its counts:

team 1: score 18.21, ..., held 8.8, rewards 0.70 (direct hits 1, pillbox damage 0.0, pillboxes taken 3 neutral 0 hostile, defends 0, all neutral no)

Checks: the_rewards_count_their_events (a hostile direct hit counted, a teammate's and an indirect shell's not; damage to a neutral pillbox not; each capture; a defence; every neutral pillbox for one team, not the other; without_rewards the old score); the brain's tests, 89 passed. On atlantic (4 seeds from 500, 300 s), with CENSUS_REWARDS=0 every team line matches LR 83's to the digit; on, every term but defences and every neutral fired.

The scale: first set at a third of these (a direct hit 0.5, a pillbox's damage 0.5, neutral 1, hostile 2, a defence 0.5, every neutral 5), the rewards were 0.2 to 0.7 a seat on those seeds against a score of about 17, of which held ground is 2 x 8.7: a nudge. Paul chose "about a third" of the score, so each was tripled, the values in the table. Tripled, on the same four seeds: 10% of the score at 300 s (1.91 of 19.04), and 25% at the census's 900 s (6.80 of 27.24). The rewards are counts and grow with the match, while held ground is averaged over it. The census reads a difference, so a term moves a verdict by how much it differs between teams, not by its size alone.

What the sim does, against Paul's description (read, not changed): pillboxes shoot tanks and drones, not each other; damage does not slow their fire; at zero health a pillbox is dead (silent, capturable), not "highly damaged and still functional"; repair takes material, which comes from forest (harvest), depots or the start; a pillbox of one's own side can be towed by two tanks or lifted, dead or alive; towing through forest does not repair it. Those differences are sim changes, each a step of its own.

85. Personalities, step 1: timid, aggressive, castle, 25 October

Backlog 67. Paul: "make sure these ambitions of personalities are obvious. Such that timid or agressive would be very vissible", and personalities "to want to get tight groupings of pillboxes". Three profiles from existing weights (profiles/): timid (the posture hard toward Defend and Fortify, Expand 0.1; close in at odds of 4, mass 30 s, home at 75 armour or 8 shells), aggressive (past raider: Expand 5; close in at 0.8, never mass, home at 10 armour or 1 shell, duck only near dead, the wounded hunted, one pillbox at home), castle (castle 8 inside and 4 on the road, posts 6 apart, Fortify 5, mending 2).

The rule, set before the run (build/personalities/NOTES.txt): a personality is plain to see if its signature reads past two errors its own way, the trial team less the other two in the same match: timid, deaths and kills both below; aggressive, both above; castle, built above. The score reported, not judged.

86. The cowboy: spending weights, and the defaults spend, 25 October

Backlog 67, step 2. Paul: "coyboy tankers who always spend the expensive ammo with long shots and using all the resources as fast lauching like artillerty". Nothing in the brain weighed spending: the gun shot at its target tank, a stormed depot's guns, and nothing else; a longshot went every 30 s and only at a hostile structure; one strike drone out at a time. Four weights (Tactics, at their old behaviour by default when added): shell_boxes (a ready gun with no tank to shoot at shells the nearest hostile pillbox in the indirect gun's reach), longshot_every (30), longshot_any (with no hostile structure, a longshot at the nearest hostile pillbox, else the target tank), strikes_max (1). The meter's new spent clause counts the shots and launches asked for, by kind and group, and scripts/traits.py reads it. At the old values an atlantic census of four seeds reads the same to the digit as LR 84's.

profiles/cowboy.weights: shell_boxes 1, longshot_every 5, longshot_any 1, strikes_max 3, retreat_shells 1. Rule, set before the run (build/personalities/NOTES.txt in the worktree treadcore-lr86): plain to see if indirect shells and longshots both read above the others past two errors.

The census (110 seeds from 120000, every map, against the defaults): plain to see, and more. Indirect shells +172 a match, longshots +141, strikes +129; +11.54 +/- 0.89 a seat, ten maps past two errors above, none below. Without the rewards (score less rewards, LR 84's old score) +5.20 +/- 0.49.

Read again (the rule for a style past two errors above the defaults, LR 77; 400 fresh seeds from 130000): +11.62 +/- 0.45, every map past two errors above (fortressisles2 the least, +5.23 +/- 1.43; generated +22.82).
