The longshots carry the fighting (+3 kills, -1.2 deaths; +2.97 without the rewards). Shelling pillboxes is mostly the reward LR 84 set for pillbox damage (+5.27 of +7.19); without it +1.92, from ground held. Strikes kill more and die less. retreat_shells does nothing: the retreat thresholds blend from the constants (4 shells, LOW_ARMOR) to the weights by w_smooth, 0.05 by default, so 1 shell rounds back to 4, and retreat_armor moves the real threshold a twentieth of the way. Turtle's "home at 50 armour" (LR 77) and timid's 75 (LR 85) barely acted: part of why timid read as the defaults.

The change, by the rule: the spending weights at the cowboy's values are the defaults (retreat_shells left at 4, since it does nothing). profiles/steady.weights keeps the old spending (the defaults of LR 77 to 85) and classic.weights gains it. make families then found the four dodge families under their 99% floors (91.3% to 96.5%): with shell_boxes, a tank waiting out a shell shot at a neutral pillbox and turned its hull into the splash. A pillbox shot now waits while a hostile shell is coming down within the splash and 3 tiles of the tank: the four back to 100%, the duel family 63% to 78.5%, every family over its floor. the_default_match_plays_as_ teams lost its pings: on its one seed the pinging seat, with the cowboy's drones, happened never to call; with longshots every 30 s it pings 12 times. The test builds its teams on steady's spending, as LR 77 pinned the mechanism tests. The brain's tests, 89 passed.

The fix came after the census, so the defaults as changed were read once more against steady (the old defaults).

The pair (the rule in build/NOTES3.txt of the worktree treadcore-lr86b, set before: the new defaults stand if past two errors above zero with no map past two errors below; trial the defaults as changed, base steady.weights, 400 fresh seeds from 150000): +11.64 +/- 0.43 a seat, every map past two errors above (europe the least, +5.54 +/- 0.43; island +21.91). Kills +6.77, deaths -2.49 (-2.09 before the fix), held +1.35, pillbox damage +17.7. Without the rewards, +5.52 +/- 0.25: four times turtle's +1.40 on the old score alone.

The verdict: by the rule, the cowboy's spending is the defaults: shell_boxes 1, longshot_every 5, longshot_any 1, strikes_max 3. The brain spends what it carries. Two notes for what follows. The rewards (LR 84) pay shelling pillboxes most, and a census on the old score would have adopted it too, at half the size. The retreat weights barely act through w_smooth 0.05; a timid or aggressive personality that goes home sooner or later needs them to act, which is the next step's first change.

87. The methods so far, and the census as a learner, 25 October

Backlog 69. Paul: "lab report about these new methods and looking forward into running census and using this like machine learning." A review, no run: what the last ten sections added to the way the brain is measured, what they found about where gains come from, and a plan for running the census as a learning loop.

The instrument, as it stands

MethodSectionWhat it does
The fair paired census24, 71A seed's difference: the trial team less the baseline's, a seat, each team in the trial seat once; the mean and its error over seeds. Outside two errors is the bar.
The rule before the run24 onWhat would confirm a change is written to the run's NOTES before it starts.
Read again on fresh seeds76, 77, 86A style past two errors is read again on 400 seeds none read before before a default changes.
The sequential census78, 79LOOKS=8: stops at an O'Brien-Fleming bound, an early stop read again. An option for large effects; it costs seeds on close calls.
Families as a guard47; 80, 86make families at every default change: scenarios over many seeds with floors. It caught a front weight a family needed (LR 80) and a tank shooting itself into a splash on 4% of seeds (LR 86), neither visible to the census.
Each part off73, 76, 81The switch census: every capability removed in turn.
Taken apart86A winning profile's weights each alone: which part carries it.
The meter5 on; 83, 86Per match and group: rungs, postures, dodges, kites, the squad's time by task, shots and launches.
rungs.py, traits.py82, 85A census read by map and by behaviour: the trial team's counts less the others', paired by seed. A personality's signature.
Rewards in the score84Direct hits, pillbox damage and captures, defences. CENSUS_REWARDS=0 the old score.
Worktrees on the machine86Uncommitted code measured on the census machine without touching its tree.

Where the gains came from

ChangeKindReading, a seat
The posture's seven weights by SPSA (LR 74)continuous tuning-0.00 +/- 0.14: a ridge, not a peak
The dodge's numbers by SPSA (LR 43, 69)continuous tuning+0.88, not read again
Turtle (LR 77)a style, hand-set+1.40 +/- 0.18
team_rotate off (LR 76)a switch+0.55 +/- 0.15
The cowboy (LR 86)new behaviour, hand-set values+11.62 +/- 0.45

Tuning the weights of behaviour the brain already had found flat ground. The large gains came from behaviour the brain did not have: spending the drones and shells it carried. A learner over today's weights will polish. It will not find the next cowboy unless the behaviour exists to be weighted. Two other findings bear on any learner. A weight can be dead: the retreat weights act through w_smooth 0.05, a twentieth of the way (LR 86), and a search over them reads noise. And a score can be gamed: shelling pillboxes earned +5.27 of its +7.19 from the pillbox-damage reward set an hour earlier (LR 84, 86).

The census as machine learning

The census is a noisy, paired, black-box objective. One evaluation of a weight vector is a census of N seeds: about 7 minutes for 48 to 110 seeds and 20 to 30 minutes for 400 on the machine's 24 threads. The pieces map onto a learning loop like this:

LearningThe census
The objectiveThe paired score difference, rewards on; the old score beside it as a check on reward hacking
A training batchA step's fresh seeds, never reused
The test set400 seeds no step saw, read once, with the rule written before
ConstraintsEvery family over its floor; no map past two errors below
RegularisationFew weights a search; a change must read on every map, not one
PreregistrationThe NOTES rule

The plan, in the order it pays:

  1. Make the dead weights act. The retreat thresholds direct (or blended by their own weight, not w_smooth), so timid and aggressive can differ and a search over survival reads something. Check every weight's slope before tuning it: a one-census screen of each weight at its range's ends, the switch census for weights.
  2. A league, not a single opponent. Every learner so far climbed against today's defaults, which move. Keep fixed opponents (classic, steady, each adopted default) and score a candidate against the league: the round robin (LR 77) as the evaluation, Elo from the ledger (ledger::ratings, LR 8) as its summary. A candidate that beats today's defaults by losing to an old one is a cycle (backlog 61) and is caught.
  3. Quality-diversity over the personalities. traits.py's signature (kills, deaths, built, pillboxes taken, spending, rung seconds) is a behaviour descriptor. MAP-Elites keeps the best brain in each cell of a grid over two or three of them: the best timid, the best cowboy, the best castle. That is Paul's list of personalities, found and tuned by the census instead of set by hand, each guaranteed to be plain to see because its cell says so.
  4. Batch search on the threads. SPSA spends a census a step on two points. With 24 threads a step can read 24 candidates on fewer seeds each (CMA-ES or a batched Bayesian optimiser), then read the best few again on more seeds: the two-stage census of LR 79, used for selection, where it saves seeds.
  5. Context, then policy. Atlantic carries the posture and rotation (LR 82); the cowboy pays +22 on island and +5.5 on europe. Weights by map features (water share, depots, size) are the first step from a weight vector toward a policy. A learned policy (the ladder picked from the board's state by a trained scorer) is the far end. The census and the families stay its judge.

Guards that do not change: fresh seeds every step; a held-out reading with its rule before any default moves; make families at each default change; the old score reported beside the rewarded one; every map read, not only the pool. A learner that wins the pool by losing a map, games a reward, or breaks a family has not won.

Next (backlog 69, step by step): the retreat thresholds made to act and the weight-slope screen; then the league evaluation; then MAP-Elites over the personalities, a night on the machine.

89. The second day: why two weights never acted, every weight screened, 26 October

Backlog 69. Paul, on the morning read: "1. yes. yes." (the two arms below, and LR 88's proposal: an SPSA point promoted only after two held-out reads, SPSA bounded to half and double), then "I want you nest ideas to improve in best industry practices. Start again. We got another day. ... we have been adding more variables. Please start more analysis and converge into good values. Start on remote."

Why w_exposure and w_mine read exactly zero

Read on the Mac with a probe in Cycle::improvement, removed after.

w_exposure is tied. Tactician::stand (tactics.rs) prices each of seven points on the arc as guns * w_exposure + (|k| / 3) * w_flank, the cheapest taken. The flank term is at most w_flank, 0.3. Any w_exposure over 0.3 makes one gun dearer than any bearing, so the order is always fewest guns, then bearing: 0.5, 1 and 2 pick the same point every time and play the same match to the bit. The weight acts only under 0.3, where a tank takes one more gun to keep its bearing. The same tie makes w_flank inert anywhere under w_exposure: its half and double (0.15, 0.6) are predicted to read exactly zero.

w_mine almost never decides. An improvement takes the largest of a mend (w_mend times the most hurt pillbox's hurt, offered once a pillbox is a tenth hurt), a mine (w_mine * 0.5, 0.25) and works (w_works * 0.5). The probe priced each improvement step at half, the default and double, over 24 census games (every map, seeds from 500000):

Improvement steps, 165Count
the same pick at all three159
a mine at double, a mend otherwise6
a different pick at half0
works offered at all0

A mine is built when nothing needs mending, at any weight. A mend loses to a mine only when the most hurt pillbox is under w_mine / 2 hurt, between a tenth and a quarter at the default: rare. Works were never on offer (no free pad by the base, or not the material). Neither is a bug; both are weights whose range of action lies outside half to double.

The day (build/day/ on the census machine, started 09:21)

Rule and expectations written first in build/day/NOTES.txt. Every census: every map, 900 s, 3 respawns, 24 jobs, rewards on.

A. Arms where the three can act, 110 seeds from 170000 (the screen's): w_exposure 0.15, w_mine 2 (a mine before any mend), w_flank 1.5.

B. The screen of every remaining scalar weight, in two stages (successive halving, as in algorithm configuration: irace, Hyperband): 68 weights at half and double (a weight bounded by 1 at halfway to 1 instead; w_duck, at 1, half only), 135 censuses of 55 seeds from

  1. Any weight with an end past 1.5 errors or a gap past 1.5 is

read again on all 110. Left out: the switches (thresholded 0/1), the skill dial, long_game (it changes the score itself), assist, upgrade_vs_mend (no upgrades yet), the front weights (off since LR 75), dodge_hold (0), and the retreat thresholds (blended by w_smooth 0.05; making them act is a change to the brain).

C. Bounded SPSA (scripts/spsa.sh, BOUND=2, new): the eight largest gaps over both screens, none inert, each kept within half and double of its default; 24 steps of 48 seeds from 800000.

D. Two held-out reads of SPSA's point: 400 seeds from 900000 and 400 from 1000000.

Rules. An arm or screen end is a candidate only on a 110-seed read past two errors above the defaults with no map past two errors below; a candidate goes to Paul, and no default changes from a screen alone. SPSA's point is promoted only if both held-out reads pass that bar and make families then holds on the Mac.

Expected. w_flank's two ends exactly zero. The three arms act and read inside the noise, w_exposure 0.15 more likely below. Stage 1: about 7 of 135 ends past two errors by chance alone, more below than above (the defaults a peak, LR 88); 5 to 15 weights inert. At most three candidates after stage 2. SPSA's point within the bounds by construction; the held-out reads +0.0 to +0.5 each; promotion about one chance in five. Cost: about 13 hours.

The weight ledger

A dashboard of the weights over time, as Paul asked: the count of weights at each commit of weights.rs (68 on 18 October, 109 on 25 October), the sixteen default changes, every weight's range tried (as a default, in a census, or in the screen) as a share of today's default, the screen's two ends a weight, and every census on the census machine (70) with its two errors. Its data are files built from git and from build/census on the machine; the day's rows are added as they read. https://claude.ai/artifact/KdMP63zpmJVWgm4ChwgBpf

Next ideas from practice

What LR 87 did not already list, from algorithm configuration, A/B testing at scale, and engine testing (Stockfish's fishtest):

  1. Correct for many comparisons. A screen of 135 ends at two errors expects seven passes from chance; LR 88's SPSA point passed its held-out read at z 2.002 and then read +0.09. Read a screen with a false discovery rate (Benjamini-Hochberg at 10%) and shrink each end toward zero by its error (empirical Bayes) before ranking. The winner's curse is the reason a searched point needs its second read.
  2. SPRT for promotion. Fishtest promotes a patch by a sequential probability ratio test between two hypotheses (no gain against a gain worth having), which stops early both ways. The census's O'Brien-Fleming looks (LR 78) stop only for large effects. An SPRT with bounds 0 and +0.3 a seat would end most held-out reads well before 400 seeds.
  3. Variance reduction. The pairs already share seeds (common random numbers). Two more: stratify by map (each map's difference weighted by a fixed share, so a run's draw of maps adds no noise), and CUPED (regress the paired difference on what is known before the match: the map, the start's distance to the nearest depot, the team's neutral pillboxes in reach). Either can cut the seeds a reading needs; how much is itself a census to run.
  4. One results ledger. Every census's commit, binary hash, run.env, trial and pooled result in one table on the machine, as experiment trackers keep (MLflow, Weights & Biases), read by the weight ledger. Today the record is spread over build/ folders and NOTES files.
  5. A canary census. Each brain commit read against the last tagged defaults on a fixed 110 seeds, nightly: drift seen the day it comes, as performance tests gate a build.
  6. Screening designs. A definitive screening design reads k weights in about 2k + 1 censuses, every census moving every weight, and separates main effects from curvature (Jones and Nachtsheim 2011). Against one weight at a time it shares each census's noise across all weights, so each estimate needs fewer seeds.
  7. More machines, one census each. Paul can add CPUs. A census stays on one machine (CENSUS.md section 5); a queue of censuses can be dealt to several, each read against its own defaults, as fishtest deals games to its workers. The screen's 135 rows would split across two machines in half the time.
  8. A learned brain. LR 90.

90. A learned brain: a study, 26 October

Paul: "If we were to use Cuda, or some sort of Mac or PC based hardware acceleration. Is there a way to define this such that we start training a normal AI neural network given our current reward system. Perhaps we need some simple low effort rewards, like moving to the center of the map. and shooting ammo with hits. It needs rewards in order to find how to aim. Write a lab report about this. Perhaps we can consider training this faster on a GPU." Then: "I can slo spin up some more CPUs." A study; nothing built or run.

Yes, and the pieces are mostly here

A learning setup needstreadcore has
an environment that steps without a windowtreadcore-sim's World, headless and deterministic by seed (the census plays it)
an observationObservation (treadcore-sim): the tank, every tank in sight, shells in flight, depots, pillboxes, mines, structures, boats, nearest forest and pads, the map's size
actionsCommand: Move { to }, FireDirect { at }, FireIndirect { at }, Launch, Deploy, Stop, Respawn, and the rest
rewardsledger::Tally::hear counts direct hits, pillbox damage and captures, defences, kills and deaths from the sim's events, tick by tick (LR 84)
opponentsthe scripted brain at any weights, the personalities, the skill dial (LR 67)
a judgethe census and make families, unchanged

What is missing: a bridge from the Rust sim to a trainer, the observation turned into a fixed array, the actions into a fixed set, and a Brain that runs a trained network.

Where the time goes: the CPU, not the GPU

The census machine plays about 625 ticks a second a thread with sixteen seats, scripted brains thinking included (110 seeds, three matches a seed, 18000 ticks a match, in 6.6 minutes on 24 threads). At a decision every 4 ticks (5 a second), 24 threads give about 60,000 seat decisions a second, roughly 200 million an hour if every seat learns.

A network for this game is small: a few hundred inputs, two layers of 256, a few outputs. Its forward pass is microseconds on a CPU. The training update (PPO over a batch of a few hundred thousand decisions) is where a GPU helps: a few times faster than the CPU for a network this size. On a Mac, PyTorch runs on the Apple GPU through Metal (the mps device); on a PC with an NVIDIA card, CUDA. Either is enough. GPU simulators (Isaac Gym, Brax) are fast because the game itself runs on the GPU; that would mean writing the sim again, and is not worth it.

So the speed of training is the speed of the sim: more CPUs is the lever, and it scales nearly linearly. A GPU is a convenience for the update step. Paul's extra CPUs help both the census and training.

The rewards: dense and simple first, the census's score last

A network starting from nothing fires at random and almost never hits, so a hit reward alone teaches nothing for a long time. Paul's instinct is the standard answer: shaped rewards that pay small steps. Proposed, each a scalar (the weights rule), all per seat per decision:

RewardWorthWhy
a direct hit on a hostile tank+1the aim; from Tally::hear as LR 84 counts it
a shell fired that hits nothing-0.05ammunition has a price, so it learns when not to fire
closer to the objectivepotential-based: +0.01 times the drop in distance to the nearest neutral or hostile pillbox (or the map's centre)Paul's "move to the center"; potential-based (Ng, Harada and Russell 1999), so it cannot pay for circling and does not change what is best in the end
armour lost-0.01 a pointstay alive
a kill+3; a death -3
the LR 84 rewardsas the census scores thempillboxes, defences, every neutral
the match+10 a winthe census's own objective

The shaping terms are annealed to zero over training, so what the network ends up optimising is the census's score. LR 86 showed a reward can be gamed (shelling pillboxes earned most of a +7.19); the census with CENSUS_REWARDS=0 beside it stays the check.

Aim: learn it from the scripted brain first

The scripted brain already aims, leads, dodges and kites. Behaviour cloning (supervised learning of its command from its observation) over a few million decisions from census games gives a network that moves and shoots like today's bot in an hour or two of training. Reinforcement learning (PPO) then starts from that network, not from random. AlphaStar began this way; it saves most of the cold start, and aiming above all.

For aim itself, the action is a target from a short list (the k nearest hostile tanks and pillboxes, or none) and a lead: an offset in the target's own frame, two numbers, which is what a gunner learns. A hit is then a reward the network can find.

The observation and the actions

  • Observation, egocentric and fixed: the tank (armour, shells, mines, reload, heading, speed, afloat); the 8 nearest hostile tanks and the 4 nearest allies (offset, velocity, armour, reload); the 8 nearest pillboxes (offset, owner, health); shells landing within 4 s (offset, time to land); a 32 x 32 tile window of terrain around the tank (a small convolution); the direction to the map's centre and to the base. About 300 numbers and the window.
  • Actions, at 5 a second: a move (8 headings, or hold), a gun (target from the list, or hold) and its lead, the indirect gun the same way. Building, drones and the squad stay with the scripted brain at first: the network drives and shoots, the commander decides the rest. Later the network can pick the commander's goal.

The plan, in steps (each recorded as usual)

  1. A bridge: a Rust crate (treadcore-gym) with PyO3 bindings, a vectorised environment of many worlds a process, each seat's observation as a NumPy array, actions in, rewards out (the Gymnasium and PettingZoo interfaces). Measure decisions a second.
  2. Behaviour cloning from census games of the scripted brain. Check: the cloned brain, as a Brain in Rust (the network exported to ONNX and run by tract, pure Rust), against the defaults in a census. Expected: below, by little.
  3. PPO, against a pool of opponents: the scripted brain at the skill dial's 0.3, then 0.6, then 1, then the personalities and earlier copies of itself (self-play with a league, so it cannot win by beating only its last self). Sample Factory or CleanRL as the trainer.
  4. Judged by the census, the same rule as any change: two held-out reads past two errors above, no map past two below, the families held, the old score beside the new.

Costs. Step 1, a day of code. Step 2, an hour of games to collect and an hour to train. Step 3 at 60,000 decisions a second: 100 million decisions in about half an hour on the census machine, a billion in about five hours, if the bridge keeps the sim's pace (a network called from Python each decision may halve it; step 1 measures it). Games of this kind have needed billions of decisions to pass a hand-built bot: a day or more of the machine, less with more CPUs.

What could go wrong. A network that games a reward (the reason the old score stays beside the new). One that beats the defaults on a map or two and loses the rest (every map read). One too strong or strange for a person (the skill dial, LR 67, must still apply). And the scripted brain's strengths may be hard to pass: ninety sections of measured behaviour. The census will say.

Next: Paul to choose whether to start step 1, and on which machine.

91. The census 2.6 times faster, every match the same, 26 October

Paul: "We have CPU . but we can also install some CUDA on Copal and perhaps adjust the brain to simulate much faster. If it won't be faster, then just keep with CPU", then "nice findings and reductions. Continue to refactor. I like these speedups."

Where the time went. macOS sample on a census on the Mac (every map, 300 s): World::visible_to took 58% of the busy time. It is asked for every shell (twice), drone, tank and boat each seat's observation holds, each tick, and each time walked every tank and drone asking allied, itself two searches of the player list.

The rule for a speed-up: the same matches. The check is a census of 6 seeds from 170000, every map, 300 s, 3 respawns (18 matches), whose every team line and meter line (scores, kills, hits, dodges, spending, the squad's seconds by task, postures) must match this morning's binary to the character, the meter's own timing alone apart. Every step below passed it; make families then held with every count as this morning (the duel 314 of 400, the guarded base 326 of 400, the ready gun 836 of 1000); the sim's and brain's tests pass.

StepWhatBots' time a seat-tick
before64.5 us
the eyesWorld::eyes: a side's tanks and drones with their reach, gathered once an observation; seen_by tests a point against them26.9 us
the ringsMap::nearest_tiles: forest and water read in rings outward, stopping once no further ring can hold a nearer tile (a tile k rings out is at least k - 0.5 off); the n nearest by (distance, row order), which is the old stable sort22.5 us
the heaproute_by's open list: f as its bits (never negative, so the same order), the heap kept between searches21.9 us
sites and radarpads and banks (few on most maps) kept as a list per map state (Map::sites, keyed as the route cache is); a seat's data centres gathered once20.2 us

The test census: 125.8 s before, 48.7 s after, 2.6 times; the bots' part 3.19 times. Two tries changed nothing measurable and were taken out: regions joined in place as ground opens (702 flood fills a census, not the cost), and the danger of a Move's route marked once (the search's size, not its pricing, is the cost).

New tests: the_ring_read_finds_what_the_whole_square_did (3000 points against the old scan, every terrain, pads, banks, edges, radii to past the map, n from 0 to all, the map edited under the kept lists). Each check was itself checked by breaking the code it guards (the ring bound a tile looser; the site key without the edits): the test failed both times.

Where it goes now: route search, about 40%. 25% is the dodge's forecast (Dodge::hold_fire asks drive_path, which asks the full route to the errand's end to use its first seconds), 15% the sim's route for each Move. Neither can be made cheaper without sometimes another route of the same cost, which changes matches (a recorded replay depends on them): that is a change to play, read by a census (LR 92).

CUDA: the census machine's GPU is an RTX 4060 Ti on Alpine with NVK. CUDA needs NVIDIA's driver and glibc; Alpine has neither. And the census would not use it: its time is branching logic, routes and per-tank rules, not arithmetic over arrays. The GPU is for training a network (LR 90), from a glibc system on that card.

Deployed: the census machine moved onto this code between two of the day's censuses. Every census builds its own binary, and the matches are the same, so the day's results are unchanged; only its hours are fewer.

92. The run: a ledger, a fit, and the census until stopped, 26 October

Backlog 69. Paul: "Is there a way to write the make census write lots that give suggestions that can be picked up and used to adjsut the next generation of runs. We essentially want a method to just start computing at night time which runs concenus to converge the values", then "so night and day need to be consolidated. we just want to have a run. a way for it to choose eventually from all of the variables . doing your methods of analyzing them".

The ledger (scripts/ledger.py, make ledger): every census on a machine, one row: host, stamp, commit (new in run.env from today, a + for uncommitted source), the frozen binary's hash, seeds, first seed, seconds, map, respawns, the trial's and baseline's weights, the difference a seat, its error, z, and each map's. --merge joins machines' ledgers without pooling a census across them. On the census machine at 10:45: 87 censuses since 22 October.

The fit (scripts/suggest.py): each census against today's defaults (since their last change, 18:13 on 25 October) gives d and se for the weights it set. A weight at v is x = log2(v / default), -1 at half and +1 at double. d is fit as the sum over weights of a x + b x^2, weighted by 1 / se^2, with a ridge of one: a response surface, each weight's curve read from the screens and searches alone and in company. Each generation, 12 trials: every weight at its curve's top where that is past two errors (none yet: the defaults sit at the top, LR 88), with it less one weight; trials past 1.5 errors on 55 seeds read again on fresh seeds (successive halving); each weight's half and double not yet read alone (up to half the generation); draws from the fit's uncertainty (Thompson sampling), four weights a draw. Weights from 0 to 1 stop halfway to 1; switches, the skill dial and the score's own blend are left out. Its first fit, at 10:45: 59 censuses, 51 weights; it asked again for coming_r at half (z +1.80), column_past at half (+1.77) and bridge_soft at half (+1.60).

The run (scripts/converge.sh, make converge): generation after generation until stopped or UNTIL, fresh seeds each (SEED0 + g * 10000). Any census past two errors above with no map past two below is read at once twice more, 400 seeds each on seeds no step saw; both must pass, then make families, then Paul. Its rule is written to NOTES before it plays; it changes no default. Tested on the Mac (two seeds of 60 s, four minutes, 36 generations): the ledger, the fit, the reads, the held-out pairs and the stop all ran; its censuses were removed after.

The day folded in. The day's queue (LR 89) stopped after stage 1's half ends; its rows are in the ledger, and the run takes up the rest: the double ends, the reads again (stage 2), and the search (in place of SPSA, whose single direction the fit replaces).

Why a census is quick (Paul: "why would a simulation go so fast"). The matches are simulated, not played in real time: a 900 s match is 18000 ticks computed as fast as the CPU goes, with no window, about 6 s each on the census machine since LR 91. A census of 55 seeds is 165 matches over 24 threads; a job of one seed (55 split 24 ways) ends in 18 s. Every match still runs its 900 game seconds ("over false at 900 s" in each log).

Checked on the census machine: one of the day's rows played again on the binary before LR 91 and the one after, 4 seeds of 300 s, 12 matches: every team line the same. The speed-up is the same matches on x86 as on the Mac.

93. The dodge's forecast along the tank's own route: off, for one map, 26 October

The dodge forecasts a drive every tick a shell is coming (Dodge::hold_fire, release): drive_path asked route_kept for the route to where the tank is going, a fresh A* each time the tank crossed a tile: a quarter of a census (LR 91). dodge_own_route (thresholded at 0.5): at 1, a drive to where the tank's route already ends follows that route, its waypoint and the rest, as the world will drive it, with no search (drive_path_by). It ships at 0, against the habit since 20 October of shipping on: at 1 the defaults would move under the run (LR 92), whose table reads every census against them. At 0 every match is the same as before (the 18-match check). Every team at 1, the test census on the Mac: 47.8 s to 38.9 s, the bots 20.0 to 15.8 us a seat-tick.

94. How it works: a primer on the faster census, the route forecast and the run, 26 October

Paul: "find why big-lizard costs and fix the route search. also give me a reading link to explain biglizard. also write out your implimentation in the lab report. be detailed, and also give me introductory information and teach the needed lessns."

This section is for reading start to finish. Part A teaches the ideas the day's work rests on. Part B is the implementation, change by change, with what each one does in the code and how it was checked. Part C is big-lizard: why the forecast cost there, the fix, and the reads. Part D is the lessons. The map is drawn and explained at https://claude.ai/artifact/Mias4vtFMwmMM6Fqsd6wsa.

A. The ideas

A1. A tick, and why a match is quick. The world advances in ticks, 20 a second (TICK_HZ). A tick moves every tank and shell, fires every ready gun, and asks every bot what it wants (run_bots). A 900 s match is 18,000 ticks. Nothing waits for a clock: with no window the census computes ticks as fast as the CPU goes, so a match takes a few seconds. "Fast" means fewer CPU instructions a tick, never a shorter match.

A2. The census, and what its numbers mean. A census plays the trial team (the weights under test) against two baseline teams on the same map from the same start, three times a seed, so each team takes the trial seat once (the fair census, LR 24). Its reading is the trial team's score less the others', a seat, averaged over the seeds. The standard error (se) is how much that average would move if the census were run again on other seeds: the spread of the seeds' differences over the square root of their number. z is the reading over its se. "Past two errors" means |z| > 2: chance alone gives that about one time in twenty. Every census since LR 24 has been read this way.

A3. Determinism. A seed fixes every random draw, so a seed plays the same match every time, to the bit, on one machine with one binary. That is what makes a pairing work (both sides meet the same luck), and it is the test for a speed-up: if the new binary's matches are the same, character for character, it computes the same game, only faster. Two machines of different kinds (the Mac's ARM, the census machine's x86) round some float operations differently, so the same seed can play a different match on each (CENSUS.md section 5).

A4. A profile. A sampling profiler (macOS sample) stops the program about a thousand times a second and records which function it is in and who called it. Over 20 s that is a fair picture of where time goes. Two traps: the compiler inlines small functions into their callers, so their time shows under the caller's name; and the linker merges identical machine code, so some time shows under a merged name (<deduplicated_symbol>). The cure for both is the call tree: who called the unnamed thing.

A5. A route: A\* over tiles. A tank told to move is given a route by Map::route_by, an A\* search. A\* keeps an "open list" of tiles to look at, always taking the one with the least f = g + h: g the cost of the cheapest way found to it, h an estimate of the rest (here the straight distance times 0.6, the cheapest terrain's cost, so it never overestimates and the route found is a cheapest one). It looks at a tile's eight neighbours, prices each step (terrain cost; 1.4142 for a diagonal; the world adds danger near hostile guns and on known mines), and stops at the goal. Two routes can cost the same; which one A\* returns depends on the order it looks at tiles. That is why a faster search that looks in another order can change a route, and with it a match.

A6. A cache, and its key. A cache keeps an answer so the same question is not worked out twice. Its key must hold everything the answer depends on, or it returns a stale answer. The map's routes, pads and banks depend on its cells, which change in play (a wall built, a tree felled), so their key holds the map's identity and its count of edits (Edits): any edit makes a new key. A clone of a map gets a fresh identity, so two worlds never share an entry.

A7. The dodge's forecast. When a hostile shell is coming, the dodge asks, for each shot and move the brain wants, where the tank will be when the shell lands (drive_path): it plays the tank's own motion forward, tick by tick, along a route (turning at TANK_TURN, braking for corners, the ground's cost), and checks the path against the splash (clearance). A shot turns the hull (a tank has no turret), so it can carry the tank into the splash (hold_fire, LR 57); a move can drive it there (release, LR 53). Until LR 93 the route it played along came from a fresh search (route_kept) that leaves the guns out.

A8. A response surface. To learn from many censuses at once, each weight's effect is modelled as a curve: the reading d is the sum, over the weights a trial set, of a x + b x², where x = log2(value / default) (x = -1 at half, +1 at double). A weighted least-squares fit (each census counts by 1 / se²) finds a and b for every weight; a small ridge (a prior that a and b are near zero) keeps a weight read once from swinging wildly. The curve's top within half and double is that weight's best value, with an error of its own.

A9. Thompson sampling and successive halving. To choose the next trials the fit draws each weight's curve at random from its uncertainty and takes the drawn curve's top: weights the census knows little about get tried, weights it knows well stay near their best (Thompson 1933). Successive halving reads many trials cheaply (55 seeds) and reads again only those that look good (past 1.5 errors), on fresh seeds (Jamieson and Talwalkar 2016; Hyperband). A read past two errors is then read twice more, on 400 seeds each, before anyone believes it.

B. The implementation

B1. The eyes (World::eyes, World::seen_by; treadcore-sim). The profile: visible_to 58% of busy time. observe(p) builds what player p may see; for each shell (twice: where it is, where it lands), drone, tank and boat it asked visible_to(p, at), which walked every tank and drone asking allied(owner, p), and allied searches the player list twice (team_of). With 16 players, 50 to 100 shells in flight under the cowboy's spending (LR 86), and every seat every tick, that is millions of list searches a second. Now observe first gathers p's eyes once: each living allied tank and each allied drone with its reach in the open and into forest (Eye { pos, open, forest }); then each point is tested against that short list. The arithmetic is the same (TANK_VISION * 0.5, vision * 0.6, the same dist), so every answer is the same. visible_to and tank_visible_to keep their signatures, now built on the eyes.

B2. The rings (Map::nearest_tiles). observe asks each tick for the 8 nearest forest tiles within 20 (forest_near), the 32 nearest pads, and the 12 nearest launch sites within 16 (launch_sites). The old code read the whole square, (2r + 1)² tiles (1,681 at r = 20), kept those within the radius, and sorted them all by distance (a stable sort: equal distances in row order). The new code reads the square in rings outward from the tank's tile. A tile in ring k is at least k - 0.5 from the tank, because the tank is somewhere inside its own tile; so once n tiles are in hand and the n-th nearest is closer than k + 0.5, no tile further out can displace it, and the read stops. The n nearest are kept by (distance, row index), which is the old stable sort's order exactly, so the answer is the same list. In forest the read stops after a few rings instead of 1,681 tiles.

B3. Sites (Map::sites). Pads and banks are few on most maps, so the rings find none to stop on and read the whole square anyway. Each is now also kept as a list of every such tile on the map, built once per map state (the cache of A6), and when the list is shorter than the square the read goes through the list instead. Again the same answer: the same tiles, kept by the same order.

B4. The radar. For each drone not otherwise seen, radar_sees searched every structure for a working allied data centre in reach. Now observe gathers the side's data centres once and tests each drone against them.

B5. The heap. route_by's open list compared f values as floats. f is never negative, and for non-negative floats the order of their bit patterns, read as unsigned integers, is the order of the floats (IEEE 754), so the list now compares integers (Open(u32, usize)): the same comparisons, the same routes, a little cheaper. The heap's memory is kept between searches. 3%.

B6. Two that did not pay. Regions (which tiles can reach which) were relabelled over the whole map whenever a tile changed whether it can be driven: 702 times in the test census. Joining regions in place as ground opens was exact (a test against a fresh labelling) but saved nothing measurable. Marking the danger of a move's route once instead of testing every gun at every tile was exact and saved nothing: the search's size, not its pricing, is its cost. Both were taken out. A profile says where time goes; only a timing says whether a change saves it.

B7. The checks. (1) Identity: a census of 6 seeds from 170000, every map, 300 s (18 matches) compared with this morning's binary line by line, the timer apart. (2) The same on the census machine: one of the day's rows on the old and new binaries, 12 matches. (3) make families: every count the same. (4) Tests that compare the new code with the old scan over 3,000 random points, every terrain, the map edited under the cached lists. (5) Each test broken on purpose (the ring bound one tile looser; the cache key without the edits) to see it fail: a test that cannot fail checks nothing.

B8. The run. scripts/ledger.py reads every census folder into a table (A8's data). scripts/suggest.py fits the surface and writes 12 trials: the best point if a curve's top is past two errors, the best less one weight, reads again of trials past 1.5 errors on fewer than 110 seeds, each weight's half and double not yet read alone, and Thompson draws of four weights each. scripts/converge.sh loops: ledger, suggest, a census of 55 fresh seeds each, and two held-out reads of 400 for any census past two errors with no map past two below. Two faults found in its first hour, both now fixed and both general lessons: ls | head -1 under set -o pipefail dies with SIGPIPE once there are enough folders that head closes the pipe before ls finishes (exit 141; fixed with sed -n 1p, which reads everything); and the run's own setting RESUME leaked into each child census, which also reads RESUME, so every census tried to resume the run's folder and failed at once (fixed by unsetting it; the run now also stops after three failures in a row instead of spinning).

B9. The own route (drive_path_by, dodge_own_route). The dodge's forecast searched a route every tick under fire: 25% of a census. The world has already planned the route the tank is driving (Tank::goal, the waypoint it is heading for, and Tank::route, the rest), and it ends where the tank is going. So when the forecast asks for a drive to exactly that end, it now plays the tank's motion along the tank's own route: no search, and the drive the world will really make, guns and mines priced in. Anywhere else (a dodge to a new point) it searches as before. The weight dodge_own_route (thresholded at 0.5, in the dodge's tune array as its ninth number) switches it; at 0 the code takes the old path exactly, so every match is the same.

C. Big-lizard

C1. The map. 256 by 256 tiles, 94% water, 60 islands. One island of 2,615 tiles holds 13 of the 16 starts, 14 of the 16 depots and 12 of the 16 pillboxes; three players start on islets and must cross by boat. Its matches mostly end in elimination (median 534 s, MAPS.md). A pillbox reaches 8 tiles, and the world prices every tile within 8.5 of a hostile one at six times its cost, so on that island nearly every route bends round a gun.

C2. What the census read. Own route everywhere, every map, 110 seeds on the census machine: -0.53 +/- 0.48, but big-lizard's 10 seeds -6.48 +/- 1.56, every one of them below. Off, by the rule.

C3. Why. A probe computed both forecasts at every ask on big-lizard and counted where they differ and where they turn the dodge's verdict (clear of the splash or not):

AsksApart > 1 tileApart > 4 tilesVerdict turned
ashore148,88714%1.8%0.6% (of 46,493 verdicts)
afloat5,54432%9.7%1.7% (of 2,036)

The forecasts part most at sea. There a boat's own route, planned when the move was given, and a fresh search from where the boat now is differ far more often than ashore (a boat's turns are slow and its routes long, and the landing is priced differently). Near guns ashore the own route is the truer one: the new test the_own_route_foresees_a_drive_round_a_gun puts a hostile pillbox two tiles beside a tank's straight way; after 3 s the own route's forecast is 0.00 tiles from the tank and the search's 7.85.

C4. How big the cost is. Big-lizard alone on the Mac, own route everywhere, 120 seeds: -0.97 +/- 0.66 (z -1.46), inside the noise. A big-lizard seed swings about +/-7 a seat (elimination either way), and ten seeds estimate their own error poorly; with twelve maps read, the worst is drawn from the tail. So the first census's -6.48 was most likely a small real cost (about -1) on top of an unlucky draw; it is not, on 120 seeds, past two errors.

C5. The fix. The own route ashore only (!me.boat); afloat the forecast searches as before. It keeps most of the saving (the test census at 1: 41.4 s against 47.8 s at 0 and 38.9 s with the own route everywhere) and removes the case where the forecasts part most.

C6. The reads (census machine, rule in build/route/NOTES2.txt first; 110 fresh seeds from 400000 each):

ReadSeedsDifference a seat
A. every map110-0.18 +/- 0.37; generated -1.83 +/- 0.87 (10 seeds), the only map past two below
B. big-lizard alone110+0.58 +/- 0.81
C. the generated maps alone (the rule's read of A's one map)110+0.51 +/- 0.43
make families at 1all nine hold (the duel 75.2% against 78.5% at 0, inside the noise)

Each passes its rule. dodge_own_route is on by default (1). The expectations held: A and B inside the noise, B nearer zero than the expected -1. The generated maps' -1.83 on 10 seeds read +0.51 on 110: lesson 7 below, in action. The census is faster for it: the test census 47.8 s at 0, 41.4 s at 1 (ashore only), every team.

The run (LR 92) reads its trials against the defaults, and the defaults moved: by CENSUS.md section 4a, a new run begins, its ledger from this change (SINCE). The rows before stay in the table for comparison.

D. Lessons

  1. Measure, then change, then measure. The profile found visible_to; the timings showed two plausible changes saved nothing. Both halves are needed.
  2. An exact speed-up is proven by identity. The same matches, to the character, on every machine that runs the census, and families with the same counts. Then no reading anywhere is in doubt.
  3. A test must be able to fail. Break the code it guards, once, and watch it fail.
  4. A cache's key is everything the answer depends on. Leave the edits out and the answer goes stale the first time a tree is felled.
  5. A truer model can still change the game. The own route is the more accurate forecast; the dodge's numbers were tuned against the old one. Accuracy is not the verdict; the census is.
  6. Few seeds on a wild map mislead. Ten big-lizard seeds read -6.48 +/- 1.56; 120 read -0.97 +/- 0.66. A map that swings needs many seeds before its error means what it says.
  7. The worst of many is not chance-free. Twelve maps give twelve draws; one past two errors is a reason to read that map again on its own.
  8. A read that looks good shrinks when read again. column_past at half read +0.39, then -0.35 on fresh seeds (the run's first reread). This is regression to the mean, and it is why the run rereads before it believes.
  9. Scripts are code. pipefail and an inherited environment variable each stopped the run once. Test a long-running script the way it will run: with many folders, and resumed.

95. The Tank Generational AI: senses, a small net, generations, in Rust, 26 October

Backlog 70. Paul: "i want to be able to start converging the neural net as well. what kind of general sensors can we give to make the smallest reasonable neural net. Can we use thsee conclusions as inputs. Can we give a sensor like interface to give only reduced knowedge so we can minimze thenumebr of inputs in the transformer. I actually dont know all the AI terminology. I want a tank that has all the actions available to it. I know we need a million generaions , but can we conceptualize this in the lab report about what data is needed. perhaps direction indicators. signal striength. we want a sensor like interface so the tank can expieience its rewards and its decision. Do do this. Can we have a good test plan with milestones to move to the next phase. First the tanks need to be able to be reward to driving towards an enemy. or driving towards bases or pillboxes. Since we have a working Brainz, we also want a parallel system so we can have train the bots. We can baby step the training to with larger goals as we develop milestones of Tank Generational AI Neural Net. Is this a plan for some library. I read earlier I can convert a box to ubuntu. i can use an 1080TI or a 4060TI. I expect to install ubuntu 26.10 I heard its LTS. I also prefer Rust Neural Nets. I really hate python."

A design, nothing built. It replaces LR 90's plan where they differ: all Rust, no Python; a sensor interface instead of raw positions; generations (evolution) first, on the CPUs we have, and gradient training on the GPU second.

A. The words, plainly

WordMeaning here
neural neta function from numbers in (the senses) to numbers out (the action's scores), built of layers; each layer multiplies by a table of weights, adds, and bends the result (max(0, x))
weights, parametersthe numbers in those tables: what training changes. A net of 100 inputs, two layers of 64, and 40 outputs has about 13,000
MLPthe plain kind of net above, layer after layer. The right size for this
transformera net for long sequences (text) or sets of things; it weighs every item against every other. More than a tank needs while its senses are a fixed list
policythe net that chooses actions
rewarda number the game gives the tank for what just happened
episodeone try: a scenario or a match, from start to end
generationin evolution: a population of nets tried and the best bred into the next
evolution strategies (ES)training by trying many small random changes of the weights and moving toward those that scored better. No gradients; every try independent: made for many CPUs
PPO, gradient trainingtraining by working out, from many decisions and their rewards, which way each weight should move. Faster per decision; wants a GPU for the arithmetic
behaviour cloningtraining a net to copy a teacher (the scripted brain) decision by decision
curriculumeasy tasks first, harder ones as each is passed
action maskactions that cannot be taken now (no shells: no shot) switched off, so the net never wastes a try on them

B. The senses: a sensor interface, reduced knowledge

The idea is Paul's: the tank does not see the world's lists of tanks and positions; it has senses, like a vehicle's instruments, each a direction and a strength. That keeps the inputs few and the same size on every map, and it is what the tank would feel.

The compass. Eight sectors round the tank, relative to its heading (ahead, ahead-right, right, ... ahead-left), so "ahead" means the same thing facing any way. In each sector, each channel reads a signal strength from 0 (nothing) to 1 (touching): the strongest thing of that kind in the sector, as 1 / (1 + d / 8) for something d tiles off (1 at 0 tiles, 0.5 at 8, 0.2 at 32). The channels:

ChannelWhat sets it
hostile tanka hostile tank in sight (its armour scales it down when hurt: a weaker signal is an easier kill)
allied tanka teammate
hostile pillboxa live hostile pillbox; strength 1 inside its reach
neutral pillboxone to take
our pillboxone to keep, mend or stand by
depot to takea neutral or hostile depot
our depotwhere shells and armour come back
shell cominga hostile shell landing in that sector within 4 s, strength by how soon
groundhow far the tank can drive that way before water or a wall (a ray), 1 at the tank, 0 at 16 tiles or more
coverforest that way, near

8 sectors by 10 channels: 80 senses. (A 16-sector compass doubles it, for finer aim later.)

The body. Armour, shells, mines, material (each 0 to 1 of full), the gun's reload (0 ready, 1 just fired), the mortar's, speed, afloat, the squad out, each drone kind ready, respawning: about 14.

The brain's conclusions, as senses. Paul asked whether the scripted brain's conclusions can be inputs. Yes: it already works out things a small net would take long to learn. Each is a few numbers:

ConclusionWhere it comes fromSenses
the team's posturecommander's five scores (LR 24, 25)5
the odds hereengage_odds reading (tanks near the fight, ours against theirs)1
the base threatenedthe team board's threat to the base1
the way to the errandthe bearing and distance to the brain's chosen goal, as a compass sector2

About 9. They are hints, and they can be taken away later (sensed as 0 in some episodes, then always) so the net learns to stand without them: "hint dropout".

Memory. A tank that loses sight of an enemy forgets it. The first nets get the last tick's senses beside this tick's (twice the inputs); a small recurrent layer (a GRU, which carries a few numbers from tick to tick) comes later if milestone 6 needs it.

In all: about 100 senses, the same on every map and every size of map. A net of two layers of 64 is about 13,000 weights: small enough to run every tick on a CPU in microseconds and to evolve by ES.

C. The actions: everything the tank can do

The net chooses five times a second, all heads at once, each from a short list (a "factored" action):

HeadChoices
driveone of the 8 sectors (a Move 6 tiles that way, routed by the world), or stop
gunhold, or fire at the strongest hostile signal in one of the 8 sectors; with a lead of left, none or right
mortarhold, or fire into one of the 8 sectors at that sector's strongest target
dronehold, or launch recon, interceptor, strike or a longshot toward the strongest target
squadhold, capture the nearest pillbox to take, build (wall, pillbox, road, mine), mend, harvest, come home
signalnone, or ping a sector for teammates

Masks switch off what cannot be done now. Early phases use only the first heads; the commander (the scripted brain) does the rest until a milestone hands it over. The net's outputs are scores, one for each choice; it takes the highest (or samples from them while it learns).

D. Rewards it can feel

Each tick the tank's reward is the sum of what it earned. In the order the milestones turn them on:

RewardWorthMilestone
closer to the target: the drop in distance to the nearest enemy, depot or pillbox+0.05 a tile, as potential (never pays for circling: Ng, Harada and Russell 1999)1
a direct hit on a hostile tank+1 (LR 84's count)3
a shell that hits nothing-0.053
armour lost-0.01 a point4
a kill, a death+3, -35
a pillbox or depot taken; pillbox damage; a defenceLR 84's worths6
the match: a win+107

The shaping terms (closer, misses) fade to nothing over training, so what the net ends up chasing is the census's own score. The census, with the old score beside it, stays the check against a reward gamed.

E. Milestones: a test plan, each gate before the next

Each phase has a scenario family (TOML in scenarios/, as the dodge's families are) and a floor, run by make families; the net moves on only when the floor holds over 1,000 seeds. The same rule as every change: the test is written before the training.

PhaseTaskPass
0. Sensesthe sensor interface, a NetBrain that drives by a net, a random net plays a censussensor tests: an enemy placed due right lights "right" at the expected strength; every match the same twice; decisions a second measured
1. Go toa lone tank on open ground; a target: an enemy, then a depot, then a pillboxwithin 3 tiles inside 60 s on 95% of seeds
2. Go roundthe same past water, walls and forest, on the prior-art maps90%
3. Aima still target, then a moving one, then one that shoots backhits per shot at least the scripted brain's on a-duel-in-the-open
4. Livethe dodge's families (a tank under one or two shells)their floors (99%)
5. Duelone against one with the scripted brain at skill 0.3, then 0.6, then 1half the duels won at each
6. Supply and takeresupply at depots, capture neutral pillboxesthe raid families' floors
7. Teamfour nets against the scripted teamsthe census: a team of nets read against the defaults, by the usual rule
8. All of itevery head, the commander's work handed over a head at a timethe census, every map, two held-out reads

F. Training: generations first, on CPUs, in Rust

Track A, evolution (now, the census machine). Paul's "Tank Generational AI": a population of nets, each a weight vector. Each generation adds small random changes to the current best weights (a population of, say, 96), plays each on the phase's scenario seeds, scores them by reward, and moves the weights toward the changes that scored better (OpenAI's evolution strategies, Salimans and others 2017). Every try is an independent episode, so the 24 threads (and any machine added) are filled; nothing needs a GPU or Python. The run (LR 92) already is a generational loop over weights; ES is the same loop over 13,000 numbers instead of 100.

Cost, estimated from LR 91's speed and to be measured in phase 0: a phase-1 episode is one tank on a small map for 60 s (1,200 ticks), so tens of thousands of episodes an hour on the census machine at the least. A generation of 96 nets on 8 seeds each, about a minute. A phase is hundreds of generations: hours, not the million generations feared. Phases 5 to 8 play full matches (about 6 s each): a generation of 96 on 4 seeds is about 2 minutes; a thousand generations is a day or two.

Track B, gradients (the GPU, later). For phases 5 to 8, if evolution slows: behaviour cloning from the scripted brain first (the senses and the brain's choice recorded from census matches, about 400 MB a million decisions), then PPO. In Rust: Burn (tracel-ai/burn, 0.21), whose CubeCL backends train on CUDA, Vulkan or the CPU, with burn-rl for building blocks. The net trained on the GPU is the same net NetBrain runs; its weights are saved to a file the census loads.

The parallel system. The census and the run go on as now. The trainer is its own program (make train PHASE=1) on the cores the run leaves, or on the GPU machine; it writes each phase's best net to nets/phaseN.bin. A NetBrain reads such a file, and the census can seat a team of them as its trial (CENSUS_NET=nets/phase7.bin), so a net is judged exactly as every weight change is.

G. As a library

Yes: three parts, each a crate or module of this workspace, in Rust.

PartWhat it is
treadcore-sensesthe sensor interface: an Observation (and the brain's conclusions) to the 100 numbers; the action heads to Commands, with masks. Usable by any learner, and testable alone
NetBrain (in treadcore-brain)a Brain whose choices come from a net: the MLP's forward pass, a few hundred lines, no framework, so the census needs nothing new
treadcore-learnthe trainer: evolution strategies over NetBrain weights (std and threads only), and later Burn for gradients, behind a feature so the rest of the workspace never builds it

H. The machine for track B

The GTX 1080 Ti is a Pascal card: NVIDIA's 580 driver series is the last to support it, and CUDA 12 the last CUDA that targets it. The RTX 4060 Ti (Ada) has neither limit: use it. Ubuntu 26.10 is an interim release with nine months of support; the long-term one is 26.04 LTS (April 2026, supported to 2031), and it is the one to install on the spare disk (LR 92's commands, with its own repository path). Track A needs none of this: it runs on the census machine as it is.

I. The first step

Phase 0, in one sitting: treadcore-senses (the compass, the body, the conclusions, tests for each), a NetBrain with a random net, and a census of a random-net team against the defaults to measure decisions a second. Its rule, before it plays: every match the same twice; the random team reads far below the defaults (it should); the senses' tests pass.

96. Phase 0 of the learned tank: the senses and a NetBrain; and dodge_horizon 2, a candidate, 26 October

Backlog 70 and 69. Paul: "yes. please. start." (phase 0, LR 95).

Phase 0

The senses (senses.rs): sense(obs, map, hints) gives 101 numbers from 0 to 1, or none for a tank that is dead.

  • The compass: 8 sectors relative to the tank's heading (sector k turns k eighths of a turn clockwise on the screen: 2 right, 4 behind, 6 left), each with 10 channels (Channel): hostile tank (weaker when hurt), allied tank, hostile pillbox (1 inside its reach), neutral pillbox, our pillbox, depot to take, our depot, shell coming (landing within 4 s, by how soon and how near), ground (a ray: 1 if the way is shut at once, 0 if open for 16 tiles), cover (forest near). A channel's value is the strongest thing of its kind in the sector, 1 / (1 + d / 8).
  • The body (12): armour, shells, fuel, reload, speed, afloat, the squad out, its material, and each drone kind's share of a full bay.
  • The hints (9): the team's five posture scores, the odds, the base's threat, the errand's sector and strength; zero when no brain gives them (phase 0's nets have none).

The net (net.rs): a multilayer perceptron in plain Rust, 101 in, two layers of 64 (max(0, x) between), 51 out: 14,068 weights. Its seed is mixed (SplitMix64) before the world's Rng, which takes 0 as 1 and starts a small seed weakly: before that, seeds 0 and 1 made the same net (found by a probe of eight seeds). Glorot-uniform weights, 0 biases. save/load: a magic, the sizes, the weights, little-endian. flat/with_flat: every weight as one vector, for a trainer.

The heads (Head, HEADS): drive (hold, 8 sectors, stop), gun (hold, 8 sectors), lead (where it is, where it will be, between), mortar (hold, 8 sectors), drone (5), squad (7), signal (9). Each head takes its highest score among the choices allowed now (choose); hold always is. NetBrain decides five times a second: drive gives a Move 6 tiles toward the sector, renewed every 2 s (a Move costs the world a route), or Stop; gun fires at the strongest hostile tank in the sector when the gun is ready and the tank in direct range, aimed by the lead head (the brain's lead); mortar fires at the strongest hostile tank or pillbox in the sector within the mortar's range. Drone, squad and signal are scored and held until their phases (LR 95, E).

The census seats a team of nets in the trial seat with CENSUS_NET (a net's file, or seed:N for a random net); run.env records it as NET, the ledger as the trial net=..., and the run's fit leaves such rows out.

Checks. Tests: a tank due right is felt in the right sector at its strength and nowhere else, at 8 headings (and fails with the compass turned the other way: checked); the body as shares, nothing for a dead tank; the ground ray finds a wall 4 tiles ahead; a net is its seed and its file; a masked choice is never taken; random nets drive (7 of 8 seeds; one prefers to hold), each its own way, a seed the same match twice. The census without CENSUS_NET is the same as before to the character (HEAD against phase 0, 18 matches); a census with a random net is the same twice. On the Mac: 7.8 us a decision (the senses and the forward pass, one tank in a world of sixteen), 128,000 a second a thread; a random team -8.18 +/- 0.78 over 6 seeds of 300 s: it does land direct hits (the gun's mask lets it fire only at a tank in range), and loses.

The census of record (build/net0/, rule first): a random team (seed 1) against the defaults, 110 seeds from 500000, every map. -19.30 +/- 0.36, every map past two errors below (from -14.54 on fortressislesx to -24.64 on atlantic): further below than the Mac's 300 s test, as full matches give the scripted teams longer to pull away. It ran to its end in 4 min 55 s. The rule's third part, a census no slower than the scripted brain's, cannot be read from it: it shared the machine with the run, and its timing with it. The decision's own cost (7.8 us on the Mac, against about 20 us a seat-tick for the scripted brain) says the net's seat is the cheaper. Phase 0 passes on the first two parts and on that measure; phase 1 (go to a target) is next.

dodge_horizon 2: a candidate for Paul

The run's fit and the day's screen read it high three times on 55 seeds (+2.71, +2.62, +2.12). By the rule Paul chose this morning (LR 88 proposal), two held-out reads on today's defaults (dodge_own_route on), 400 seeds each, rule in build/horizon/NOTES.txt first:

ReadEvery mapLowest map
from 3500000+1.82 +/- 0.27 (z 6.7)constanine -0.32 +/- 0.60
from 3600000+1.66 +/- 0.29 (z 5.7)fidos-airstrip -0.63 +/- 1.07

Both past two errors above, no map past two below. make families at dodge_horizon 2: all nine hold (a standing tank under one or two shells 999 of 1000, floor 990). A candidate: the default (4 s) changes only with Paul. Expected about +1 and one chance in two of passing both: it read higher and passed both.

What it means: the dodge starts out from under a shell 2 s before it lands instead of 4. A tank that starts later spends less of the fight running from shells that would have missed or that it could outrun at the last moment, and more of it shooting. The families say it still gets out from under nearly every shell.

The run found the same weight on its own: its fit's best point at generation 2, from every census since the new defaults, is dodge_horizon 2 alone. Read on 55 seeds at 12:28 (+1.61 +/- 0.76, past two errors), it went to its own two held-out reads, under way as this is written.

97. The learned tank drives and shoots; periscope senses and calls to friends; dodge_horizon 2 the default, 26 October

Backlog 70 and 69. Paul: "write a lab report. and lest go to the next . teach to network tank to drive and shoot, and enemy a depot a pillbox." Then, mid-step: "can you create documentation and show the html about the new design of the sensors. add more sensors if approporate. give tank like sensors. like pariscope tank controls. almost like a submarine ... Also give general request commands to the training to it can cally friends through shared requests queues ... Accept the new defaults." The plan as a whole is written out in AI-BRAINZ-PLAN.md.

A. dodge_horizon 2, the default

The run (LR 92) on the census machine found dodge_horizon 2 as its best in generations 2, 3, 4, 8 and 9 and read it held out, 400 fresh seeds each, ten times:

GenerationHeld-out reads
2+2.66 +/- 0.30, +1.71 +/- 0.29
3+1.61 +/- 0.27, +1.44 +/- 0.29
4+1.09 +/- 0.26, +1.98 +/- 0.27
8+2.44 +/- 0.30, +1.84 +/- 0.30
9+2.00 +/- 0.30, +1.73 +/- 0.28

Every read past two errors with no map past two below, beside LR 96's two (+1.82, +1.66). Three more trials passed (gen 3 samples 1 and 4, gen 8 sample 0), each dodge_horizon 2 with Thompson draws of other weights riding on it; only the common part is taken. make families holds at 2 (a-duel-in-the-open 76.2%, a-raid-on-a-guarded-base 81.5%, a-tank-under-a-ready-gun 83.6%, the rest 99.9 to 100%). B. The senses, second edition: instruments, a periscope, calls

Paul's "tank like sensors ... pariscope ... almost like a submarine". Three blocks added to senses.rs, 101 senses to 133:

  • A Call channel in each compass sector (11 channels): a friend's call from that way, its strength fading over the call's 10 s.
  • The instruments (Nav, 6): x and y as shares of the map, the heading's sine and cosine (each shifted to 0 to 1), the nearest edge's strength, a hit just taken (1 as it lands, 0 after 2 s, from last_hit_tick). A tank's GPS, gyro compass and hull-hit light.
  • The periscope (Contact, 18): the compass says which eighth; the periscope gives the exact bearing (sine, right +; cosine, ahead +) and strength of four contacts: the nearest hostile tank (with its closing speed, 0.5 still and 1 closing at top speed; its aspect, 1 pointing at the tank; and whether it is in the direct gun's reach), the nearest thing to take, the nearest depot of ours, and a friend's newest call (with its kind, one of three). A submarine's plot: bearing, range, closing, aspect.

Each is a few comparisons over lists the observation holds already; none is a raw list or a picture, so the count is the same on every map and the cost stays in microseconds.

Tests: an enemy dead right reads sine 1, cosine 0, its strength, closing 1 driving at the tank, aspect 1 pointing at it and 0 turned away; the instruments place the tank; a friend's call north of an east-facing tank lights the left sector's Call channel at its strength and the call contact as a Take.

C. Calls to friends: a shared queue

calls.rs: a team's Calls, one queue a match shared by its learned tanks (as the team board is shared by Commanders). A call is a kind (help: come to me; attack: the nearest enemy; take: the nearest thing to take), a place and a tick; it lives 10 s; the queue keeps 16; a tank calls at most every 2 s. The Signal head is now hold or one of the three kinds (it was hold or a sector), masked to kinds with something to point at. Each call is also said in the world as a Command::Ping, so scripted teammates converge on it as they do on one another's; a teammate's ping heard in the world (Event::Pinged) comes into the queue as an attack call. Brains think in seat order on one thread, so the queue reads the same in every replay. The census gives its net team one queue a match. Test: a call is heard by the others and not its caller, for its life and no longer; the queue keeps the newest. Nothing yet rewards calling (AI-BRAINZ-PLAN.md section 8, upgrade 5).

D. Training: evolution strategies in Rust

learn.rs and make train. A ground: the seed's generated map with a square of grass 49 tiles across round its centre, the tank at the centre facing a way the seed draws, the target 10 to 20 tiles off (12 to 20 to shoot) in a direction the seed draws: an enemy tank standing still, a neutral depot or a neutral pillbox, in turn by the seed; nothing else. The enemy is felt at any range (the scenario's advantage): a tank sees 10 tiles, and phase 1 is going to, not searching. An episode: 60 s at most, five decisions a second.

Evolution strategies (Salimans and others 2017): each generation draws 48 directions in weight space (normal, from the generation's seed), plays the weights 0.05 along and 0.05 back along each on the same 12 grounds (97 nets with the weights themselves, 1,164 episodes), ranks every score from -0.5 to 0.5 (one lucky score cannot pull the weights alone), estimates the gradient as the rank differences times the directions, and takes an Adam step (0.02; decay 0.0005). Threads take episodes from a shared counter. Tests: a ground is open and its target is where it says; a straight drive reaches each kind inside 30 s and a tank that holds never does; an enemy fired at from inside the gun's reach is hit; one generation from a seed moves the weights the same way twice.

Two faults on the way: a world with one seat that the trainer ran past its end (a guard on over now); the trainer's files written under the crate's folder (cargo test runs there; make train gives absolute paths).

E. Phase 1: go to. The rule, then the reads

Rule (LR 95, E; build/train/NOTES.txt on the Mac): within 3 tiles inside 60 s on at least 95% of 999 held-out grounds (seeds 9,000,000 on, a third each kind), each kind alone. On the Mac (M1, 8 threads):

NetEnemyDepotPillbox
first senses (101), 200 generations, 110 s96.4%100%94.3% +/- 1.3
first senses, 500 generations99.7%100%95.2% +/- 1.2: passes
untrained, new senses (133)13.2%1.8%0.3%
new senses, 500 generations, 130 s100%100%100%

With the periscope the net passed every training ground by generation 67 (the compass alone needed hundreds): an exact bearing is what going to a point wants. nets/phase1.bin.

F. Phase 3: drive and shoot. Three rules, in order, each before its run

  1. First rule: a hit inside 60 s on 95%. Read on the untrained net first: it passed at 99.8%, its mortar landing 7,728 shells on a tank that never moves. Not a test.
  2. Amended: the direct gun only (a direct hit counts 1; a direct miss or a lobbed shell -0.05; up to 0.2 for closing to the gun's reach), and hits a shot at least RuleBot's. Read first: RuleBot fires no shot on these grounds (a still tank that never fires is no threat to it), and a direct shell at a still tank never misses (the untrained net: 666 of 666). Hits a shot is phase 3b's measure, a moving target. The untrained net: a direct hit on 44.2%.
  3. Shoot only, from phase 1, 300 generations (83 s): a direct hit on 999 of 999, 2,995 direct shots all hits, none lobbed, 998 kills. But read on phase 1's grounds it had forgotten the going: enemy 100%, depot 9.3%, pillbox 23.7%.
  4. Amended again: drive and shoot, all three grounds in turn: the enemy shot (a direct hit), the depot and pillbox gone to. Rule: each kind 95% on the 999. From phase 1, 400 generations (98 s):
GroundHeld out
enemy: a direct hit333 of 333 (999 direct hits of 999 shots, 3 a tank, each killed; none lobbed)
depot: within 3 tiles333 of 333
pillbox: within 3 tiles333 of 333

nets/phase3.bin: one net that drives to a depot or a pillbox, and drives in on an enemy, holds the mortar and kills it with the gun.

G. The census: a team of drive-and-shoot nets

On the census machine at 7d0af54 (build/net3/NOTES.txt, before it played): a team of nets/phase3.bin in the trial seat against the defaults, 110 seeds from 500,000, every map; then a random net of the same shape on the same seeds. Rule: the trained team above the random by more than two errors. Expected: both far below the defaults (phase 3 knows nothing of supply, capture, cover or teams).

Team in the trial seatEvery mapLowest mapHighest map
nets/phase3.bin (drive and shoot)-23.52 +/- 0.38island -28.91 +/- 0.42fortressislesx -18.02 +/- 0.76
a random net, the same shape (seed:1)-20.35 +/- 0.25atlantic -23.43 +/- 0.83fortressislesx -16.99 +/- 0.57
(LR 96: a random net, the first shape)-19.30 +/- 0.36

The rule fails: the trained team reads 3.17 +/- 0.45 below the random, seven errors. What it learned on open grass, it does in a match: it drives straight at the nearest enemy, depot or pillbox. On the training grounds nothing shoots back and every pillbox is neutral; in a match the nearest pillbox is often hostile and the nearest enemy is a team of five under its own guns. The random team dithers near its start and lives longer. Skills on a clean ground do not carry into a match until the ground teaches what kills (phase 4, live; hostile pillboxes and an enemy that fires back on the grounds). The census did its job: it says the drive-and-shoot net is not yet a better tank, and by how much. Both censuses took two and a half minutes (the net teams go down fast).

Lessons

  • Read the rule on the untrained net first. Two of phase 3's rules were met or made meaningless before any training; ten minutes of baselines saved a false pass.
  • Train what must be kept. A net trained only to shoot forgot how to go to a depot in 300 generations. A curriculum rehearses.
  • The right sense is worth hundreds of generations. The periscope turned a 500-generation pass into a 67-generation one.

98. The record made part of the source; guided training and training maps planned, 26 October

Backlog 70. Paul: "ok. all these artifacts make sure they are reports in the documentation and the process of project createion is part of the MIT open source expieience of developing this. . The pages, in the repository. Three pages had been published only as shared links; each is now a report of the site (reports/), so it ships with the code under the MIT licence:

  • 2026-10-26-the-weight-ledger.html: rebuilt as a static page from the dashboard's data (the dashboard's framework is not redistributable), with the day's two default changes added (dodge_own_route 1, dodge_horizon 2): 110 weights (68 on 18 October), 18 defaults changed, the 56 screen reads, 98 censuses.

The process (PROCESS.md): who does what, how a prompt becomes a commit (with a diagram), where every conversation is kept (the prompts verbatim in the check-ins; their consequences in the decision logs and the journal), how the brain is designed by measurement (weights, families, the census, held-out reads, the run), the fifteen days, what the process caught, the daily rhythm, and the pages.

Guided training and training maps (AI-BRAINZ-PLAN.md section 9): one scenario file in scenarios/training/ a behaviour, read three ways: its [expect] and floor by make families (the scripted brain), its [reward] by make train GROUND=file (the net), its held-out seeds by both. A series of ten training maps, in curriculum order, the first two done (open ground, the gun); maps 4 to 6 (the guarded depot, under a shell, an enemy that fires back) next, as the census of LR 97 asks. Each file is to be written and read on the scripted brain and the untrained net before any training on it.

The run stays on the census machine all day on the new defaults (build/converge/20261026-181011/, generation 1 at 18:34), reading the coefficients; the morning read takes what it found.

99. Training maps from scenario files: the guarded depot learned, the duel not yet, and what a match says, 26 October

Backlog 70. Paul: "ok. and for this dev version. lets continue to develop. impliment ." The plan of LR 98 (AI-BRAINZ-PLAN.md section 9), built: one scenario file a behaviour, read by the scripted brain and by the trainer on the same seeds.

A. One file, three readings

scenario.rs:

  • a [reward] section;
  • kind = "net" brains (the net in file, else the trainer's; a team's nets share one queue of calls);
  • alive and dead checks;
  • run_net;
  • reward, which sums the file's terms for the learner's seat: pass, alive, damage_taken, damage_given, killed, depot_taken, pillbox_taken, pillbox_killed, and closer (the share of the way to a point closed, a potential).

A term the trainer does not know is refused when the file is read.

learn.rs: a Course is a built phase or a training map. A generation's episodes take the courses in turn, so a mix rehearses what was learned before (LR 97's lesson, built in).

  • make train GROUND=phase1,phase3,scenarios/training/....toml trains on a mix.
  • BASELINE=1 reads the scripted brain first, with a Commander on Auto in the net's seat.
  • CHECK=net reads a net on every course's held-out seeds.

net.rs: the gun head also takes a live hostile pillbox in direct reach when no tank is in its sector. A net could not kill a guard before.

Tests:

  • every training map reads as a course;
  • a net plays one the same way twice;
  • the scripted reading has no net seat;
  • an unknown reward term is refused;
  • the gun takes a hostile pillbox in reach, and never a neutral one, a dead one, or one out of reach.

B. Maps 4 to 6, read before training

Rules in build/train/NOTES-maps.txt (the Mac), before any training. Held-out seeds from 9,000,000:

MapScripted brainUntrained netFloor
4. the guarded depot: a hostile pillbox 3 tiles before a neutral depot, the tank 18 off; pass: the depot taken, alive, in 60 s100% (300)0%80%
5. under a shell: rolling at full speed, a shell within 2.5 tiles in 1.5 to 3.5 s; pass: not hit99.8% (1,000)79.3%99%
6. an enemy that fires back: a RuleBot 16 tiles off; pass: it destroyed, alive, in 60 s20.3% (300)0%50%

Each untrained reading is under its floor, so each map is a test. Map 6's floor is above the scripted brain's own reading, on purpose: the plan's "half the duels won".
