  • CENSUS.md sections 2, 4 (and its resume prompt) and 4a;
  • PROCESS.md sections 7 to 9;
  • CLAUDE.md.

C. Next

  1. Test orrery in fleet management mode (Paul).
  2. The user guide (Paul's request of 28 October).

D. The first promotion stopped at a test

r0009 (gen62/best) failed at the brain's tests, and nothing was committed or pushed. That is the guard working. Two findings:

1. The defaults were in two places. default_weights_are_todays_constants holds the weights! table to Tactics::default() (tactics.rs), whose numbers are written out by hand (kite_in as DIRECT_RANGE - 1.0). set-defaults.py now writes a changed weight into Tactics::default() too, where a field has its name (an integer field stays an integer).

2. A designed behaviour changes with every strong candidate. focus_fire_picks_the_wounded_tank_a_teammate_is_on places a tank among two enemies:

  • a fresh tank 6 tiles away;
  • a wounded tank (armour 40, one shot from dying) 14 tiles away, which a teammate is already shooting.

Today's defaults pick the wounded one, which is focus fire. Tried in a scratch worktree:

  • gen62/best (held out +2.54) picks the near one, p1 at 3.93: "6 out, 1 on it, in range, a threat". Its w_near is 2 and its w_in_range 1.4.
  • gen58/best-less-w_near (w_near at its default, held out +2.08) picks the near one too: w_in_range 1.4 alone tips it.
  • All 30 candidates with w_near 2 or w_in_range 1.4 (every one past +2.10 held out) do the same.

The census says shooting the near tank in range plays better, by about +2.5 a seat; the test says focus fire is the design. Which is Paul's call:

  • accept the new behaviour, and change the test to the situation in which focus fire should still win;
  • or keep focus fire as it is, and hold those two weights at their defaults (most of the gain goes).

Until he says, nothing is promoted.

117. Focus fire or the near tank: a scalar, 28 October

Paul's answer to LR 116 D: "Make it a scalar." That follows his rule: either/or is a weight that blends the two, defaulting to his preference, plus a Caps switch, and the census finds the value.

A. The weight

focus_blend (weights.rs, default 0.5) chooses between:

  • 0: the near tank in range;
  • 1: the wounded one a teammate is on.

It is on behind the Caps switch FOCUS_BLEND (bit 21, on; off holds the blend at 0.5).

In the tactician's rate() (tactics.rs), today's score is kept as it was accumulated, and to it is added

(2 · focus_blend − 1) · (team − near)

where:

  • team is the wounded term, the one-shot term, and the focus and overkill terms (a teammate on the target);
  • near is the distance term and the in-range term.

At 1 the team's terms count double and the near terms nothing; at 0 the reverse. At 0.5 the addition is skipped (lean == 0), so the default scores as before, bit for bit.

focus_fire_picks_the_wounded_tank_a_teammate_is_on now holds the two ends:

  • focus_blend 1 picks the wounded tank;
  • focus_blend 0 picks the near one;
  • both on today's weights and on the search's (w_near 2, w_in_range 1.4).

The default's choice is the census's to set.

B. The rule before the run

  1. At the default the brain plays as before: a census of the defaults on the new code matches the old code's, to the character.
  2. The fleet's search reads focus_blend at its half and double (0.25, 1.0) by itself, as it does every weight. A value becomes a candidate only by the usual rule: past two errors, both held-out reads, the families. A promotion is Paul's, by Approve.

C. (1), and a fault found on the way

The first comparison differed: per-seed scores 4.29 against 5.39. Bisecting each part of the change in a scratch worktree, every part played identically (the table line, the field and its mapping, the Caps line, the scoring), and so did a fresh build of the real tree:

old binary twice identical the real tree, a fresh target folder identical to the old binary <- (1) holds

The "new" binary of the first comparison was not the real tree.

  • The scratch promotion worktree (gen62/best's defaults written in) and the real tree were built into one CARGO_TARGET_DIR.
  • The test binary's file name is the same in both, so the scratch build overwrote it.
  • Cargo, holding a separate fingerprint for each worktree, still called the real tree's build fresh.

The same hazard is on the fleet.

  • Every census job ran scripts/census.sh, which rebuilds with cargo, in the commit's worktree, into the one ~/fleet/target a machine keeps.
  • On a machine that has built two commits there, a job for the older commit could be handed the newer commit's binary.
  • No result so far is affected: the brain's source is the same in every commit the fleet has built (8e5df88 to 49f2f3b).

Fixed:

  • CENSUS_BIN= (census.sh) copies a binary already built for the tree, with no cargo. orrery's census, held-out and judge jobs pass $C/census-bin, the binary the machine built for that commit at its prepare job. The families already used it.
  • A promotion builds into its own worktree's target/.
  • Jobs planned before this commit still rebuild. They play the old or the new binary, and at the defaults those play the same, by (1).

D. Next

The fleet builds the commit at its next top-ups. The families of its defaults run by themselves (defaults@SHORT), and the search adds focus_blend's half and double to its screen. Promotion waits for the census.

118. Auto mode: the cycle closes itself, 28 October

Paul, on reading the first full-size spsa and tune runs: "queue the held-out reads for both. can we have this system auto incorporte the reults. accept them. incorporate them, sync them ... Cadidates. Should self promote, unless we are not in AUTO mode. We should be in auto mode". And, while it was being built: "i want to see some sort of confirmation that seach confirmation candidate shows that the values propigated to all computers in lab/infleet." Before that, on the candidate flags: "I'm going to always accept them. Just make them visible, so I can see progress."

A. The two runs (r0014, r0015)

Both asked 11:02 as tests of the full-size runs, finished 13:38 on commit 92972b7 (the brain's source unchanged since). Neither clears two errors.

r0014, spsa on the dodge (8 steps of 12 seeds, naval.map). Each step is a paired difference f(+) − f(−):

k d se host dodge_horizon dodge_margin dodge_clear 1 +0.18 0.85 boburner 1.83 0.87 2.29 2 -0.16 1.33 nasser 1.71 0.93 2.14 3 +0.36 2.73 calber 1.50 1.07 2.44 4 +0.38 2.00 saskatchewan 1.33 1.21 2.15 5 -0.76 1.14 mac 1.67 0.96 1.71 6 -1.04 1.30 saskatchewan 1.25 0.72 2.29 7 -1.43 2.34 mac 1.00 0.49 1.58 8 -3.84 2.51 mac 1.65 0.82 1.25

From the defaults (2.0, 0.8, 2.5) to 1.65, 0.82, 1.25. dodge_clear stopped at its bound, half its start. The largest step is 1.5 errors. A hint that the dodge runs too far, not a finding.

r0015, tune on fifteen target, odds and stand weights (5 generations of 4 children, 2 seeds each). The best: +2.52 ± 2.47 over the defaults, 1.0 error, picked as the best of noisy reads. It moves the way every strong candidate has (LR 116 D): w_near 1.0 → 1.35, w_in_range 0.7 → 1.16, w_focus 0.5 → 0.38, w_overkill 0.6 → 0.98, w_threat 0.6 → 0.40, press_odds 2.0 → 1.64, c_boat 5.0 → 2.64.

By ROADMAP goal 4 each must beat the defaults on seeds the search never used. Both are now adopted (B) for exactly that.

B. The cycle (orrery tools/plans/treadcore.py)

  1. Adopt. A finished spsa or tune run's result (theta.weights, best.weights) becomes a trial of the queue: its census and its two held-out reads queued together, not one after the other (a search already chose it). By hand: treadcore.py Q adopt FILE WHAT.
  2. Current. Every census records its COMMIT (run.env). A candidate is current when its census and both reads ran on commits whose defaults are today's (same_defaults, number by number).
  3. Auto promotion. The strongest current candidate whose families hold is asked as a promotion run (by: auto):
    • one at a time;
    • each candidate tried once;
    • two failures in a row pause auto mode with a flag.
  4. Auto approval. A promotion whose tests and full families pass on its branch is pushed to main by the planner (a fast-forward). The commit is read again, so the same pass asks every machine to build it.
  5. Propagation. Every machine's build now brings back the defaults it compiled (defaults.toml, before only the census host's). Each approved promotion is checked machine by machine against the values it changed:
    • confirmed at SHORT;
    • building SHORT;
    • differs at SHORT: names;
    • build failed.

Confirmed on all, it is a progress record. A difference or failure is a flag.

  1. Again. Every candidate's families are read again on the new defaults. The search's history counts only censuses on them.

Auto mode is "auto" in the queue's config.json, on unless false:

  • fleet-queue.py Q auto on|off;
  • a button on the Candidates page.

With it off, promotion waits for a person, and so does Approve.

Flags and progress. A candidate, a run finished, a promotion asked, approved or confirmed is progress. Progress needs no answer and is not brought to the agent's hook. A flag asks a person:

  • a run failed;
  • a family under its floor;
  • a machine lost;
  • the breaker;
  • push refused;
  • auto mode paused;
  • a machine whose build differs.

The window. The Candidates tab counts candidates. Its page shows:

  • auto mode and its switch;
  • the last three promotions, each with every machine's state (ok, .., !!);
  • every candidate with its two held-out reads.

C. What happens when Orrery closes

The keeper is its own process: its parent is launchd, and it was started apart from the window. Closing Orrery stops none of it:

  • the jobs;
  • a sync every 30 minutes;
  • the plans and auto mode.

The window only reads status and sends its buttons' commands.

D. The first pass

At 14:14 the planner, on this code, asked r0019 to promote grove/w0034:

  • census +2.49 ± 0.94;
  • held out +2.67 ± 0.27 and +3.17 ± 0.20;
  • families hold.

It moves seven defaults: w_near 1.0 → 2.0, w_in_range 0.7 → 1.4, kite_in 9.0 → 4.5, w_pos_fortify 3.0 → 6.0, dodge_horizon 2.0 → 1.08, castle_inside 4 → 8, longshot_every 5.0 → 2.5.

Its tests and families run on a branch on the Mac. Every machine has the newest commit's build queued (9c9abc1). r0014 and r0015 are adopted at the pass after the census host's build of it brings back today's defaults.

E. Next

  • The promotion's tests (the focus-fire test, LR 117, holds both ends on the search's weights) and families decide r0019.
  • Then the propagation check reads each machine's build.

119. The first auto promotion, the rotation, and a pass that failed, 28 October

A. grove/w0034 is the defaults (r0019)

The promotion asked by auto mode at 14:14 ran on the Mac in 66 s.

  • The brain's tests: 109 passed, 0 failed. That includes the focus-fire test, which since LR 117 holds both ends of focus_blend. It stopped r0009, and w0034 moves w_near and w_in_range to double.
  • Every family held (every_family_holds_its_floor):

a-duel-in-the-open 83.5% +/- 1.9% floor 55% a-naval-start-by-a-bare-islet 100.0% floor 95% a-raid-on-a-guarded-base 81.5% +/- 1.9% floor 75% a-raid-on-the-held-ground 100.0% floor 95% a-rolling-tank-under-a-shell 99.8% +/- 0.1% floor 99% a-rolling-tank-under-two-shells 99.3% +/- 0.3% floor 99% a-standing-tank-under-a-shell 100.0% floor 99% a-standing-tank-under-two-shells 99.7% +/- 0.2% floor 99% a-tank-under-a-ready-gun 82.4% +/- 1.2% floor 80%

Auto mode pushed it to main as dc3df01. The new defaults:

WeightWasNow
w_near1.02.0
w_in_range0.71.4
kite_in94.5

Its evidence: census +2.49 ± 0.94; held out +2.67 ± 0.27 and +3.17 ± 0.20 (400 seeds each). Every machine has a build of dc3df01 queued, and the propagation check (LR 118 B 5) reads each one's defaults when it is done.

B. A pass that failed, and what it left

The pass that approved r0019 pushed, then failed writing the ticker line. It read set-defaults.py's echo line Tactics::default() kite_in: DIRECT_RANGE - 1.0 -> 4.5 as a number, which it is not.

  • Unsaved state. The planner never saved its state, so r0019 still read "running".
  • Two rotation runs. The pass had already written two (r0020, r0021). A second pass failed the same way and wrote nothing more: the rotation saw its lanes taken.
  • No harm to main: the push is a fast-forward, and pushing the same commit again does nothing.

Fixed in orrery (cf242d0):

  • changed_vars() reads only name old -> new lines. That serves the ticker, and the propagation check too, which would have read Tactics::default() as a weight every machine lacked.
  • New run files are written by keep(), with the state, so a failed pass leaves no run behind.
  • The rotation's counters were set by hand to match r0020 and r0021.

The next pass marked r0019 approved.

C. The rotation

Paul: "Is there a do one of each on rotation method that is the default choice? Can we have that." Auto mode now asks one run of each kind in turn, with one run active in each of two lanes:

LaneKinds, in turn
brainspsa, tune, census of @best, parts, switches, roundrobin, record
netgauntlet, train, school

How it runs:

  • Fresh seeds. tune replayed seeds from 0 every run and spsa from
    1. Both now take seed0, from 500,000,000 a million a turn. record

    takes the next 48 seeds.

  • Measurements once per set of defaults: parts, switches, roundrobin and gauntlet.
  • Adoption: a search's result is adopted, and so is a passing census of @best.
  • Priority: rotation runs share the fleet with the standing search; a run asked by hand goes first.

fleet-queue.py Q rotation on|off turns it on or off, and so does a button on the Runs page. The first two rotation runs are r0020 (spsa) and r0021 (train).

The Runs page, for the cycle:

  • each button says what it does now: searches adopted, measurements once per set of defaults, families automatic after a promotion, promote now;
  • the runs list marks the rotation's runs (r) and auto mode's (a).

D. The keeper's gap

A keeper plans when its queue runs low or runs/ changes. A prepared promotion therefore waited behind a full queue: r0019 was approved only when a file in runs/ was made and removed by hand.

  • Now: every sync (30 min) ends by doing the same, so a finished run or prepared promotion waits at most one sync.
  • From the next keeper start: a keeper also plans every 5 minutes.
  • Not restarted now: restarting the keeper would requeue its six running jobs.

E. Next

  • Each machine's build of dc3df01, and its confirmation (the Candidates page).
  • The search goes on from the new defaults. Every candidate before it is now on older defaults, so none promotes itself until it is read again.
  • r0014 and r0015 are adopted when the census host's build brings back today's defaults. They are now read against w0034's defaults.

120. The Machines view, and whitewolf joins, 28 October

Paul: "these steps should be made into the process of orrery ... Which the Orrery then applies scripts and allows idempotent running of the config scripts. Make then difficult to press (with confirm with details), we dont wont to run the wrong scripts." His choices:

  • root is typed by him, in a Sessions tile;
  • commands run as the user (root only in a tile);
  • the Machines page first.

A. The view (orrery 4a06d8f, c5d09e9)

Machines (Cmd-3, after Media) takes a machine from a fresh install (Ubuntu 24.04 with OpenSSH and ssh-copy-id) to a fleet node.

  • Probe reads it over ssh as the user: system, CPU, memory, disk, addresses, NVIDIA card, driver, CUDA, Rust, treadcore, tmux, copal-tm, the login banner, and the power-off rule. This Mac is read locally.
  • Recipes are orrery's four setup scripts (fleet-node, cuda-node, fleet-login, poweroff). Each shows applied or not, read from what it leaves behind.
  • Apply… opens a screen with:
    • the machine and its system;
    • what the recipe does;
    • each file and its sha256;
    • the command.

Its button lights only when the machine's name is typed. The files are copied and their sums checked on the machine. The command is typed into the machine's Sessions tile, in a window of its own, where Paul types the password. orrery holds no password.

  • Join… adds the machine to the queue (caps from what it has) and to the nodes list, after the same kind of confirmation.

tools/machines.py does the work. The first read showed what was still open across the fleet:

  • calber and boburner: no power-off rule;
  • boburner: no tmux;
  • the three Alpine machines: no power-off rule.

B. whitewolf

whitewolf: an i7-6700K (8 threads), 62 GB, a GTX 1080, Ubuntu 24.04.5, two network ports (.124 used).

  • Setup. tools/fleet-node.sh (one run: build tools, Rust, treadcore, cuda-node.sh, topnotch, the power-off rule) left every recipe applied.
  • The CUDA check: compute 6.1, 20 SMs, 7.9 GB, a million-float add correct.
  • Joined: caps cpu and gpu, power-off sudo -n /usr/bin/systemctl poweroff.
  • First build of c1e00c0: started 15:10. The census binary was built (c4c9d0361ea7abe7), then the trainer.

C. Found on the way

  • cuda-node.sh stopped at its first check on a machine whose driver was not yet loaded, before tmux and copal-tm. It now tries the module, and says to reboot if the card is held.
  • join would have written sudo -n poweroff, which whitewolf's rule (/usr/bin/systemctl poweroff) refuses. The probe now records the command exactly as the rule allows it.
  • The Command-digit shortcuts moved: Training is Cmd-4, Sessions Cmd-5.

D. Next

  • Commands by OS: one machine, all, or Alpine / Ubuntu 24.04, as the user, with the same confirmation.
  • Then Media: the Ubuntu install stick and its checklist.

grove's marks at 67b289a: 1 promoted, 0 current, 36 on older defaults.

B. crucible

~/code/crucible, one CUDA file of 543 lines. Everything is on the card:

  • the arena: 64 × 64, six pillars, two tanks, a shell each;
  • the sensors: twelve rays, bearing, own state;
  • the nets: 32-32-32-3, one per individual;
  • the play: one block per individual, one thread per duel, both nets in shared memory;
  • selection and mutation: a sort, the elite kept, the top quarter bred with gaussian noise;
  • the hall of fame: 4,096 past champions, the learner's opponents.

The host reads back two numbers a generation. --save and --load carry the population and the hall, so training resumes.

C. Results

CardMachineTank-steps per secondNet arithmetic
GTX 1080 Ticalber581 M2.5 TFLOP/s
GTX 1080whitewolf462 M2.0 TFLOP/s
GTX 1070 Tiboburner419 M1.8 TFLOP/s

All three are at 22 % of FP32 peak in the net alone.

  • Registers. Limiting each thread to 128 registers doubled the speed (190 registers: 236 M on the 1080). 80 and 64 registers spill and are slower.
  • Width. H = 48 fits; H = 64 does not: two nets need 51.7 KB of shared memory, against 48 KB.
  • Memory. A run at population 2,048 uses 313 MB. Population is limited by time, not by 8 GB.
  • Power. 55 % (1080) and 66 % (1080 Ti) of the cards' limits: headroom remains (the report's VII.B).

Learning, against a scripted yardstick tank, last five reads of 4,096 duels:

RunYardstick winsVerdict
self-play against the hall only, 300 generations8.2 % ± 2.7 %
half the games against sparring bots of drawn skill, whitewolf, 400 generations21.9 % ± 3.0 %3.4 se: confirms the rule written before the run
the replicate, calber, seed 814.3 % ± 2.1 %1.8 se: short of the bar

D. treadcore's simulation in CUDA

Fits:

  • the combat core: movement, shells, rays and tile collisions;
  • the learned net.

Fits poorly:

  • the lists, events and diplomacy (8,437 lines of treadcore-sim);
  • the scripted brain's branches.

So the census stays on the CPU fleet. A GPU arena made from treadcore's combat rules could pretrain the net by the billion steps, with the census still the judge.

E. Found on the way

  • -DH=32 on the command line broke Thrust's headers. The macro is HID.
  • A zsh $h:c in an rsync target is a modifier. It made three local folders instead of copying. They were removed, and the targets are now written ${h}.

F. Next

  1. orrery's interface in treadcore's brushed metal: every tab and page, the story from a new machine to a promoted result, and projects (treadcore, crucible) in the orchestration.
  2. crucible as a run kind on gpu hosts, the three cards as islands passing champions.
  3. Then commands by OS, and Media's Ubuntu stick (from LR 120).

Else they self modify, and have a flag for please resovle this by commiting the weights and nets. The cards , when they find good values at the same time, how do we keep consistent generations with keeping data. Also battling known milestones in the bast is a good idea."

Why: calber and whitewolf won 98% and 96% of their yardstick duels and had not moved in 400 generations (boburner was still climbing, 72% to 78%). The cards were busy and the measure had saturated.

A. The ladder (crucible bfe74b2, cbf76fa; orrery 8f14f0f, edc6a45)

  • Milestones. --ladder loads past champions, oldest first. In training they are opponents (--lad, 20% of duels), so an island keeps beating the fleet's whole past, not only its hall's last few thousand. Every --yard generations the champion's score against them is written beside the yardstick's; --exam writes the final champion's score against each, 2,048 duels apiece.
  • The score is wins less losses (-1 to 1). Wins plus half the draws would give a net that never fights 0.5: in the first test a 20-generation net drew 87% and 100% of its duels with two champions.
  • Keeping data. Every segment's champion is kept (crucible/champions/, one line each in crucible/lineage.tsv: job, island, generation, yardstick, the milestone examined against, wins, draws, losses, margin in standard errors, ladder mean, and what became of it).
  • Consistent generations: the milestone rule, written before the run. A champion becomes milestone M(n+1) only when its exam was against M(n), the newest, it beat M(n) by more than two standard errors (wins less losses), and it does not lose to the ladder on balance. When two islands qualify in one pass, the larger margin becomes M(n+1); the other is kept and meets M(n+1) in its next segment. An exam against an older milestone (a segment queued before the newest was made) makes nothing. So the milestones are one line, each beating the one before, whichever card found it, and nothing a card found is lost.
  • Sent at dispatch. The ladder (at most 8: the newest four and four spread over the older) and the other islands' newest champions are slot files the keeper reads when it dispatches a segment, so a segment queued six ahead meets the ladder as it is then.
  • Tested on made-up exams: two islands past M1 at once (the larger margin, 16.2 se, became M2; the other, 6.5 se, was kept); a stale exam (kept); a net drawing everything (0 se, kept).
  • Projects draws the chain of milestones (those a segment meets outlined) and the lineage, newest first; each island's card shows its ladder score against all milestones and the newest.

B. Paul Accepts, or the promotion self-modifies (orrery 8f14f0f)

A prepared promotion (its tests and families passed on a branch):

  • Paul Accepts: auto mode on and this coordinator can push. It is pushed to main and tagged brain-RID, as before; the record says "Paul Accepts (auto mode)".
  • Self-modifies: otherwise (no push permission, or auto mode off). It goes onto the local line orrery/accepted, main with the promotions not on it. The fleet builds the local line: each build is sent plan/accepted.bundle, the line's own commits, and fetches it before its worktree. A resolve flag stays up: "please resolve this by committing the weights and nets", naming the promotions, the line's commit, and the nets not committed (files under nets/ git does not track, and the net rounds' best). fleet-queue.py $Q resolve, or the Runs page's To main, pushes the line. When main holds the line it ends; when main moves on without it, the line is replayed on main; in auto mode, when the credentials come back, it is pushed by itself.
  • Nothing waits for a person. Before, a promotion without push permission failed at once, and with auto mode off one waited for Approve. A failed accept (main's brain source moved under it) now self-modifies instead of failing.
  • Tested in a scratch repository with a bare remote: self-modified (no permission), main moved under it (replayed, its parent main), permission back (pushed, resolved, tagged).
  • The words: the Runs page's push line, its Accept and To main buttons, the Candidates page's auto mode line, fleet-train.md and CENSUS.md say Paul Accepts or self-modifies; none says "waiting for Paul".

The nets in this checkout today: nets/bc1.bin and nets/bc2.bin are not tracked; nets/map7.bin is the official net. The flag will name them until they are committed or ignored.

C. Found on the way: three CPU lanes idle

At 20:40 calber, boburner and the Mac had no treadcore work: weights queue 0. The planner counted crucible's segments, queued six ahead, against the share it gives treadcore, and the keeper running since 12:28 plans only when its queue is low (it never was: 15 crucible segments and 5 builds waiting behind whitewolf's school run) or at its half-hourly sync. Fixed: the GPU lanes' jobs take none of treadcore's share, and the share is three queued jobs a CPU machine whatever the keeper asks (a census is 15 minutes, a sync 30). At 20:42 every lane was running. This was LR 124's fault: the islands came with it.

D. The first exam, and the draw

The keeper planned at 20:30 while A was being written, and made the first finished segment, whitewolf's at generation 7050, milestone M0001 (by order, not by evidence: its line says "yardstick 0.000", written before segments without an exam fell back to their last yardstick read). The first exam, calber's champion at generation 11,550 against M0001: yardstick 99.5% won; against M0001, 2,048 draws of 2,048. The champions learned to wait for an attacker: the scripted yardstick charges in and is shot, two waiters time out. The rule made no milestone, rightly, and the ladder would have stood still.

Fix (either/or is a scalar): --draw, a drawn duel's worth in training, default -1 (a win is +2 and a loss -2, besides damage); 0 is the old behaviour. The rule before the run: within four segments an island, the exams' draws against the newest milestone fall below 50%.

127. Physical ranges, the wingman's numbers as weights, eight personas, and the escort gauntlets, 2 November

Paul: "we are on max fix mode ... we start this process by locking physical constraints into a range of values ... I want a wingman trained, such that the wingman following my moves, like an escort mission ... use the topography of local maxima to differentiate different named personas". The week's reason came first: base_r walked 16, 32, 8, 6.15 in a day and coming_r reached 221 tiles on a 128-tile map, numbers with a meaning in the world searched as preferences. RANGES now holds every distance, time and count of the brain to limits the simulator sets (the gun's reach, the map, a reload, the bay, the squad), the shares to 0 to 1, and bio_period as a decree at 60 by Paul's word of 1 November; every default lies inside, so nothing plays differently and the search stays in. The wingman's five constants (ESCORT_DISTANCE, WING_LEASH, WING_GREED, WING_THREAT, WING_AHEAD) became weights with those defaults, so a person's two wingmen can be pulled in or let roam from the U page, a persona can say, and the census can read them. Eight personas, slots 8 to 15 of the roster, each a short weights file over today's defaults named after a culture for the strength it plays (Rome the builder hill of the terrain at w_pos_economy 2.1 and posts_max 12, +2.56 +/- 0.66; Mongolia the Raider with the bold hill's w_duck 0.25; Venice the steady economist's hill; China, Shoshone, Polynesia and Assyria on styles and profiles; Japan a hypothesis), each certified by its own census and none yet read. Three escort gauntlets (G10 to G12) with a station expectation and reward, the share of a run a seat spends within a distance of another: the scripted wingman reads 29 of 30 covering the leader and 30 of 30 coming back after a shell, and holds eight tiles for 57 to 59% of a keep-station run, 0 of 30 at the seven-tenths bar first written, so the bar is a half and 57% is what a trained wingman must beat. rules::physical() gives the constants in real units at 25 m a tile and a tempo of six (a tank 60 km/h, a recon 75, an FPV 97, a full-range shell 36 s), with a test. The whole account, the goals rewritten, the lessons, the register of 380 open entries and the plan's other chapters (the scale system, the transform's shape, the zero-sum economy and the logistics of a base, play with bots and every feature an element, the testing plan, 106 references in IEEE form): reports/2026-11-02-the-plan/, from orrery's docs/book/.

Its Burn lock wants rustc 1.95 and this Mac had 1.93, so 1.95.0 was installed beside it and the road walked here on Burn's CPU backend (BC_DEVICE=flex), on a recording of four seeds of 120 s (36,743 samples; seed 9,000,003 held out). One thing to know for the watch: at three epochs the deeper net had not left the floor (drive 46.6%, the commonest label's share, against 56.2% for two layers); at twelve, the watch's count, the two stand a point apart. The card's run on the fleet's shards and the read against 64 are the first shape-watch job's.

128. Game 6: ore, the smelter and the harvester, 2 November

Paul: "continue. implement the economy." The plan's chapter 13 named four stages and built the first (game 5); this is the second, the large one: a map object, a works kind, the depot's rule, and the brain that runs it.

The rule. A map holds deposits of ore (Deposit { pos, stock }, deposit x y [stock] in a file; Map::seed_deposits gives a map that places none one for every second start, six tenths of the way from that start to the centre, on open ground, DEPOSIT_STOCK 120 each: between two bases rather than at either, and fewer than there are sides). On an ore map (World::ore_game, on unless the scenario says ore = false; treadcore-server --no-ore) DepotStock::make_from(true) passes over shells and the refinery's stop gives fuel alone: a shell exists only because a squad mined ore and a smelter made it. The squad mines a deposit with the same order it fells a tree (PillboyTask::Harvest on the deposit's tile: ORE_TICKS six seconds, ORE_HARVEST four, PILLBOY_ORE_MAX eight aboard), Event::Mined says what is left. A tank within SMELTER_RANGE three of a working smelter of its side unloads all it carries (Event::OreUnloaded); every SMELTER_MAKES_EVERY second a smelter with ore puts one shell into the nearest depot of its side within SMELTER_REACH thirty that has room (Event::Smelted), and the pillboxes draw on the depot as game 5 made them. The smelter and the harvester are StructureKinds that stand at their resource (at_resource): on open ground within SMELTER_SITE three of a deposit or HARVESTER_SITE three of forest, not on a pad, and on their own crew, not the grid (needs_power false); works_site_refused names the refusal. A harvest within HARVESTER_RANGE eight of a working harvester of the side yields HARVESTER_MATERIAL six and the tree returns in HARVESTER_REGROW_TICKS sixty seconds. Four tests in the simulator (the mine, unload and smelt chain against a depot that made nothing; the works' siting and the harvester's yield and regrowth; the map's read, write and seeding; game 5 under ore: false), 64 in all.

The brain. Two weights inside RANGES: ore_low 10 (0 to 20, the depot's stock) and ore_reach 40 tiles (0 to 128). The ore run (Commander::ore_run, Act::OreRun): a tank that is quiet, idle, aboard and not in its base cycle, when the side's nearest depot holds fewer shells than ore_low and a working smelter of the side stands within its reach of the base, drives to the nearest deposit within ore_reach, sends the squad to mine, drives to within the smelter's range and unloads; with no smelter yet, the side's nearest tank to the ore with the material builds one beside it first (Cycle::smelter_site, the open tile by the deposit nearest the base). Who runs: the base's guard (DefendBase) whenever the depot is short, which is the logistics of a base; every other tank once after each fill-up, as the base cycle is once. The cycle's improvement offers the smelter too, at w_works. A tank standing at a depot with nothing to give, its armour whole, leaves after FILL_PATIENCE ten seconds and does not come home for shells for DRY_FOR a minute (needs_home takes the tick): without this a side without ore parked at its depot for the match. The census counts ore mined and shells smelted a seat (ledger::Outcome, the composition line) and a scenario's reward may name ore and smelted.

Read. the_bots_build_a_smelter_mine_and_smelt: nine seats in three teams on the naval map for ten minutes, the fleet's own team brains: 5 smelters built, 40 ore mined, 9 unloads, 36 shells smelted, the longest wait at a dry depot 8 s. A two-seed census of 300 s on the generated island: teams mined and smelted 12 and 20; the team that did neither scored between them, so the economy is not yet the score, which is the floor the trade guard will want (ore, smelted in the composition line, read by orrery from now). An old fault came out under the new rule: the hunter had always lobbed one blind shell at a neutral pillbox its squad was about to take (shell_boxes, LR 86), and the depot's free shell replaced it before a_commander_takes_a_neutral_pillbox_without_a_shot looked; game 6 replaces nothing, so the test failed and the lob is gone: a live target keeps the gun (gun_reserved = p.alive()).

What this is not yet. Workers as stock in transit, the city's four shapes and the quartermaster (stage 3); oil and the refinery's source; trade (stage 4). The deposits are seeded by a rule rather than drawn by a map's author; maps/*.map carry none of their own. The window shows the heap, the smelter, the harvester, the ORE lamps and the smelter's ring, and says what was mined on the wire; it has no page for the side's stocks. GAME_VERSION is 6: every read before this is of the old game.

129. Game 7: oil, the workers, supply and the quartermaster, 2 November

Paul: "continue. stage 3 (workers in transit, the city's shapes, the quartermaster, oil) and stage 4 (trade); map authors' own deposits; a stocks page in the window." Stage 3, the logistics of a base, the same evening.

Oil. Well { pos, stock } as a deposit (well x y [stock], Map::seed_wells for the starts the deposits skipped, WELL_STOCK 120). The squad pumps as it mines (OIL_TICKS, OIL_HARVEST, PILLBOY_OIL_MAX, Event::Pumped); a tank within REFINERY_RANGE of a working refinery of its side unloads (OilUnloaded). A refinery with oil does what it did, the side's burn halved and a fuel stop; one without does neither, and the stop costs an oil a visit. On a map with wells (World::oil_game) a depot's fuel comes from a refinery of the side within REFINERY_REACH 30 with oil, one oil a stop, else DEPOT_FUEL_DRY ten a stop. Fuel is thus not yet zero-sum (a depot still trickles), which is a decision for Paul: the plan's table has oil for fuel and drone batteries, and the trickle is the mercy that keeps a tank from being stuck, as the dry-depot rule was for shells.

The workers. Carry { owner, kind: Shells | Supply, amount, pos, route, from, to } on the world and in the observation (allies', and any the eyes see). A smelter now makes its shells into its own store (Structure.shells) and every WORKER_EVERY ten seconds a worker leaves it with up to WORKER_LOAD five for the nearest depot of the side with room; game 5's instant pillbox supply is a worker too, counting what is already on the way. Workers walk map.route at WORKER_SPEED 1.5 over the terrain's cost, so a road is faster; step_carries delivers (capped at the depot's or pillbox's room), a hostile tank within WORKER_TAKE two takes one (the shells to the tank, WorkerTaken), a splash within SPLASH_RADIUS loses one (WorkerLost). A base with no road to its smelter is a base whose shells come slowly, and a convoy is a thing a raider can take, which stage 4 trades on.

Supply. PROPOSAL-UPGRADES 1.1 as it had it: Player.supply, a live pillbox one every HOLD_EVERY_TICKS, a depot two, a powered structure one every second hold, a kill ten, a capture five. Observation.supply is the side's (supply_of_side): a team spends as one, buyer's own first (spend_supply), the proposal's "a fleet can fund its leader" without the hand-over, because the guard who buys earns the least. One upgrade, Command::Buy { upgrade: DepotStores, at } for STORES_COST 60 beside a depot of the side: it holds STORES_STOCK_MAX thirty and makes twice a second (Depot::cap, DepotStock::make_from(_, cap)). Refusals named in the chair. The window's top strip carries the side's supply, shells, ore, oil and workers; F6 buys.

The quartermaster. city.rs: the next item a base wants, read from what stands, as far as city_plan allows (none | camp | town | works | city; works as built). A camp is the castle plan's and the cycle's. The town: a harvester beside the nearest wood within CITY_R 20. The works: the stores when the side has 60; the road to the smelter, a tile a visit (Cycle::road_to); the industrial zone taken, a structure of nobody's or a wreck within CITY_HAS 40, the power plant first because nothing else of the zone works without it; a refinery built on a pad a plant of ours covers where there is oil and none to take. The city: a data centre and a workshop. The guard is the quartermaster: defend_base runs Commander::city when nothing threatens, before it rotates; the tank drives within the squad's reach, sends the squad, holds, and asks again when the squad is back (city_run, city_ordered, the ore run's patience). The oil run is the ore run with OreRun.oil: when the side's refinery holds less than oil_low 4 and a well is within ore_reach, after the shells. Weights oil_low, city_plan, w_up_stores inside RANGES.

Read. the_bots_build_a_smelter_mine_and_smelt under game 7: 2 smelters, 28 ore mined, 24 smelted, 7 workers left and 6 arrived, 7 of the city's works built or taken, the longest wait at a dry depot 3 s; no oil yet pumped in ten minutes, because the refineries of the zone were taken late and a captured refinery wants the zone's plant first, which this commit teaches the city. A two-seed census of 300 s: supply 258 to 583 a team, ore 8 and 4 smelted for one team, oil 0. Simulator 66 tests (two new: the oil chain and the depot's fuel; the workers taken and lost, supply earned and the stores bought and refused three ways), brain 154 (the city's order). maps/industry, island and naval carry their own deposits and wells, written by the seeding rule (seed_maps.py, the scratchpad), so a map's author can move them.

Not built. Trade (stage 4). Fuel zero-sum. The city's concrete walls, gatehouse and dock (the proposal's other upgrades). A stocks page of its own: the top strip is the page for now.

130. Game 8: trade, the raider, the logistics gauntlets and the economy's senses, 2 November

Paul: "write lab report of the new economy. create a series of gauntlets to train the AI (both). to reward trains moving from oil refinery to factory, to port, to city. continue implementation." Stage 4 and the gauntlets, the last of the evening. The lab report proper is orrery's docs/economy-lab-report.md, on the site as The Zero-Sum Economy; this section is the record.

Trade. step_trade: from TRADE_FIRST twenty seconds and every TRADE_EVERY minute, each owned depot sends one convoy (Carry of CarryKind::Supply) to its nearest partner of the side, by map.route on land or, when there is no land route and both are ports (World::is_port, water within PORT_WATER two tiles), straight over the water at CONVOY_SEA_SPEED. Its load is the route's length times TRADE_RATE_OWN a quarter (a seat's own two depots) or TRADE_RATE_TEAM a half (two seats of a side); trade_arrives pays both ends. One convoy a depot a round, not a pair: the first cut paid a side by the square of its depots (16,951 supply in five minutes on the island). A convoy is a worker: lost to a splash, taken by a hostile tank within WORKER_TAKE, the supply to the taker. The embargo of the plan is not built: allies cannot hurt each other in this game, so nothing can be embargoed until a betrayal is a thing.

The raider and the cover. The scripted brain on Auto or the hunt, not resupplying: a convoy of ours within COVER_REACH 40 with a hostile tank within RAID_REACH 16 of it or six tiles of its road is covered, the tank driven to seven tenths of direct range of the hostile and the doctrine's gun left free; else, quiet, a convoy of another side within 16 is driven onto, meeting it ahead on its road (Act::Raid). Found on the way: the cover must run with the squad out (the tank that fortifies has its squad out for most of a match), and a sitter it cannot see it cannot cover.

The gauntlets. Five files in scenarios/gauntlet/, for both brains as the others are: G13 ore to the smelter (a deposit of forty eight tiles east, a smelter of the side and its empty depot eight tiles west; passed: a shell smelted within the minute); G14 oil to the refinery (a well, a refinery with its plant, dry; passed: oil unloaded); G15 the convoy comes through (two depots of the side twenty-four tiles apart on a road, a tank of another side sitting by it with a fifth of its armour, a third tank of that side far off so the match does not end; passed: a convoy arrived in two and a half minutes); G16 the raider takes the convoy (two depots of another side on a road, the tank six tiles from it; passed: a worker taken); G17 the chain to the city (a deposit, the smelter as the factory, the side's depot on the shore as the port, the side's depot inland up the road as the city; passed: shells delivered to a depot and a convoy arrived, in two minutes). A scenario file now places [[deposit]], [[well]] and [[structure]] (kind, owner, powered = true for a plant beside), road is a terrain kind, and the counts ore, smelted, oil, oil_in, delivered, convoys, taken and lost are expectations ({ seat, at_least | max }) and reward terms (learn::REWARDS, 20), so a training course can pay each leg of the chain.

The senses. Seventeen more (senses::ECON, AT_ECON, 160 in all): the nearest deposit with ore and the nearest well (sine, cosine, nearness over 64 tiles, stock as a share of 120), the side's nearest working smelter and refinery (sine, cosine, nearness), the ore and the oil aboard as shares of eight, the side's supply as a share of 200. An old net of 143 is widened with zeros as before (SENSES_BEFORE_SHELL is now the fixed 133, not "SENSES less the last two blocks", which the third block broke); a transform made for fewer inputs is widened with zero columns where the new senses are (Patch::from_bytes), so slot 1 plays as it did.

Read, ten seeds a cell.

G15 as written is therefore a check that the chain runs when the road is clear, not a decision read; it wants a raider with a brain that cannot capture a depot, which the scenario form does not yet say ([[depot]] held = true is the next line to add). The rest of the gauntlet under game 8 (ten seeds): G1 10, G2 10, G3 9, G4 10, G5 10, G6 10, G7 9, G8 3, G9 10, G10 10, G11 8, G12 10, as before within a seed. the_bots_build_a_smelter_mine_and_smelt under game 8: 3 smelters, 36 ore, 32 smelted, 63 workers left and 62 arrived, 2 stores bought, 7 city works, supply 10 to 376 a seat at ten minutes, the longest wait at a dry depot 5 s. Simulator 66 tests, brain 154; build/train/gauntlet.tsv holds the table for the console's Results → Gauntlet page.

What the fleet will read. Every census from here is game 8. The trade guard's floors (built, road, kd) stand; ore, smelted, oil, supply are on the composition line and read by orrery, not yet floors. The first thing to watch is supply's scale (hundreds a seat in ten minutes with one thing to buy) and whether the quartermaster's city costs the guard its guard.

131. The nets on the logistics gauntlets: the squad head wired, and what the search found, 2 November

Paul: "train the nets on the gauntlets." The first thing the attempt found was in the code, not the training: NetBrain scored the squad head and never acted on it (Head::Squad => false, "held until its phase"), so no net could order a mine, and the zeros of section 130 on G13, G14 and G17 were the mask's, not the nets'.

The squad head. NetBrain::squad(obs, me, map, c) gives the head's seven choices their orders, each offered only when there is one to give within SQUAD_REACH eight tiles (the squad walks at PILLBOY_SPEED, so the tank need not stand on the work, but a long walk is a long time without a squad, so the drive head has to bring the tank near): 1 capture the nearest pillbox or structure that is neutral or dead and not ours; 2 build a smelter where Cycle::smelter_site would put it, beside the nearest deposit that has no working smelter of the side within SMELTER_REACH, with the material; 3 mend the nearest damaged pillbox or structure of the side, with the material; 4 fell the nearest forest tile; 5 mine the nearest deposit, or pump the nearest well, that the squad has room for (the load unloads itself when the tank drives within range of the side's smelter or refinery, as the scripted brain's run relies on); 6 home while the squad is out. None without a squad; 1 to 5 none while it is out, since the world refuses a second order but Return. The recorder (record) now labels the squad head from the Deploy the scripted brain gave (a Harvest on a deposit or a well is 5, on wood 4), without a mask, so tools/bc still leaves the head. Test: the_squad_head_orders_what_is_in_reach.

The courses. G13, G14 and G17 pay the drive as a potential beside the counts: closer = { at = "centre", offset = [-8, 0], worth = 1 }, the share of the way from where the tank began to the works that takes the load, start to end, so the search feels the way west before the first unload pays. The pass conditions did not change; the scripted brain's 10 of 10 stands.

The training. GROUND= the five files, train as built (48 pairs, 12 episodes a generation, sigma 0.05, Adam 0.02, decay 0.0005, 8 threads; 4.3 s a generation on this Mac), 20 held-out seeds a course.

  • From bc2 (the clone): flat from generation 6, every perturbation 3.000 exactly, the net's own 3.000, passed 0.17. The clone stands still in an empty field with nothing in sight; its squad mines eight ore where it stands (a quarter each, the 2.0 of the 3.0) and the tank never drives, so the potential is zero everywhere near its weights and the search has no gradient. Stopped at 17.
  • From map7 (the evolution milestone that drives to things), 300 generations, 22 minutes: the population's mean 1.46 to 2.64, best 1.95 to 2.88. Held out: G15 20 of 20 (reward 6.25); G16 20 of 20 (2.35: it raids the convoys, where the untrained net read 7 of 10 and map7 9); G13 0 of 20, reward 0.78; G14 0 of 20, 0.83; G17 0 of 20, 4.73 (the world's four convoys and the drive). The net learned the drive west, the potential, and not the mine: the mine is one choice of one head at the one moment the deposit is within reach, before the drive carries the tank off, and a step of 0.05 on seventeen thousand weights seldom flips a head whose rows were never trained.
  • From the 300th net, 300 more at sigma 0.1: mean 2.43 to 2.75; G16's reward 3.10 (more taken), G13 0.82, G14 0.91, G17 4.88; the same zeros. A wider step did not find the mine either.

The cause, measured. A probe (squad_head_margin, ignored, NETS= a list) senses G13's start, a deposit eight tiles ahead, and prints what each net's squad head scores. The clone bc2: hold 0.44, mine 4.58; it mines. map7: hold -8.9, mine -237.6; the 300th net from it: hold -80, mine -277; the 600th: hold 12, mine -290. A head that was never trained but rode along through a thousand generations of the search has rows at the search's scale, and a margin of two hundred is not crossed by a step of a twentieth on any weight: the search was moving the rows, in the wrong direction as often as the right, and the drive it could feel.

The reset. Net::zero_head(head): one head's output rows and biases at zero, the rest untouched; its choices tie, choose takes hold, and a step of 0.05 is a choice. RESET=squad in train applies it to the start net. Test: a_zeroed_head_ties_and_leaves_the_rest. From map7 with the squad head at zero, 300 generations, 20 minutes: passed 0.25 at generation 0, 0.92 at 4, 1.00 at 20; the mean 2.34 to a high of 10.02 at 200, 7.68 at the end (the mean moves with the dozen seeds drawn a generation). Held out, every course PASS: G13 19 of 20 (reward 8.93), G14 20 of 20 (10.02), G15 20 of 20 (6.05), G16 17 of 20 (1.50), G17 20 of 20 (17.41). The one G13 miss and the three G16 misses are the seeds' geometry (the heading is "any"); G16 gave back some of what the second net had (3.10 to 1.50) for the mine, which pays more. The net is nets/logistics.bin, 160 inputs, 64, 64, the heads; the gauntlet reads it by name.

Read, ten seeds a cell, the whole gauntlet, with the clone and the new net.

132. The squad head cloned, a course on the fleet's maps, and the Quartermaster's census, 3 November

Paul: "a mask byte for the squad head so tools/bc can clone it; a course on the fleet's maps with the squad on, since a net that mines in a field of two may not among sixteen; the census to read what the trained head does to a side's ore and smelted lines." The three next lines of section 131, the same night.

The mask byte. A recording is TCBC3: the header as before, and each sample's masks are four bytes, the fourth the squad head's choices 1 to 6 as bits 0 to 5, from NetBrain::squad at the moment the window opened, as the gun's, the mortar's and the drone's come from their own target functions. tools/bc reads TCBC2 and TCBC3 alike, a file at a time (a TCBC2 sample's fourth byte is zero, so its squad head is never allowed and never trained), takes the senses from the header rather than assuming 143 (the proof of section 127 was of the old width; today's recordings are 160 wide), and trains the squad head where its mask allows, as the others. agree in the harness reads both and prints six heads. Proved as section 127 was: eight matches of 180 s recorded in 6 s (108,207 samples), trained on the Mac's CPU in 7 s, and Rust's Net::forward agrees with the trainer's held-out figures to the decimal on every head, the squad's included (drive 73.9, gun 78.1, lead 99.9, mortar 91.5, drone 95.0, squad 95.1).

What the clone's squad head learned: the floor. Forty matches of 600 s (1,708,039 samples, recorded in 106 s, trained in 311 s on the CPU), held out the last four seeds: drive 78.0% (always-9 55.8), gun 67.5 (41.5), mortar 92.5 (92.3), drone 92.2 (87.9), squad 95.8 against an always-hold floor of 96.6. The recording says why. Of 1.7 million samples the squad head's labels are hold 1,630,160, capture 1,861, build 13,053, mend 17,698, fell 7,106, mine 169, home 37,992; where it had a choice (1,497,295 samples) the offers were capture 182,461, build 13,013, mend 132,517, fell 415,734, mine 35,942, home 1,014,933. The scripted brain in a dealt match of sixteen mines a hundred and sixty-nine times in forty matches: its guard runs ore only when the depot is short, and a dealt match's depots are seldom short of shells. A cross-entropy over that learns hold, and no clone of these matches will mine; the squad head that mines is the one the courses trained (section 131). The clone with the squad head is nets/bc3.bin (styles.tsv); Scholar stays bc2 until Paul says otherwise.

Read, ten seeds a cell, the clones beside the logistics net.

A course on the fleet's maps. A scenario file may now say map = "maps/naval.map": the fleet's own map in place of one generated from the seed, with its deposits and wells as seed_maps.py wrote them, the seed still dealing the seats. Three courses in scenarios/training/, 11-the-fleets-industry, -island, -naval: nine seats in three sides, the learner at seat 0 as its base's guard, eight scripted Commanders on Auto round it sharing their team boards, four minutes; paid a quarter an ore, a half a shell smelted, the same for oil, and one for being alive at the end; passed when it mined four. An episode of nine seats and four minutes runs in about three tenths of a second, so a generation of the eight courses together (32 pairs, 16 episodes) took 17 s alone and about a minute beside the recorder. From nets/logistics.bin, with the five gauntlets in the same course so they are not forgotten, 28 generations before it was stopped for this record: the five gauntlets held (at generation 20: G13 10 of 10, G14 10, G15 10, G16 7, G17 10), and the fleet's maps 0 of 10 each, the same as the start net: on industry and island the reward is 0.00, the learner dead at the end of every seed; on naval 1.00, alive and idle on its island. A trace on industry says how it dies: it drives from its start into the ring of a neutral pillbox (the map's) and is shelled for 15 three times in the first fourteen seconds, then a hostile tank finds it at 24 s. The scripted brain's first act on these maps is to take that pillbox with its squad. The net has the capture choice now and no reason yet to use it; the course pays only the ore. Paul's premise holds in the reading: a net that mines in a field of two does not among sixteen, and the thing between them is not the mine but the first minute of a dealt match, which is what the phase courses and the gauntlets G5 to G9 are about and the nets read 0 to 6 on.

The census. Style 16, the Quartermaster, is nets/logistics.bin in styles.rs, so a census trial of bot_style = 16 reads it among sixteen seats on the fleet's maps. Asked of the fleet at 03:06 (fleet-queue.py grove new census trial="bot_style = 16" name=quartermaster seeds=400 adopt=no, run r0155); it was running on saskatchewan by 03:15. Its lines, ore, smelted, oil and supply on the composition line beside kills and built, are what section 130 asked for; they will stand on the console's Results → Personas page beside the eight personas when the four hundred seeds are in. Nothing is read from it in this section; it is the fleet's to finish.

What is next. The first minute: a course on the fleet's maps that pays leaving a pillbox's ring and taking it (pillbox_taken, damage_taken) before the ore, from the phase nets; a longer run than 28 generations on the Mac, or the fleet's train kind on a machine with the hours; weighting the rare labels in tools/bc if a clone is ever to mine, or recording matches where the guard is short (ore_low high) so the scripted brain mines on the record.

The trained squad head does among sixteen what it learned in the field of two: a team of Quartermasters mines more ore than a team of the rules and pumps oil where the rules pump none (the rules' oil run waits on a refinery of the side in reach, which they seldom have in ten minutes; the net pumps when a well is within eight tiles). It smelts a tenth of what the rules smelt, because a team of nets builds nothing (0.49 a match against 23.9): no smelter stands for its ore, and the squad head's build choice is offered only beside a deposit with the material and no smelter of the side within thirty, which among sixteen it reaches seldom. The ore is mined and carried and not delivered; the ore line is up and the smelted line is down. It takes a pillbox and a worker as often as the rules do, and dies more (16.4 for 13.3) and kills a quarter as much, which is the −31. The Results → Personas page carries the slot.

The guard kept short: not the lever. RECORD_TRIAL="ore_low = 20" gives every scripted brain of a recorded match dials over the defaults (fleet::with_settings round fleet::brain; a bad name panics, roles = 2 changes the record, so the dials reach the brains). Forty matches so recorded are byte for byte the forty of section 132: 1,708,039 samples, 169 mines. ore_reach = 128 beside it, the same. The guard of a dealt match is short already (a depot on an ore map starts with no shells and ore_low is 10); what keeps it from the ore is the smelter, which wants four material and a tank of the side with it beside a deposit within reach of the base, and in ten minutes a side builds about one. No dial of the ore run puts more mines on a record of dealt matches.

The courses on the record. RECORD_COURSE=g13,g14,g17 (files, in turn, a seed each) records the scripted brain on a course's learner seat as a match is recorded: a Recorder brain round the Commander the file would deal (course_brain: the file's goal, the defaults), opening a window every DECIDE_EVERY with open_window and closing it with write_sample, the two halves of the match recorder lifted out so both use one. Three hundred runs of the three in 3 s: 119,700 samples, 1,023 mines and 663 builds of a smelter (G17 has none to begin with), mine offered 22,148 times (the squad is out or the tank is driving for most of a run), so the scripted brain mines in one offered window in twenty-two. Six times the mines of forty matches, in a fourteenth of the samples.

The rare labels weighted. RARE=cap in tools/bc: a sample with label l of head h counts the head's commonest label's count over l's, no more than cap, in the loss (label_weights, into valid); RARE_HEADS=squad for some heads only. On the forty matches at RARE=20 over every head: drive 73.1 (78.0 without), gun 76.4, mortar 83.4 (92.5), drone 66.4 (92.2), squad 88.5 (95.8); every frequent-label head agrees less, as it must when a shot counts twenty. On the gauntlet it is the better brain all the same: G1 10 of 10 (bc3 8), G2 10 (8), G3 10 (8), G8 10 (0), G16 4 (0), and G13, G14, G17 still 0: a weight of twenty on a hundred and sixty-nine mines is not a policy either.

The matches and the courses together. tools/bc on the directory of both (1,827,739 samples; held out the matches' last four seeds and the courses' last thirty): on the matches drive 77.9, gun 86.2, mortar 92.5, drone 92.3, squad 96.2 (floor 96.6); on the courses drive 96.7 (floor 61.7), drone 99.7, squad 98.8 (floor 98.8). Rust's forward pass agrees to the decimal on the courses' range. With RARE=20 RARE_HEADS=squad the squad head reads 93.1 on the matches and 94.9 on the courses, the rest within a point. The squad head trained apart. TRAIN_HEADS=squad in tools/bc: only the named heads in the loss, from a START clone. From bc3 on the forty matches and the thousand enemy-free runs (bc7): the squad head reads 96.2 and 99.5 (floor 98.8), and the drive head, out of the loss, fell from 78.0 to 34.7 on the matches and to 18.2 on the courses. The hidden layers are shared: training one head moves what every head reads, and the others, out of the loss, drift wherever the squad's gradient takes the layers. On the gauntlet bc7 fires (G1 10, G2 10) and mines nowhere, and G4 fell to 0. A head trained apart wants the hidden layers frozen, which the trainer does not do; the fix is a flag on the layers, not another recording.

The reading of the four clones of the day. bc3 (matches) fights and holds; bc5 (matches and the squad courses) mines and loses the fight; bc6 (with an enemy in the courses) fights and holds; bc7 (the squad apart) fires and drifts. The clone's road to the mine is a frozen trunk or a separate net for the squad head; neither is built, and neither is needed while the reward-trained net plays. bc6 and bc7 are not shipped; the data files are under build/bc/ on this Mac. The question of section 134 was whether a Quartermaster whose side builds the smelter delivers what it mines: it does not. With the leader and the guard of the rules on its team the smelter stands (built 10.5 a match, where a team of nets built 0.5), the team's ore is what a team of the rules mines (4.0 for 4.3), and the team's smelted line is two thirds of the rules' (2.25 for 3.42). The seat the Quartermaster took was one of the rules' ore runners (every tank of the rules runs ore once after each fill-up), and in its place the net mines about as much and delivers 1.2 shells less a match: the ore it carries does not come to the smelter, which is what r0155 said at a larger scale (ore up, smelted a tenth) and the field of two does not say (G13 ten of ten). The oil line is the one thing the seat adds, 0.58 a match where the rules pump 0.01, and nothing uses it: the refinery of a ten-minute match seldom stands. The rest of the seat's cost is the fight: three kills fewer and two deaths more a match than a rules seat, and three builds and two road tiles fewer, which is the −5. So one Quartermaster costs a team five points a seat where sixteen cost thirty-one, and the saving is the rules' leader and guard, not anything the net does better in company.

Two notes on the read. The rules' own lines moved between r0155 and r0159 (ore 6.0 to 4.3, smelted 5.1 to 3.4, score 51.2 to 47.5 for the default teams), with no change to the rules between the two commits that was meant to move them; the two runs are compared within themselves above, and the drift is a thing to watch across the next censuses of game 8. And the trial team's lines in a bot_mix census are the team's, so a seat's share is read as a difference; a per-seat ore and smelted line in the census log would make the next such read direct.

What is next. The first-minute course wants its reward reshaped (the damage paid less, alive paid as a potential, or a shorter horizon ending at the pillbox) and a pillbox placed within reach on naval, before another train run. The Quartermaster's squad head wants the delivery, not the mine: a course where the smelter is the side's and far, with the ore aboard at the start, so the drive to the works is what the reward pays; and the census log a per-seat ore and smelted. nets/first-minute.bin is shipped as the better gauntlet net; style 16 stays the logistics net until a net reads better among sixteen.

137. The three follow-ups of 136: the first minute reshaped, the delivery course, and the census seat by seat, 3 November

Paul handed back the follow-up list of section 136 as the next job: "reshape the first-minute reward and put a pillbox within reach on naval before another train run; a delivery course for the squad head: the smelter far and the side's own, ore aboard at the start; a per-seat ore and smelted line in the census log, so a mixed-team read is direct." The fourth line, the rules' drift between r0155 and r0159, is a thing to watch, not to build.

The first minute reshaped. The three courses 12-the-first-minute-* pay alive_time one (the share of the ninety seconds the tank lived, a potential the search feels every tick, where alive paid once at the end) and take back half a hundredth a point of damage (two hundredths in section 134, which outweighed the pillbox and the armour and made the reward of a passed run negative, −1.6). Held out, ten seeds, the logistics net: industry 7 of 10 at reward 0.58 (was −1.61), island 7 at 0.58 (was −1.51); the same shares as before, the reward now the right sign, so a run that takes the pillbox and lives longer is paid more than one that does not. The scripted brain on the same courses: industry 0 of 10, island 7 of 10. The scripted Commander on defend_base at seat 0 does not take industry's pillbox in ninety seconds at all; the net does, seven times in ten.

Naval's pillbox. Naval's starts are at sea on landing craft, and the [[pillbox]] placer walked from the start toward the centre asking for a land route from the anchor at every step, which a boat has to nowhere: "no open ground toward the centre" at every distance tried. The placer now skips the route when the anchor is afloat (afloat: a boat stands where the anchor is) and takes the first open ground toward the centre, where the craft lands; the course places one neutral pillbox fourteen tiles south of seat 0's start, on the island with the neutral depot. It stands and nobody takes it: the logistics net 0 of 10 at reward −1.05 (shelled on the way in), the scripted brain 0 of 10 at 1.00 in ninety seconds and at 0.41 in a hundred and eighty (it lands, is hit, and takes nothing). The course is honest now and no brain passes it, so it stays out of the train run below until the landing is a thing a brain does; the naval map's first minute is a landing, not a drive out of a ring.

The delivery course. scenarios/training/14-the-delivery: G13's shape with no deposit, the tank starting with eight ore aboard (ore = 8 on a [[tank]], new) and the side's smelter far to the west with its depot beside it, empty, two minutes; passed when a shell is smelted; the drive paid as the closer potential, the unload by ore_in (new: the ore the seat unloaded, from OreUnloaded, an expectation and a reward term), the shell last. The distance was found by reading three: with the smelter at twenty-four tiles the logistics net delivers 10 of 10 (reward 19.0), at forty 6 of 10 (6.55), at sixty 0 of 10 (0.48); the scripted brain 10 of 10 at every distance (about 20). The course is set at sixty. The net learned the drive to a works eight tiles off (G13) and does not carry it past forty: the senses hold the side's nearest working smelter as a bearing and a nearness, and at sixty tiles either the nearness has gone to its floor or the works is outside what the seat observes, and the net has no run in its training that began that far away. This is the gap r0155 and r0159 read among sixteen: in a dealt match the smelter the leader builds stands where the base is and the net mines where the deposit is, and the way between is the forty tiles the net does not drive. The course is the lever.

The census seat by seat. fleet.rs prints, after the trial team's line and only when a style is on the trial, a line for each of its seats: seat N (<style> | rules): score, kills, deaths, built, ore, ore in, smelted, oil, supply, with styled computed once from the same bot_mix rule the seating uses (the rule itself is hoisted out of the seating closure to serve both). ore in is the ledger's new ore_in; smelted stays the smelter owner's, as the ledger credits it, which is why a seat's delivery is its ore in and not its smelted. The lines begin with seat, so orrery's readers of team (\d) lines (tools/terrain.py, the fortify report) read past them.

Asked of the fleet. Three runs, after this commit was pushed so the machines build it: r0163, train first-minute-2, the five logistics gauntlets and the industry and island first-minute courses from nets/first-minute.bin, 400 generations; r0164, train delivery, the five gauntlets and the delivery course from nets/logistics.bin, 300 generations; and r0165, the census of one Quartermaster beside two of the rules again (quartermaster-one-seats, 400 seeds) for its seat lines, which say directly what r0159 said as a difference. Nothing is read from them here.

138. The Duelist with world senses: crucible's world champion in the game as the Gladiator, and the bridge that reads each new one, 4 November

Paul, to the second of three steps orrery offered that morning ("Champions enter the line: a GPU stage on the Pipeline, the world champion bridged into treadcore, read like a candidate"): "yes do step 2, the duelist with world senses." Since the evening of 3 November crucible's islands train under the game's rules (orrery's WORLD build: the tank's speed, turn and braking, the gun of ten tiles at forty a second for 45 every four seconds, 24 shells, eyes of ten tiles, four neutral pillboxes of range 8; 55 inputs, two hidden layers of 48, 5,236 weights). The bridge of section 126, style 7, reads only a hybrid of the old toy arena: 35 inputs, rays to forty tiles, the toy's yardstick. A world champion fed those 35 would hear a world it did not train in.

What a world champion is given (styles.rs, world_senses, as crucible.cu's sense under WORLD builds them). The thirty-two of the arena as before, but that a tank further than TANK_VISION is seen on no ray (its bearing and armour still are, as crucible gives them), the rays meeting a tank at crucible's radius of one tile; the yardstick at the world's range (the turn is the bearing as a heading error, it stands off at eight, it fires when aimed within the gun's ten); then the twenty of the world: twelve rays to a live pillbox that would fire on this tank (a neutral one or an enemy's; a side's own is nobody's threat), the nearest such pillbox as a sine and a cosine of its bearing, its reach, its reload and health as shares, and 1 when it has the tank (in PILLBOX_RANGE, a clear line on the map), and the tank's own shells and reload as shares. The incoming shell is the nearest hostile direct shell where it is along its flight now, as crucible's is. What the game has and the arena has not is left as the bridge always left it: the rules keep everything but the fight, the gate is the champion's, and among many enemies the duel is with the nearest within forty tiles.

How it drives. As crucible's world driver: the turn output names a heading error (-1 to 1 is a half turn either way) and the throttle has no reverse, so a throttle at or under a twentieth holds the tank (Stop) where the hybrid would back off. A Move goes STRIDE six tiles along the heading at full throttle, as style 7's. The gun stays the rules' (section 126: the champion's fire output means "my hull is on him", which a turret does not need).

Slot 17, the Gladiator. A slot once given keeps its meaning, so style 7 keeps the hybrid it has always played and the world champion is a new style, Gladiator, "the rules, with a champion of crucible's world islands in every fight". The roster is 18; bot_style's choices name it. Champion reads either size by its length and refuses a plain build's (no gate, 3 outputs: it would take every fight). nets/gladiator.bin is the world ladder's newest milestone that morning, M0015, calber's champion at generation 241,300 (nets/styles.tsv).

The rule before the run (written before either census began): the Gladiator is better than the Duelist if, on the same 48 seeds, its read against the defaults is higher than the Duelist's by more than two standard errors of the paired difference; otherwise the world senses have not shown they help.

Read. Two pairs on this Mac (eight jobs, 900 s, respawns 3), the trial team all Gladiator or all Duelist against the defaults, the pairs seed by seed (a seed: lists, the same jobs). The first pair asked for every map and played the generated one only: BSD paste on a Mac drops the map list, the slip orrery's census jobs shim since 1 November and a census by hand here did not. Both of the pair played the same, so the pair stands; the second pair plays every map.

The bridge waits until this commit is on main.

Not done. The client's tests key_setup_shows_the_pick_and_the_wait and the_page_moves_a_weight_and_puts_it_back fail on main before this change and after it, untouched here.

139. The score counts building and the base: a thing built half a kill, the guns' life and kills, repairs, wood by the base, and guns ready; the score versioned, 4 November

Every candidate the search had made by the morning of 4 November was held by the trade guard of section 127 (orrery, trades_away): each scored more by building less, w8466 +1.27 a seat held out for 1.48 fewer things built a team, w8669 +2.42 for 3.07 fewer and less road. A thing built was worth a tenth (a kill is 1, a win 10), so the search sold building cheaply and the guard held the line still. Asked whether to promote one by hand, raise the floor, keep holding, or count building, Paul: "Count Building the score. give well defended pillboxes average time duration and longest best turrent survivial and its killrate and for bases, repair rate and usefull rate. in fact, workers mining forrests around bases should receive reward, because now base fills faster." Asked the numbers, he chose each of these.

What the score counts now (ledger.rs, ScoreWeights, all in rewards but the build, which is the score's own term):

TermWorthWhat it is
a thing built0.5 (was 0.1)half a kill
repairs0.25 a repaira pillbox or structure of the side mended by the seat's squad (PillboxRepaired, StructureRepaired)
wood near a base0.2 a loada tree the seat's squad felled with wood gained, within BASE_REACH 16 tiles of a depot of its side (Harvested)
pillbox life3 at the whole matchthe team's: the share of the match each pillbox it held stood alive in its hands, averaged over them
the longest turret2 at the whole matchthe team's: its longest-standing pillbox's share
turret kills1 a killthe team's: a hostile tank its pillbox killed (a PillboxFired on that tank in the tick it was destroyed by the pillbox's owner); the owner's seat counts the kill among its own as before

The team's terms are each of its seats', as castles are. CENSUS_REWARDS=0 is still the score of sections 8 to 83, a thing built at its tenth. The census prints a line a team beginning base of team, which orrery's readers (anchored at team and rewards of team) read past.

Paul, asked whether reads under the old score should keep counting: "Start anew". orrery's planner now takes a commit's game as both numbers, so a read of the old score is no evidence about the new, as a read of an old game is not: the waiting candidates are read again before any promotes.

The rule before the run: under the new score, w8466 against the defaults on the same 48 seeds reads under its read of the old score by more than two errors of the paired difference, if the new terms see what it gives away.

Building carries the most points; turret kills are rare enough that their rate weighs little yet.

Not done. A scenario's reward terms do not name the new counts; the trade guard's floors are unchanged (1 built, 0.5 road a team, 0.1 kills less deaths a seat); the weights are Paul's, not the search's, as the rewards of section 84 are.

141. The boundary as a test: five gauntlets of which pillbox to shoot, which tank to answer, and when to kite or stand, 4 November

Paul, of the montage's first film, where the champions fought only one pillbox in twelve chapters: "we need to setup a goal where it would be smart to attack some pillboxes while avoiding others. like enemy preference, last attacked enemy we would kite and dance, unless we have backup with fleet allies nearby. we should solve it by creating a coded set of tests that should result into that being the boudray of your question." The gauntlet (section 102) is that form already: one decision a file, little else on the map, read for the scripted brain and every net on the same seeds, a floor a file.

The game's own rules draw the line. A tank's gun reaches ten tiles and a pillbox's ring eight, so a pillbox can always be shot from outside its ring. A live hostile pillbox must be shot dead before a squad can take it ("pillbox is live and theirs: shoot it dead first"), but a live neutral one is taken whole, and fires on whoever enters its ring. So the smart thing with a hostile pillbox is to shoot it from the edge of the gun's reach, and with a neutral one to keep out of its ring and leave it to the squad.

FileThe decisionPassedFloor
G18 shoot the hostile pillbox, spare the neutral onea hostile and a neutral pillbox 9 tiles off either waythe hostile one dead in 12 s, the neutral one untouched, the armour whole0.8
G19 two pillboxes, one at a timetwo hostile pillboxes in a line at 9 and 14both dead in 30 s, the armour whole0.7
G20 the one that shot youa passive enemy at 7, one at 9 that opens fire while the tank's gun reloadsits first hit is on the attacker0.7
G21 alone: kite the one that shot youa Raider of the same armour closing from 9more hits given than taken in 40 s, and alive0.6
G22 with friends near: stand and fightas G21, two friends beside it holding their groundthe attacker dead in 30 s, more hits given than taken, within 10 tiles of a friend for seven tenths0.7

G21 and G22 are a pair: alone the smart tank kites, with backup it stands; a brain that does one in both places fails one of them. The scenario form gained three expectations: pillbox_struck (the damage a placed pillbox took), first_hit (of the tanks a seat hit, the first) and pillboxes_standing (how many placed pillboxes stand at the end). Two were found weak and changed before this record: G22's friends first escorted the tank, so staying with them was theirs and not its choice (an untrained net passed 44%), and they now hold their ground; and two hits asked of the tank were most of a kill that friends share (the rules 14%), so it now asks the tank to outscore the attacker.
