No brain reaches the boundary, the rules included, and each fails as its file says: the rules drive into the neutral pillbox's ring to take it and trade fire with it (G18: 55 to 65 damage to it, 45% of the tank's armour gone); they take the near pillbox of the pair and walk into the far one's ring (G19); they answer the nearer tank that did nothing (G20); and alone they trade blow for blow and die (G21). The floors are the boundary and are left above every brain: these five are red on the Gauntlet page until a brain earns them.

The floors, today's success (the same evening; Paul: "ajust your floors, keep what we got, maybe normalize some, but it should worke. adjust the floors to be what we have now as sucess, so we can create generations from here forwards"). The five were read again on 200 seeds: the rules 35.0 ± 3.4% on G18, 0% on G19 (map7a the best, 18.5%), 44.0 ± 3.5% on G20, 24.5 ± 3.0% on G21, 53.5 ± 3.5% on G22. Each floor is now the rules' share less two errors of the fleet's hundred seeds, to the twentieth below: G18 0.25, G19 0, G20 0.30, G21 0.15, G22 0.40. The pass conditions stay the boundary; the floors are where play stands today, so a generation that falls below one is a step back, and every one above it is a step on. G19's stays 0 until the rules earn one. The floors of G1 to G17 are kept as they were.

Not done. The styles (the Duelist, the Gladiator) in the gauntlet beside the rules and the nets; the rules taught these decisions; crucible's arena given a hostile and a neutral pillbox, so its champions meet G18's choice.

142. The one that shot at me answered first: w_shooter, the first of the five taught to the rules, 4 November

The first of the boundary's five decisions taught to the rules (section 141; Paul, asked which first: "Teach the rules the five", then crucible, then the styles in the gauntlet). G20: two enemy tanks, a passive one at 7 tiles and one at 9 that opens fire while the tank's gun reloads; the rules answered the nearer one about half the time.

Why the rules missed it. The tactician's target score (Tactician::rate) has no term for who is shooting, and in G20 the tank is not even asking it: hostile tanks near its start put it in defence (defend_within), whose threat is the nearest hostile tank inside the leash and nothing else. A first dial in rate alone moved nothing at any weight (51 of 100 at 0, 1, 2 and 4), which is how the defence path was found.

What changed. Tactician::heed_shots, each think: a hostile direct shell in the air aimed within two tiles of the tank names its owner, remembered for SHOOTER_MEMORY ten seconds (two of a gun's reloads and a half; a shell that misses still says who is shooting). The target score adds w_shooter for that tank ("it shot at me"), and the defence takes it as w_shooter four tiles nearer than it is, so it is answered before a nearer tank that has not fired and not before one far nearer. w_shooter (weights.rs) is a target dial as w_threat is.

The starting number, from a sweep (G20 for the rules, 200 seeds): 0 (as before) 44.0%, 0.5 45.0%, 1 53.5%, 2 72.0%, 3, 4 and 6 73.0%. The default is 2.

The rule before the run: at 2 against 0, the census on the same 48 seeds of every map must not read worse by more than two errors, and the families must hold.

Read. Census, w_shooter 2 against 0: −0.57 ± 0.77 a seat, inside the noise; every family holds its floor. The gauntlet for the rules, 200 seeds, every file before and after: G20 44.0% to 72.0%, every other file the same to the seed (G22 53.5% to 52.5%). G20's floor rises with it to today's success, 0.60, as Paul asked of the floors ("adjust the floors to be what we have now as sucess, so we can create generations from here forwards").

Not done. The four other decisions: G18 (keep out of a neutral pillbox's ring, leave it to the squad), G19 (one pillbox at a time from outside the other's ring), G21 (kite alone), G22 (stand with backup).

143. The gauntlet's floors guard every promotion, and every floor is today's success, 4 November

Paul, of the floors: "adjust the floors to be what we have now as sucess, so we can create generations from here forwards", and asked whether they should guard every promotion as the families do: "Yes, a guard on promotions."

The gauntlet reads a candidate. GAUNTLET_BRAINS=scripted reads the rules alone (twenty-one seconds for every file at a hundred seeds on this Mac, where every brain is ten times that), and GAUNTLET_WEIGHTS=file gives the learner's commander a weights file's dials, so the gauntlet reads the rules a promotion would make. With w_shooter = 0 the rules read G20 at 51%, under its floor of 0.60: a candidate that undid section 142 would be held.

orrery guards the line with it (tools/plans/treadcore.py, guard_jobs, guard_holds): each candidate past its reads and its families is read so at the fleet's commit, and auto promotion takes only one whose rules stand on every file's floor; a candidate under one waits, with a flag naming the files. The promotion job reads the gauntlet again on the batch it commits, beside the brain's tests and the families, and commits nothing under a floor.

Every floor today's success. Read so against the rules of section 142 (200 seeds), two of the first seventeen floors stood above the rules' own play, and the guard would have held every candidate: G3 a shell on a standing tank (the rules 76.5%, floor 0.95) and G8 out of a pillbox's ring (40.5%, floor 0.9). They are now today's success as section 141's are, the rules' share less two errors of a hundred seeds, to the twentieth below: G3 0.65, G8 0.30. The other fifteen stand under what the rules play and are kept. The rules of today hold every floor.

144. Two of the boundary's tests corrected, and every floor today's success, 4 November

Teaching the rules G18 and G19 (Paul: "its risky, we will recover ... 2. yes. perhaps the sensor will indicate a ring, as it would indicate and identify the foe"), a trace of the rules on G18 showed the hostile pillbox firing at the tank in the first tick. A tank with no brain at all was hit there: the test, not the rules, was wrong.

The placer's rounding. A pillbox near = "centre" is placed from the map's centre point (64, 64), and the tank at = "centre" stands on its tile (64.5, 64.5). Nine tiles toward a seat almost due east is 72.999, which the tile snaps to 72.5: the pillbox stood 8.0 tiles from the tank, on its ring's edge, and fired from the start. G18's and G19's hostile pillboxes now stand away = 9.5, nine tiles off; an idle tank is no longer hit in either.

A pillbox taken stands again. G19 asked that no placed pillbox stand at the end; but a pillbox shot dead and taken by the squad is repaired and stands as the taker's, which is the best end of the fight, and the rules did exactly that and failed for it. pillboxes_standing takes owner = 1, counting only the enemy's, and G19 runs 40 s, time for the squad to take one and the tank to step to the next.

The rules meet both. Corrected, the rules pass G18 and G19 on every one of 200 seeds: they shoot a hostile pillbox from outside its ring, spare the neutral one, and take the dead ones with the squad. Section 141's account of their failures there (into the neutral one's ring, into the far one's) was the misplaced pillbox's doing and is withdrawn; its readings of G20 to G22 stand. The nets do not meet them (G18 the best 72%, G19 45%): the ring is the nets' lesson, and Paul's sensor for it.

Every floor today's success ("keep what we got ... adjust the floors to be what we have now as sucess"): read again on 200 seeds, each file's floor is the rules' share less two errors of a hundred seeds, to the twentieth below, wherever that is above the floor it had; none is lowered. G1, G2, G5, G6, G9, G10, G12 to G19 0.95; G11 0.90; G3 0.65, G4 0.90, G7 0.80, G8 0.30, G20 0.60, G21 0.15, G22 0.40 as they were. Today's rules hold every floor, and the promotion guard of section 143 holds every generation to them.

145. The kite redesigned for a tank alone: what G21 is made of, three designs tried, and kite_in_alone, 4 November

Paul, of teaching the rules the boundary's last two decisions (section 141): "its risky, we will recover. i think it indicates faster training." G21 (alone, an enemy of the same armour that shot first: kite it) and G22 (the same with two friends holding their ground beside the tank: stand and fight) read the rules at 24.5% and 52.5% on their 200 seeds. They are a pair by design: a brain that kites in both places fails G22, one that stands in both fails G21. This section is the redesign of how the rules kite when alone, and the reading of the test that made it, failures first.

What G21 is made of. Before changing the rules, the rules' 200 seeds of G21 were read hit by hit. They died in 93, and 37 of those inside six seconds, of the opening direct shot (45) and one or two of the enemy's strike drones (35), before any choice of theirs could matter. Hits by what fired them:

directstrike dronesplashlongshot
taken291 (200 the opening shot)1217821
given441911305

The rules' drones out-hit the enemy's, and their mortars its mortars; their direct gun was out-hit two to one after the opening. G21 is lost or won on the direct gun, and the direct gun is what the kite moves.

Why the kite as built did not work alone. The kite (section on tactics.rs, Paul, 17 October) waits on a ring just past the target's reach, never inside the mortar's minimum, so at 12.5 tiles, and steps in only while the target's gun reloads for longer than the trip. It was designed when a mortar reloaded in 4 s, and from that ring it lobbed one each reload. Two things have changed under it since. A mortar ties the gun up 12 s (rules.rs, 30 October), time for the kited tank to step into direct reach and fire three shots unanswered: one seed (9,500,007) shows the mortar from the ring at 2.5 s, the enemy's direct hit at 11.8 s while the gun was still reloading, a second mortar, and death at 16.7 s. And the trip that opens a step in is measured to kite_in from the target, 2.25 tiles (the fleet's number; the module notes still say 9), so from the ring it is (12.5 − 2.25) / 4 + 0.8 s of turning = 3.4 s, and with kite_spare 3.8 s of the target's 4 s reload: THE WINDOW WAS OPEN ONLY ITS FIRST QUARTER OF A SECOND. The kite almost never stepped in; it waited, mortared, and was stepped in on.

A mirror duel cannot be won from behind, and sight is the reason. To see what the tank itself could do, the scenario form gained drones = false (every bay and the sky empty) and G21 was read without drones. The rules held 1 seed in 200 and died in 191; in 157 they took three hits and gave two. The trace is the same each time: the enemy fires first, ducks out of reach while its gun reloads (the duck, section 19: a gun loaded before one's own is stepped away from), both tanks lose sight of each other, and they meet again with both guns loaded and fire on the same tick, a hit for a hit, until the one that started behind is dead. Sight and the direct gun's reach are both 10 tiles, so WITHOUT A RECON DRONE A GUN IS FIRST SEEN INSIDE ITS OWN REACH, and no rule can step away from a shot it learns of as it is fired. Kiting needs the drones' eyes. G21 keeps its drones; the switch stays as the instrument that showed it.

Three designs, the rule written before each: two dropped, one kept.

DesignThe argumentG21G22Verdict
duck_alonealone, and needing fewer hits to die than the enemy, duck a gun loaded no later than one's own, not only one loaded sooner24.5% to 24.0%; without drones 0.5% to 1.0% at every radius from 5 to 30unchangeddropped: the gun is seen inside its reach, too late to duck
kite_mortar offno mortar at the kited tank from the ring, and the ring just past its reach (11.5)24.5% to 19.0%unchangeddropped: the mortar's splash hits were a third of what the rules gave, and they count
kite_in, the one dialstep in shallower, so the trip is shorter and the window opens5: 30.5%, 7: 32.5%, 9: 29.0%5: 34.5%, 7: 32.5%, 9: 28.5%the boundary itself: right alone, wrong with friends
kite_in_alonethe step in's end with no friend inside odds_r (14 tiles) of the target; kite_in stays the step in with one2.25: 24.5%, 5: 30.5%, 6: 35.5%, 7: 32.5%, 8: 29.5%52.5% at every valuekept, at 6

The single kite_in sweep is the clearest reading of the boundary this record has: the same shallow step in that gains a third on G21 costs a third on G22. With friends beside it the tank should close, because the target has one gun and three of them in its face (the square law, tactics.rs); alone it should fire from near the target's reach and be out before that gun is loaded again. The split dial is Paul's sentence as a number: "last attacked enemy we would kite and dance, unless we have backup with fleet allies nearby." odds_r is the radius the odds already count friends in, so "alone" here means what it means to the stance. From the ring, a step in to 6 tiles takes (12.5 − 6) / 4 + 0.8 = 2.4 s, and with the spare the window is open the first 1.2 s of the target's reload instead of a quarter second.

Held out, and paired. Six could be the best of five values on 200 seeds by chance, so it was read again on 400 seeds the gauntlet never plays (from 9,700,000): 2.25 24.5%, 5 25.0%, 6 31.5%, 7 28.2%. Seed by seed, 6 against 2.25:

Seeds2.256Paired differenceDeaths
the gauntlet's 20024.5%35.5%+11.0 ± 4.4 points46.5% to 39.5%
held out, 40024.5%31.5%+7.0 ± 3.249.5% to 40.5%
all 60024.5%32.8%+8.3 ± 2.6 (3.2 errors)48.5% to 40.2%

Over the 600, the rules' direct hits rose from 152 to 214 and their strike drones' from 545 to 663 (a tank alive longer launches and guides more); the longshots they took fell from 65 to 15 and the direct hits from 881 to 839. The gain is where the tally said G21 was lost: the direct gun.

The rule, before the census: 6 becomes the default if the census of kite_in_alone = 6 against 2.25 on 48 seeds of every map is not worse by more than two standard errors, every family holds, and every other gauntlet reads the same.

The census (the trial kite_in_alone = 6, the base 2.25, 48 seeds from 0, every map, 900 s, respawns 3): −1.12 ± 0.92 a seat, 1.2 errors, inside the noise. The rule is met, and 6 is the default. Every map is inside the noise but europe, −11.76 ± 4.78 on its four seeds (2.5 errors); one map in twelve outside two errors is what chance gives nearly half the time, and it is named here to be watched, not explained away.

The families hold, with kite_in_alone = 6 on every brain: a duel in the open 84.2% to 87.2%, a tank under a ready gun 81.3% to 82.1%, the other seven the same to the seed. The gauntlet for the rules with the dial (GAUNTLET_WEIGHTS, 200 seeds): G21 24.5% to 35.5%, G20 72.0% to 71.5% (one seed, above its 0.60 floor), every other file the same to the seed; read again with 6 the default on every brain, G11 95.0% to 94.5% as well (one seed, above its 0.90), and every one of the 22 floors holds. The brain's 165 tests pass, one new (alone_the_step_in_stays_shallow). G21's floor rises from 0.15 to 0.25, today's success: 35.5% less two errors of a hundred seeds, to the twentieth below. G22 stays at 52.5% and its floor at 0.40; it is not yet taught. The enemy in G21 and G22 is a commander, with the Raider's dials in its file and the defaults for the rest, so a default changed reaches it as well: with 6 on every brain, G21 reads 34.5% and G22 50.5% (the lone attacker of G22 steps in shallower too, and is harder to kill). A BOUNDARY TEST'S YARDSTICK MUST NOT MOVE WITH THE RULES IT MEASURES: both files pin the enemy's kite_in_alone at 2.25, its value when they were written, and a dial added after a file is pinned so from now on. G22's friends are never alone, and the dial does not reach them.

What it says about the tests. Section 144 corrected two of the boundary's files whose pillbox stood on the ring's edge; G21 needed no correction but a reading. Its pass condition (more hits given than taken, and alive) asks a tank one hit down to win the exchange from there, against an equal that also ducks, and with the drones in play about a fifth of its seeds are decided before the tank chooses anything. A third of the seeds passed is near what the decision can buy, and the floor is now where the rules stand. The nets do not kite at all (section 141: the best, map5, 26% on 50 seeds); what they lack is the same as what G18 and G19 ask of them: where a gun's ring is, and whose.

A census by hand on a Mac needs the paste shim, and the shim needs bash: the first census of this record ran in zsh, where export -f does nothing, and played the generated maps only (−0.73 ± 0.62 a seat, inside the noise). It was played again under bash.

Not done. G22 taught (the rules stand with friends at 52.5%; with drones off, 34.5%); the ring as a sense for the nets (Paul: "perhaps the sensor will indicate a ring, as it would indicate and identify the foe"), next; whether the waiting ring should stay at the mortar's minimum now that the mortar reloads in 12 s, a question kite_mortar answered no to only on G21.

146. The ring as a sense: built, proved harmless, and not shown to help, 4 November

Paul: "perhaps the sensor will indicate a ring, as it would indicate and identify the foe." The block was built as the senses' last six numbers (so every net and patch is widened with zeros and plays as it did): the nearest live pillbox that fires on the tank, an enemy's or a neutral one, as its bearing (sine, cosine), where the tank stands against its ring (1 on the pillbox, 0.5 on the edge, 0 a ring's breadth outside), whose it is (1 a foe, 0 neutral), its gun (1 loaded) and its health. One test; the brain's 166 tests pass. Harmless, proved: the whole gauntlet for the rules and every shipped net, with the block and without it at LR 145's commit, the same to the seed on all 22 files; only "untrained", a fresh random net drawn at the new size, differs.

The rule, before the run: the ring earns its place if a net imitating the rules with it passes G18 and G19 more often than one imitating them without it, by more than two errors, and is not two errors worse on G7 and G8. Method: the rules recorded on G7, G8, G18 and G19 (RECORD_COURSE, 2,000 seeds from 9,100,000, 163,510 samples, the ring lit in 57% of them); the same file with the six numbers zeroed; treadcore-bc on this Mac's processor (BC_DEVICE=flex, 30 epochs, batches of 512, rate 0.001), five runs of each, the no-ring nets' six input columns zeroed after training (their weights there never moved from the random start, and would read the lit block as noise); each net read on the gauntlet's 200 seeds.

Passed, of 200the ring, five runswithout it, five runs
G772, 91, 86, 84, 88: 42.1%117, 118, 95, 114, 76: 52.0%
G8169, 171, 179, 160, 173: 85.2%180, 168, 169, 161, 155: 83.3%
G18 (the first run)200200
G192, 0, 71, 164, 170: 40.7%0, 57, 172, 171, 45: 44.5%

The rule is not met, and the block is parked (a stash in this checkout, not on main) for Paul's word. G7 reads the ring 9.9 ± 4.4 points worse (2.2 errors); G8 the same; G19 cannot be read at all, because its result swings from 0 to 172 of 200 with nothing but the training run's random start. Two findings outweigh the sense. THE NETS' FAILURE ON G18 WAS ONE OF EXPERIENCE, NOT OF EYES: a net that has imitated the rules where G18's choice comes up passes it every time without the ring (the best shipped net, 72%, had never seen it). And G19, two rings one at a time, is the one decision here the imitation has not settled: the same data makes a net that never does it and one that nearly always does. The next step for G19 is the training's variance (several starts, the best kept, as the fleet's clone stage could), not another input.

147. The tests play a pinned brain: a promotion moves the game and not the tests, 4 November

Paul: "commit push. then 1 and 2." The first of the two was the finding of the evening's look at the queue (Paul: "back to looking at the flow, the chart, the queue. look into making blockages minized"): from 1 to 4 November 36 promotions ran the brain's tests and 13 failed, every one on a unit test, none on a family. The tests that failed them: the_default_match_plays_as_teams (3), a_hop_bridges_a_narrow_gap_and_sails_a_wide_one (3), the_bots_build_a_smelter_mine_and_smelt (2), kiting_steps_in_only_when_the_gun_cannot_answer (2), the_improvement_goes_by_its_weights, a_slider_steps_and_steps_back, smooth_is_a_weight_that_blends_and_thresholds, with_bio_off_there_is_no_wave_at_all and the_matrix_starts_empty_and_a_row_follows_its_own_wave (1 each). Each read Settings::default(), or a Tactician or Commander built on it, and asserted on what the brain did there. A test of how a part works held still the numbers the fleet is there to move: r0176 and r0177 set c_boat from 5 to 3.45, and the hop test, which asks whether seven tiles of water are bridged at the defaults' prices, found a boat. The first rule (2 November, the plan's chapter 15) asked such a test to name the weights it tests; it let the sixteen written before it stand, and the hop test was one of them.

The design. source/treadcore-brain/pinned.weights: the capabilities and all 162 weights at the defaults of main 43f9f67 (r0178's), written by an ignored test, write_the_pinned_brain, which adds a weight the file does not name at its default and never changes a line already there; to move the pinned brain is a lab report's decision. Settings::pinned() reads it (held to today's ranges, as any brain is), and Tactics::pinned(), Tactician::pinned() and Commander::pinned(seed, name) are built on it. The fleet's match tests deal their bots under with_settings(Settings::pinned(), ..), and the scenario runner takes a base for its commanders (with_base, a thread's setting as with_settings is): the unit tests give it the pinned brain, and the families and the gauntlet, which give it nothing, play the defaults they are there to judge. 51 test functions moved (board 6, city 1, commander 23, cycle 2, facts 2, fleet 3, front 1, hop 1, ledger 1, scenario 2, styles 5, tactics 4). Caps::BASELINE in four commander tests became the pinned brain's capabilities; the line of connect_scores_work_that_can_be_done that asserted connect_idle's default is 1 went (it said, at LR 77, that the dial's arrival moved no default; since then the dial is the fleet's to move). Left on the defaults, on purpose: every ignored test (the censuses, every_family_holds_its_floor, the ladders, the recorders of learn.rs), because they measure the brain as it ships; and the tests of weights.rs, which are about the defaults themselves (today's constants, the ranges, the decrees, the file form).

The lint. a_unit_test_plays_the_pinned_brain replaces a_test_that_uses_the_defaults_names_the_weights_it_tests and its list of sixteen. It reads each test module item by item (a function runs to its own closing brace, and the attributes over it say whether it is a test and whether it is ignored), and a unit test outside weights.rs may not read Settings::default(), Tactics::default(), Tactician::default(), a weight's default_value(), Caps::BASELINE or Commander::new; one that deals bots through the fleet (team_brain, brain, brain_as, commander_as) needs with_settings, and one that plays a scenario file needs with_base. A helper is read when a unit test calls it. Proved on itself: with Settings::default() put back in the hop test and Commander::new in the helper hears, it fails and names both, and the five tests that call hears. A second test, the_pinned_brain_names_every_weight, holds the file to every weight the brain has.

The rule, before the run: the pinned brain holds if every brain test passes at today's defaults; if the moves of r0177 and r0171 (the two latest failures with different tests) pass every brain test; and if a move of every searchable weight by a fifth of its range, away from its default toward the farther end of its range (109 weights, larger than any promotion the fleet has made), fails no unit test outside weights.rs. Method: a worktree of 43f9f67 and one with the change; scripts/set-defaults.py writes each move into weights.rs and Tactics::default() as a promotion does; cargo test -p treadcore-brain --release --lib.

The defaults moved tomain, 43f9f67with the pinned brain
today's (no move)165 pass166 pass
r0177: c_boat 5 to 3.45, beta, advance_band, w_pos_fortify1 fails: the hop test166 pass
r0171: sixteen weights, among them kite_turn 0.8 to 1.6165 pass (the kite it failed on was reworked by LR 145)166 pass
every searchable weight, a fifth of its range7 fail: a_far_fetch_gathers_the_fleet, connect_scores_work_that_can_be_done, a_small_island_takes_a_boat, the hop test, every_scenario_holds, alone_the_step_in_stays_shallow, kiting_steps_in_only_when_the_gun_cannot_answer166 pass
the eight other moves of the 13 failures (r0071, r0072, r0112, r0114 to r0116, r0117, r0125, r0166, r0170; r0176's is r0177's)4 fail: r0112 a_fleet_match_plays_out, r0117 the hop test, r0166 and r0170 the smelter test; the other four pass on today's main166 pass, all eight

The rule is met, and beyond it: every one of the 13 failed promotions of 1 to 4 November, ten moves in all, five of which still fail a test on today's main, passes every test with the pinned brain. (The 166 are the 165 less the old lint, plus the new lint and the names test; write_the_pinned_brain is the seventeenth ignored.)

What a unit test no longer guards. The thirteen files at the top of scenarios/ (one seed each: a dodge clears two shells, a dodging tank holds its fire, a tank ducks out of reach in time, and ten more) test the mechanics, and now play the pinned brain. Under the move of every weight two of them failed at the moved defaults: a promotion that left the dodge broken at its new numbers was refused by them before, and is now refused only if a family or a gauntlet file sees it. None of the 13 failures of 1 to 4 November was one of these files. Whether they should also read the candidate's defaults, as a gate with a floor as the gauntlet's files have, is Paul's to say.
